      minhash: cXgibOGOicslLKjTk2AJd6vQHgXJk4cnXYCdu3W+lbYTsvWpUYpxML5boG5nMzxjZ9DTEuftzQxohmu9iFGCwK0IHr4lwACq5tmJD4XtX1+g5+/VdNeDkfRd49DpYrbUeFtJK875ZiKrOPhOda1kK1LMYMs6bUhedEmy5qbCXcasNhVZvPBDKTgGgOc095AdCg7bGeI/zmHdwiLxEugadVABcLuLBkgz9xSKBKA+FZOJLuHFQ56lfnd7YFjy/fBru+fhC5ujpUHKjb+hhgYkGgG9VK1L1iDvKdvVlFwR7numkWixfwK2V46i6GXZ3TdPVmko6TRbgDbOPpBB0FqSOg==

<!-- assu-1: body_md -->
\label{assumptions}
	The potential $V$ satisfies:
	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
		\item\label{assumption1} $V\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})$, is real-analytic in a complex neighborhood $\mathcal{U}$ of $[-1,1]$ and $\liminf\limits_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{V(x)}{\log|x|}=+\infty$ 
		\item \label{assumption2}(One-cut) The support of $\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}$ is the connected interval $[-1,1]$ and is given by:
		$$\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}(x)}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x}=\dfrac{S(x)\sqrt{1-x^2}}{\pi}$$
		where $S(x)>0$ on $[-1,1]$. Recall that $S$ can be extended analytically on $\mathcal{U}$ by
		\begin{equation}\label{eq: S}
			S(z)\coloneqq\dfrac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^1\dfrac{V'(t)-V'(z)}{t-z}\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t}{\sqrt{1-t^2}}.
		\end{equation}
		\item \label{assumption3}(Off-criticality) The function $x\mapsto V(x)-2\displaystyle\int_{-1}^1\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(y)$ achieves its minimum value on $[-1,1]$ only.
	\end{enumerate}
	Furthermore, we assume that $\alpha=N\beta\gg( \log N)^{k}$ for all $k>0$.
<!-- end assu-1 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main CLT global}Let $k\geq0$, $\beta\gg N^{-1+\kappa}$ for some $\kappa>0$. Let $f\in\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})$ for $r$ big enough (depending only on $\kappa$), then for all $q\geq1$:
	$$	\mathbf{W}_q\left(\sqrt{N\alpha}L_N^{(k)}(f),\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sigma^{2}(f) \right)\right) \leq C(f) \Big(\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}+\dfrac{\sqrt{N\alpha}}{\alpha^{k+1}}\Big)$$
	where
	\begin{equation}\label{eq: norm H 1/2 global}
		\sigma^2(f)\coloneqq\dfrac{1}{2\pi^2}\iint_{[-1,1]^2}\left(\dfrac{f(x)-f(y)}{x-y}\right)^2 \cdot \dfrac{1-xy}{\sqrt{1-x^2}\sqrt{1-y^2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:mesoc clt bulk}
	Let $N^{-1+\kappa}\ll\beta_N\ll \tfrac{1}{(\log N)^{1+\kappa}}$ for some $\kappa>0$. For some $k\geq1$, large enough, for all $E\in(-1+\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon)$ for some fixed $\varepsilon>0$ and all $q\geq1$, with $f_\eta\coloneqq f\left(\eta^{-1}(\cdot-E)\right)$ and explicit rates $(\mathfrak{R}_i)_{i\in\llbracket1,4\rrbracket}$ given in Theorem \ref{thm:meso sec5}, the following holds:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}\textit{(Random Matrix regime)}\endgroup if $\tfrac{1}{\alpha}\ll\eta\ll1$ then for all $f\in\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$:
\begin{equation*}
	\mathbf{W}_q\left (\sqrt{N\alpha}L_N^{(k)}(f_\eta), \mathcal{N}\left(0,\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^{2}\right)\right ) \lesssim \begin{cases}
		\mathfrak{R}_1 & \text{if } \eta \geq \tfrac{N^\delta}{\alpha}, \\
	\mathfrak{R}_2&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
\end{equation*}Furthermore, for all fixed $q\geq1$, $\mathfrak{R}_1$ and $\mathfrak{R}_2$ are a $o(1)$.
	\item \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}(\textit{Poisson regime})\endgroup if $\tfrac{1}{N}\ll\eta\ll\theta\ll\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$ then for all $f\in\mathcal{C}_c^{3}(\mathbb{R})$:
		$$		\mathbf{W}_q\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(f_\eta), \mathcal{N}(0, \|f\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}^2)\right) \lesssim \mathfrak{R}_3.$$
		Furthermore, for all fixed $q\geq1$, $\mathfrak{R}_3=o(1)$.
			\item \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}(\textit{Critical regime})\endgroup If $\alpha\eta\mu_\mathrm{eq}(E)\tau= 1$ for some $\tau>0$ then for all $f\in\mathcal{C}_c^{3}(\mathbb{R})$:
				$$\mathbf{W}_q\left (\sqrt{\tfrac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(f_\eta), \mathcal{N}(0, \Sigma_\tau^2(f))\right )\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_4.$$
				Furthermore, for all fixed $q\geq1$, $\mathfrak{R}_4=o(1)$.
	\end{enumerate}
	The semi-norm $\|\cdot\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}$ is given in \eqref{def: H12 norm} and
	\begin{equation*}\label{eq:Sigmac}
	\Sigma_\tau^2(1):=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{|\xi|\cdot|\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:local law}
		Let $V$  satisfying Assumptions \ref{assumptions}, $\beta_N\gg N^{-1+\kappa}$ for some $\kappa>0$, and $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\tilde{\eta},C,k>0$ (depending on $V$, $\kappa$ and $\varepsilon$) such that for any $q\geq1$, fixed $\delta>0$ and $z=x+\mathrm{i} \eta$ with $-1+\varepsilon\leq x\leq1-\varepsilon$, we have for $N$ large enough:
	 $$\|s_N(z)-m_k(z)\|_q\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{C\frac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}<\eta<\tilde{\eta}}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha\eta^{2}}}+\mathbf{1}_{\eta\leq C\frac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\eta}}+\dfrac{q}{N\eta}\right) .$$
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:free energy}
	Let $V, \beta_N$ satisfying Assumptions \ref{assumptions}, there exists real numbers $\left(F_{i,j}\right)_{i,j}$ such that for all $K,L\geq1$:
$$\dfrac{2}{N\alpha}\log\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_N[2x^{2}]}=\sum_{j=0}^{L}\sum_{i=0}^{K-j}\dfrac{F_{i,j}}{\alpha^iN^{j}}+o\left(\dfrac{1}{ \alpha^{K+1}}+\dfrac{1}{N^{L+1}}\right).$$
Furthermore, $\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{2}{N\alpha}\log\mathcal{Z}_N[V]=-\mathcal{E}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})$ and $F_{1,0}=2\mathrm{Ent}\left[\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\right]-2\log\pi+1$.
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:asympcorrelators}
		Let $n\geq1$, for all $i\geq1,j\geq0$, there exist linear maps $b_{i,j}^{(n)}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([-1,1]^{n})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ such that for any $K\geq L\geq0$, there exist $r=r(n,K)\geq1$ such that for all $\psi_n\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ for some $\kappa>0$, one has the expansion as $N\to\infty$:
		\begin{equation}\label{braexp}
			\braket{\psi_n}=\sum_{j=0}^{L}\sum_{i=0}^{K-j}\mathbf{1}_{(i,j)\neq(0,0)}\dfrac{b_{i,j}^{(n)}(\psi_n)}{\alpha^iN^{j}}+O\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K+1}}\right)+O\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,r}}{N^{L+1}}\right).
		\end{equation}
		Moreover, one has  $|b_{i,j}^{(n)}(\psi_n)| \lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\mathcal{C}^{q}([-1,1]^n)}$ for some $q=q(n,i,j) \le r$ and $b_{i,j}^{(n)} = 0$ if $i+j<n$.
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- def-1: body_md -->
\label{def:mup}
		Let $k\geq0$, we define the linear map $\mu_k$ defined on $\mathcal{C}^{r}([-1,1])$ for $r$ large enough by:
		$$\mu_{k}\coloneqq\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\dfrac{\nu_j}{\alpha^{j}},$$
		where we defined for all $i\geq1$, $\nu_i\coloneqq b_{i,0}^{(1)}$.
<!-- end def-1 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{a priori bound} Let $n\geq1$, $\kappa>0$, then for all $\psi_n$ depending on $n$ variables:
		$$\left| \Braket{\psi_n}\right|\lesssim\left(\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right) ^{\frac{n}{2}} \left \|\psi_n\right\|_{\kappa,n}.$$
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- def-2: body_md -->
\label{def:ope D}
		We define the operator $\Xi$ called the master operator which is defined for all $1\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_\mathrm{loc}(\mathbb{R})$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}$ by:
		$$\Xi[1](x)\coloneqq \dfrac{V'(x)}{2}1(x)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{1(x)-1(y)}{x-y}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \mu _{\mathrm{eq}}(y).$$
		We define the operator $\mathcal{D}$ for all $1\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_\mathrm{loc}(\mathbb{R})$ and $x,y\in\mathbb{R}$ by:
		$$\mathcal{D}[1](x,y)\coloneqq
		\begin{cases}
			\displaystyle
			\dfrac{1(x)-1(y)}{x-y},
			& \text{if } x\neq y, \medskip \\
			1'(x)
			& \text{if } x=y,
		\end{cases}$$
<!-- end def-2 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:inverse Xi}
		Let $f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})$, there exists a unique $g\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\Xi[g]=f-a_f$ with $a_f\coloneqq\int_{-1}^{1}f(t)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t}{\sqrt{1-t^{2}}}$.  Moreover, defining $\Xi^{-1}[f]\coloneqq g$, for all $j\geq0$,
		$$\|\Xi^{-1}[f]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{j}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{j+1}(\mathbb{R})}.$$
		Furthermore $\Xi^{-1}[f]_{|[-1,1]}$ is given by:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:inverse Xi formula}
			\Xi^{-1}[f]_{|[-1,1]}=\dfrac{1}{\pi S(x)}\int_{-1}^{1}\dfrac{f(x)-f(y)}{x-y}\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y}{\sqrt{1-y^2}}.
		\end{equation}
		where $S$ is given in \eqref{eq: S} and for all $j\geq1$, $\|\Xi^{-1}[f]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{j}([-1,1])}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{j-1}([-1,1])}.$ Furthermore for $|x|\geq1$:
		    
		                                 
					$$\Xi^{-1}[f](x)=
							\displaystyle 
							\frac{\displaystyle\int_{-1}^1 \frac{\Xi^{-1}[f](t)}{x-t} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(t)+f(x)-a}{ \dfrac{V'(x)}{2}-\displaystyle\int_{-1}^1 \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(t)}{x-t}} ,
							$$
							and if $f(x)=0$ for $|x|\leq M$ for $M>1$ then, $\Xi^{-1}[f](x)=0$ for all $|x|\leq M$.
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- def-3: body_md -->
\label{def:nu1 theta}Let $n\geq1$ and $\psi_n\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})$, we define:
		$$\tau[\psi_n](x_2,\dots,x_n)\coloneqq\int_{-1}^{1}\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_n](x_1,x_2,\dots,x_n)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x_1).$$
		Note that when $n=1$, $\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]=\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]'$ and $\nu_1(\psi_1)$ is just a constant.
		Furthermore if $n\geq2$, we define the operator $\Theta$ by:
		$$\Theta[\psi_n](x_1,\dots,x_{n-1})\coloneqq \sum_{k=2}^{n}\Xi^{-1}[\partial_k\psi_{n}](x_1,\dots,x_{k-1},x_1,x_k,\dots,x_{n-1}).$$
		We also define:
		$$\theta[\psi_n](x_2,\dots,x_{n-1})\coloneqq\int_{-1}^{1}\Theta[\psi_{n}](x_1,\dots,x_{n-1})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x_1).$$
<!-- end def-3 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:DSequations}Let $\kappa>0$, the level $1$ loop equation holds for all function $\psi_1\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,1}$ and takes the form:
		\begin{equation}\label{DSlvl1}
			\Braket{\psi_1}=	\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right)\tau[\psi_1]
			+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right)\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]'}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]}.
		\end{equation}
		For all $\psi_n\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,1}$, the level $n\geq2$ loop equations reads:
		                                                        
			                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           
			                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
			                  
		\begin{equation}\label{DSlvlnthm}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Braket{\psi_{n}}
				&= \left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{n}]}+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{n}]}+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{n}]}\\
				&\quad + \dfrac{1}{N\alpha}	\Braket{\theta[\psi_n]}+\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}
				\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{n}]}.
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- rem-1: body_md -->
\label{rem:continuity}	Every operator involved in the above loop equations are continuous. Indeed the following bounds hold for all $\kappa>0$, $k\geq0$:
		$$\|\tau[\psi_n]\|_{\kappa,k}\lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,k+2},\quad\quad\|\Xi^{-1}[\psi_n]\|_{\kappa,k}\lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,k+1},\quad\quad\quad\|\mathcal{D}[\psi_n]\|_{\kappa,k}\lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,k+1}$$
		and
		$$\|\Theta[\psi_n]\|_{\kappa,k}\lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,k+2},\quad\quad\|\theta[\psi_n]\|_{\kappa,k}\lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,k+2}.$$
		These bounds are crucial to apply Proposition \ref{a priori bound} for brakets of the form $\Braket{\mathcal{W}[\psi_n]}$ where $\mathcal{W}$ is any finite word in $\tau$, $\partial_1$, $\Xi^{-1}$, $\mathcal{D}$, $\Theta$ and $\theta$. Because of these bounds and Proposition \ref{a priori bound}, we have for any $n\geq1$,
		$$\braket{\mathcal{W}[\psi_n]}\lesssim\dfrac{\log^{k/2} N}{\alpha^{k/2}}\|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,r}$$
		for some $r\geq0$ where $k$ is the number of variables of the function $\mathcal{B}[\psi_n]$.
<!-- end rem-1 -->
<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:L=0}
			Let $n\geq1$, for all $i\geq1$ there exists $b_{i,0}^{(n)}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([-1,1]^{n})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ such that for all $K\geq0$, there exists $r=r(n,K)\geq1$ such that for all $\psi_n\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ for some $\kappa>0$, one has the expansion as $N\to\infty$:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq: proof prop21 eq intermed}
			\braket{\psi_n}=\sum_{i=n}^{K}\dfrac{b_{i,0}^{(n)}(\psi_n)}{\alpha^{i}}+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K}}\right)+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,K}^{(n)}(\psi_n),
		\end{equation}
		where $\mathsf{B}_{0,K}^{(n)}(\psi_n)\in\mathcal{B}(\psi_n)$. Moreover, one has $|b_{i,0}^{(n)}(\psi_n)| \lesssim \|\psi_n\|_{\mathcal{C}^{q}([-1,1]^n)}$ for some $q=q(n,i,j) \le r$.
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- pro-1: body_md -->
We prove this statement by induction on $K\geq1$.
		
		By Proposition \ref{prop:DSequations}, the second loop equations gives:
		\begin{align}\label{eq:2nd loop eq}
			\Braket{\psi_{2}}
			&= \left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
			\Braket{\tau[\psi_{2}]} +\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
			\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]}+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]}+\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}\nonumber
			\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{2}]}\\
			&\quad  + \dfrac{1}{N\alpha}\theta[\psi_2]\nonumber
			\\&=\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\left(\Braket{\tau[\psi_{2}]}+\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]}\right)+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]}+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(2)}(\psi_2),
		\end{align}
		where $\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(2)}(\psi_2)\coloneqq \tfrac{1}{\alpha}(\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{2}]}+\theta[\psi_2])-\tfrac{1}{2}(\Braket{\tau[\psi_{2}]}+\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]})\in\mathcal{B}(\psi_2)$. Furthermore, because of Proposition \ref{a priori bound}, of Remark \ref{rem:continuity} and since $\alpha\gg\log ^{k} N$ for all $k\geq1$, we have the following bounds:
		$$\dfrac{1}{\alpha }|\Braket{\tau[\psi_{2}]}|\lesssim\dfrac{\log^{1/2} N}{\alpha^{3/2}}\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}=o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right),$$
		$$\dfrac{1}{\alpha }|\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]}|\lesssim\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha^{3/2}}\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}=o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right),$$
		and
		$$ |\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2}]}|\lesssim\dfrac{\log^{3/2} N}{\alpha^{3/2}}\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}=o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right).$$
		Using \eqref{eq:2nd loop eq} and collecting all the previous bounds, we deduce that for general $\psi_2\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ for some $r$ large enough:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:improved bound psi2}
			|\braket{\psi_2}-\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(2)}(\psi_2)|= o\left (\dfrac{\|\psi_2\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right )
		\end{equation} for some $r\geq0$. Now the first loop equation, by Proposition \ref{prop:DSequations} gives for $\psi_1\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$:
		\begin{align*}
			\Braket{\psi_1}&=\dfrac{\tau[\psi_1]}{\alpha }-\dfrac{\tau[\psi_1]}{2N}
			+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right)\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]'}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]}
			\\&=\dfrac{\tau[\psi_1]}{\alpha }+\dfrac{1}{\alpha }\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]'}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]}+\dfrac{1}{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,1}^{(1)}(\psi_1),
		\end{align*}
		where $\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,1}^{(1)}(\psi_1)=\tfrac{-1}{2}\tau[\psi_1]-\tfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]'}$.
		Using Proposition \ref{a priori bound} as before, we have:
		$$\dfrac{1}{\alpha }|\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]'}|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\dfrac{\log ^{1/2} N}{\alpha^{1/2}}\|\psi_1\|_{\kappa,r},$$
		and by \eqref{eq:improved bound psi2} since $\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ is a function of 2 variables, we have:
		$$\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]}= o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_1\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right)+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(2)}(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]).$$
		Finally, collecting the previous bounds and noticing that $\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,1}^{(1)}(\psi_1)+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(2)}(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1])\in\mathcal{B}(\psi_1)$, we obtain:
		$$\Braket{\psi_1}=\dfrac{\tau[\psi_1]}{\alpha }+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_1\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right)+\dfrac{1}{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,1}^{(1)}(\psi_1)+\dfrac{1}{2N}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(2)}(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_1]).$$
		Finally, for $n\geq3$:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq: n th loop eq}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Braket{\psi_{n}}
				&= \dfrac{1}{N\alpha}	\Braket{\theta[\psi_n]}+\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}
				\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{n}]}+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{n}]} +\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{n}]}
				\\	\quad&\quad+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{n}]}
				,
				\\&=\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{n}]}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{n}]}+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{n}]}+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(n)}(\psi_n)
				\\&=o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_1\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha}\right)+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,1}^{(n)}(\psi_n),
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		where the last lign comes from Proposition \ref{a priori bound}. Noticing that $|\tau[\psi_1]|\lesssim \|\psi_1\|_{\mathcal{C}^{q}([-1,1])}$ for some $q\geq1$ because of Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi} concludes the case $K=1$.
		
		We now assume that for all $n\geq1$ and $\psi_n\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ for $r$ large enough, \eqref{eq: proof prop21 eq intermed} holds for $K$, we show that the property holds for $K+1$.
		
		Let $n\geq1$ such that $\left(\tfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{n/2}\ll \tfrac{1}{\alpha^{K+1}}$, thus for all $m\geq n-1$:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq: n th loop eq'}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Braket{\psi_{m}}
				&= \dfrac{1}{N\alpha}	\Braket{\theta[\psi_m]}+\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}
				\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{m}]}+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{m}]} +\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}
				\\	\quad&\quad+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}
				,
				\\&=\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{m}]}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}+\dfrac{1}{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\psi_m)
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		The third term is a $m+1$ braket so by Proposition \ref{a priori bound}, we have:
		$$|\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_m]}|\lesssim\left(\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{n/2}\|\psi_m\|_{\kappa,r}=o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_m\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K+1}}\right).$$
		By induction hypothesis and since they both exhibit a prefactor $\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$, we have:
		\begin{align*}
			\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
			\left(	\Braket{\tau[\psi_{m}]}+
			\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}\right) &=\sum_{i=m-1}^{K}\dfrac{b_{i,0}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+b_{i,0}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])}{\alpha^{i+1}}+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_m\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K+1}}\right)
			\\&\quad+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+\dfrac{1}{N}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])
		\end{align*}
		where $\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])\in\mathcal{B}(\tau[\psi_{m}])$ and $\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m-1)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])\in\mathcal{B}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])$. Thus by \eqref{eq: n th loop eq'}, we obtain that for all $m\geq n-1$, 
		$$\begin{aligned}
			\Braket{\psi_{m}}&=\sum_{i=m}^{K+1}\dfrac{b_{i-1,0}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+b_{i-1,0}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])}{\alpha^{i}}+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_m\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K+1}}\right)
			\\&\quad+\dfrac{1}{N}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\psi_m)+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])\right) 
		\end{aligned}$$
		and since $\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\psi_m)+\tfrac{1}{\alpha}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+\tfrac{1}{\alpha}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])\in\mathcal{B}(\psi_m)$, it establishes the induction for $m\geq n-1$ . By the induction hypothesis, we know that both $b_{i-1,0}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\cdot])]$ and $b_{i-1,0}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\cdot])$ are continuous for $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^{q}([-1,1]^{m})}$ and linear for $i\in\llbracket m,K+1\rrbracket$. This implies that $b_{i,0}^{(m)}(\cdot)$ is continuous for the same norm. For $m=n-2$, taking the loop equation at level $m$, we have similarly as \eqref{eq: n th loop eq'}:
		\begin{equation*}
				\Braket{\psi_{m}}=\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{m}]}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha }
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}]}+\dfrac{1}{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\psi_m).
		\end{equation*}
		Since we have now proved the statement for the $m+1$ brakets, and by induction hypothesis using the prefactors $\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$ of the first two terms in the RHS above, we obtain:
			$$\begin{aligned}
				\Braket{\psi_{m}}&=\sum_{i=m}^{K+1}\dfrac{b_{i-1,0}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+b_{i-1,0}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])}{\alpha^{i}}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{K+1}\dfrac{b_{i,0}^{(m)}(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])}{\alpha^{i}}+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_m\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K+1}}\right)
				\\&\quad+\dfrac{1}{N}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\psi_m)+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m-1)}(\tau[\psi_{m}])+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])+\dfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{B}_{0,K+1}^{(m)}(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{m}])\right).
			\end{aligned}$$
		Repeating the exact same argument succesively for $m=n-3$, and then $n-4$ etc... proves the statement for all $m\geq1$. This concludes the induction.
<!-- end pro-1 -->
<!-- pro-2: body_md -->
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Asymptotic expansion:\endgroup To show the statement, we first prove by induction on $L$ that for all $n\geq1$ and $\psi_n\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ for $r$ large enough, $K\geq1$:
		$$\braket{\psi_n}=\sum_{j=0}^{L}\sum_{i=0}^{K-j}\mathbf{1}_{(i,j)\neq(0,0)}\dfrac{b_{i,j}^{(n)}(\psi_n)}{\alpha^{i}N^{j}}+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K}}\right)+\dfrac{1}{N^{L+1}}\mathsf{B}_{L,K}^{(n)}(\psi_n)$$
		where $\mathsf{B}_{L,K}^{(n)}(\psi_n)\in\mathcal{B}(\psi_n)$ and $b_{i,j}^{(n)}$ linear and continuous for $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^{q}([-1,1]^{n})}$. The case $L=0$ is proved in Lemma \ref{lem:L=0}. We now assume that the above equation is true for $L\geq0$ and we show it for $L+1$. 
		
		Since $\mathsf{B}_{L,K}^{(n)}(\psi_n)\in\mathcal{B}(\psi_n)$, there exists $p\geq0$, $(c_\ell)_{\ell=1}^{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}$ and $(A_\ell)_{\ell=1}^{p}$ finite words in $\tau,\partial_1,\Xi^{-1}, \mathcal{D}$, $\Theta$ and $\theta$ with $A_\ell[\psi_n]$ possibly depending on 0 variables and $c_\ell=O(1)$, such that:
		\begin{align*}
			\mathsf{B}_{L,K}^{(n)}(\psi_n)&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{p}c_\ell\braket{A_\ell[\psi_n]}
			\\&=\sum_{i=0}^{K-L-1}\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{i}}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{p}c_\ell b_{i,0}(A_\ell[\psi_n])\right) +\dfrac{1}{N}\left( \sum_{\ell=1}^{p}c_\ell \mathsf{B}_{0,K-L-1}^{(n)}(A_\ell[\psi_n])\right)+o\left(\dfrac{\|\psi_n\|_{\kappa,r}}{\alpha^{K-L-1}}\right).
		\end{align*}
		Note that the second equality holds because of the induction hypothesis and that the sum starts at $i=0$, because the sum might contain 0-brakets. The linearity and continuity of $b_{i,j}^{(n)}(\psi_n)$ follows from the one of $b_{i,0}^{(k_\ell)}(A_\ell[\cdot])$ for $\ell\in\llbracket1,p\rrbracket$. It is straightforward to verify that the second term belongs to $\mathcal{B}(\psi_n)$, this concludes the induction. Finally, by construction, any element of $\mathcal{B}(\psi_n)$ is a $O(1)$ which proves that the expansion of Proposition \ref{prop:asympcorrelators} holds.

               
		
		\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. $b_{i,j}^{(n)}=0$ for $i+j<n$.\endgroup In this proof, we only work with smooth functions with apropriate growth at infinity such that the expansion  proved above holds at all order and don't specify the dependance in $\psi_n$ in our estimates. We show by induction that for $\alpha=\beta N$ with fixed $\beta>0$ and all $n\geq1$, $\braket{\psi_n}=O\left(N^{-n}\right)$. This is enough to show that $b_{i,j}^{(n)}(\psi_n)=0$ for $i+j<n$ because the obtained expansion gives: 
		$$\braket{\psi_{n}}=\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\dfrac{1}{N^{k}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\beta^{-i}b_{i,k-i}^{(n)}(\psi_n)+O\left(N^{-n}\right)=O\left(N^{-n}\right).$$
		Thus for every $k<n$ and every $\beta>0$:
		$$\sum_{i=0}^{k}\beta^{-i}b_{i,k-i}^{(n)}(\psi_n)=0.$$
		This implies that $b_{i,j}^{(n)}(\psi_n)=0$ for $i+j<n$.
		
		We now go back to the proof that for $\beta>0$ independent of $N$, $\braket{\psi_n}=O\left(N^{-n}\right)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:L=0} with $n=1$, $\braket{\psi_1}=O(1/N)$. Now assume that for all $k< n$, $\braket{\psi_k}=O(N^{-k})$. Using the $2n$-th loop equation:
		\begin{equation*}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Braket{\psi_{2n}}
				&= \dfrac{1}{N}\left (\dfrac{1}{\beta }-\dfrac{1}{2}\right )
				\Braket{\tau[\psi_{2n}]} +\dfrac{1}{N}\left (\dfrac{1}{\beta }-\dfrac{1}{2}\right )
				\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2n}]}+ \dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2n}]}\\
				&\quad + \dfrac{1}{N^2\beta}	\Braket{\theta[\psi_{2n}]}+\dfrac{1}{N^2\beta}
				\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{2n}]} .
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation*}
		The \textit{a priori} bound Proposition \ref{a priori bound} gives the following estimates:
		$$\dfrac{1}{N}|
		\Braket{\tau[\psi_{2n}]}\lesssim\dfrac{\log ^{n-\frac{1}{2}}N}{N^{n+\frac12}},\quad\dfrac{1}{N} |
		\Braket{\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2n}]} |\lesssim\dfrac{\log ^{n}N}{N^{n+1}},\quad|\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2n}]}|\lesssim\dfrac{\log ^{n+\frac12}N}{N^{n+\frac12}},$$
		and $$\dfrac{1}{N^2}|\Braket{\theta[\psi_{2n}]}|\lesssim\dfrac{\log ^{n-1}N}{N^{n+1}}, \quad\dfrac{1}{N^2}	|
		\Braket{\Theta[\psi_{2n}]}|\lesssim\dfrac{\log ^{n-\frac{1}{2}}N}{N^{n+\frac32}}.$$
		This leads to the improved bound $|\braket{\psi_{2n}}|\lesssim N^{-n-\frac12}\log ^{n+\frac12}N$. Applying to the $(2n-1)$-th loop equation the previous control for $\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi_{2n-1}]}$ and the \textit{a priori} bound for the remaining terms leads to the estimate $\braket{\psi_{2n-1}}=o(N^{-n})$. Doing the same for the $2n-2$, $2n-3$ ... $n$-th equation and applying the best bound between the improved bound and the bound from the induction hypothesis when available or the \textit{a priori} bound otherwise leads to $\braket{\psi_n}=O(N^{-n})$. This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-2 -->
<!-- rem-2: body_md -->
We proved Proposition \ref{prop:asympcorrelators}, in the case $\alpha\gg(\log N)^{k}$ for all $k\geq1$ but up to a slight modification of the proof, the result remains true for $\alpha\gg(\log N)^{1+\varepsilon}$ for some $\varepsilon>0$.
<!-- end rem-2 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop: nu holomoprhic}
		For all $n\geq1$ and $f\in\mathcal{C}^{r}([-1,1])$ for $r$ large enough:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:constrait nu i}
			\nu_n(f)=\nu_{n-1}\left(\Xi^{-1}[f]'\right)+\dfrac{1}{2}\Bigg(\sum^{n-1}_{k=1}\nu_k\otimes\nu_{n-k}\Bigg) \left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[f]\right) 
		\end{equation}
		Furthermore, denoting the set of $n$-compositions of by $$\mathscr{C}_{k}^{n}\coloneqq\bigg\{\bm{i}=(i_1,\dots,i_n),\,\sum_{j=1}^{n}i_j=k,\,i_j\geq1\bigg\},$$
		we have for all $m,n\geq1$, $$b_{m,0}^{(n)}=\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}_m^{n}}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}\nu_{i_k}.$$
		Finally, let $\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{C}$ an open set, and for all $z\in\mathcal{O}$, let $f_z\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ such that for all $\lambda\in\mathbb{R}$, $z\in\mathcal{O}\mapsto f_z(\lambda)$ is analytic. Thus $z\in\mathcal{O}\mapsto\nu_n(f_z)$ is analytic for all $n\geq0$.
<!-- end prop-5 -->

\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Tensor formula for $b_{m,0}^{(n)}$ and constraints on $\nu_i$'s.\endgroup
		                                                                                                                                                                             
		                                                                                                                                                              
		The scheme of the proof is represented in Figure \ref{fig: triangular induction}.

				\node[box] (c24) at (2,-7) {$\substack{2\nu_1\nu_4
						\\+2\nu_2\nu_3}$};

						\\+3\nu_1\nu_2^{\otimes2}}$};

			\end{tikzpicture}
			\caption{Coefficients $b_i(\psi_j)=b_{i,0}^{(j)}(\psi_j)$ where $\psi_j$ is a function of $j$ variables. We prove by induction that the result holds on the $k$-th diagonal \textit{i.e.} for $(b_{n+k}(\psi_n))_{n\geq1}$. For each $n,k$, the contributions to the $n$-th term on the $k$-th diagonal $b_{n+k}(\psi_n)$ are given by the previous term on the $k$-th diagonal $b_{n+k-1,0}^{(n-1)}$ (the top-left term), and two terms on the $(k-1)$-th diagonal, a $b_{n+k-1,0}^{(n)}$ (the top-middle term) and by a $b_{n+k,0}^{(n+1)}$-term (the right term) which is summarized in \eqref{eq:figure}.}
			\label{fig: triangular induction}
		\end{figure}We first show that, $b_{n,0}^{(n)}=\nu_1^{\otimes n}$. By Proposition \ref{prop:asympcorrelators} the $n$-th loop equation (see \eqref{DSlvlnthm}), we have for $\psi_{n}:(x_1,\dots,x_{n})\mapsto\prod_{i=1}^{n}1_i(x_i)$ with for all $i\in\llbracket1,n\rrbracket$, $1\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,r}$ for $\kappa>0$ and $r$ large enough:
		$$
		\Braket{\psi_{n}}
		=  \dfrac{1}{\alpha }
		\braket{\tau\big[\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n}1_j\big]} +o\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\right)=\dfrac{\tau[1_1]}{\alpha}\braket{\bigotimes_{j=2}^{n}1_j}+o\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\right)=\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{n}\tau[1_i]}{\alpha^{n}}+o\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\right).
		$$
		Thus since $\tau[1_1]=\nu_1(1_1)$, $b_{n,0}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})=\nu_1^{\otimes n}(\psi_{n}).$
		We conclude by density of $\mathcal{C}^{r}([-1,1])^{\otimes n}$ in 
		$\allowbreak\mathcal{C}^{r}([-1,1]^{n})$ and the continuity of $b_{n,0}^{(n)}$ for $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}([-1,1])}$. We now proceed by induction and assume that:
		$$b_{j,0}^{(n)}=\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}_j^{n}}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}\nu_{i_k}$$
		holds true for the $k$ first diagonals in Figure \ref{fig: triangular induction} \textit{i.e.} for the linear maps $b_{n+j,0}^{(n)}$ for $j\in\llbracket0,k-1\rrbracket$ and all $n\geq1$. We now show that this decomposition holds for the diagonal $b_{n+k,0}^{(n)}$ for all $n\geq1$. We only treat the case of functions of the form $\psi_{n}=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n}1_{j}$ where each $1_{j}\in\mathcal{C}^{r}([-1,1])$ for $r$ large enough and conclude on the general case by density.
		
		By definition, $b_{1+k,0}^{(1)}=\nu_{k+1}$. We proceed by induction and assume that the result is true for $n-1$ and show it for $n$.  By Proposition \ref{prop:asympcorrelators}, identifying the coefficients in \eqref{DSlvl1}, we have for all $i\geq1$:
		\begin{equation*}
			b_{i,0}^{(1)}=\nu_i=\mathbf{1}_{i=1}\nu_{1}+\mathbf{1}_{i>1}\nu_{i-1}\left(\Xi^{-1}[\cdot]'\right) +\dfrac{1}{2}b_{i,0}^{(2)}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\cdot]\right).
			                                                                                                                                                                        
		\end{equation*}
		By the induction hypothesis (that the result holds on the $k$-first diagonals), for all $j\in\llbracket0,k-1\rrbracket$,
		$$b_{2+j,0}^{(2)}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\cdot]\right)=\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}_{j+2}^{2}}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{2}\nu_{i_k}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\cdot]\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{j+1}\nu_i\otimes\nu_{j+2-i}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\cdot]\right)$$
		from which we deduce that for all $i\in\llbracket1,k+1\rrbracket$:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:constraint nui}
			\nu_i=\mathbf{1}_{i=1}\nu_{1}+\mathbf{1}_{i>1}\nu_{i-1}\left(\Xi^{-1}[\cdot]'\right) +\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}\nu_\ell\otimes\nu_{i-\ell}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\cdot]\right).
		\end{equation}
		Using again Proposition \ref{prop:asympcorrelators} and identifying the coefficients in the $n$-th loop equation \eqref{DSlvlnthm}, we obtain:
		\begin{align}\label{eq:figure}
			b_{n+k,0}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})&=\tau[1_1]b_{n+k-1,0}^{(n-1)}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=2}^{n}1_j\Big) +b_{n+k-1,0}^{(n)}\Big(\partial_1\Xi^{-1}[1_1]\bigotimes_{j=2}^{n}1_j\Big) 
			\\&\quad+ \dfrac{1}{2}b_{n+k,0}^{(n+1)}\Big(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[1_{1}]\bigotimes_{j=2}^{n}1_j\Big) \nonumber.
		\end{align}
		Now, using the induction hypothesis (that the result holds true for the $(n-1)$-th coefficient on the $k$-th diagonal and on the whole $(k-1)$-th diagonal) and that $\tau[1_1]=\nu_1(1_1)$, leads to:
		\begin{align*}
			b_{n+k,0}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})
			&=\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{n}_{n+k}}\mathbf{1}_{i_1=1}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\nu_{i_j}\left(1_{j}\right)+\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{n}_{n+k-1}}\mathbf{1}_{i_1>0}\;\nu_{i_1}\left(\Xi^{-1}[1_{1}]'\right) \prod_{j=2}^{n}\nu_{i_j}\left(1_{j}\right)
			\\&\quad\quad+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{n}_{n+k}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_1-1}\nu_\ell\otimes\nu_{i_1-\ell}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[1_{1}]\right) \prod_{j=2}^{n}\nu_{i_j}\left(1_{j}\right).
		\end{align*}
		We now use, use $i_1+1\rightarrow i_1$ in the second sum and \eqref{eq:constraint nui} to deduce that:
		\begin{align*}
			b_{n+k,0}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})&=\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{n}_{n+k}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{i_1>1}\;\nu_{i_1-1}\left(\Xi^{-1}[1_{1}]'\right)+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_1-1}\nu_\ell\otimes\nu_{i_1-\ell}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[1_{1}]\right)\Big)  \prod_{j=2}^{n}\nu_{i_j}\left(1_{j}\right)
			\\&\quad+\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{n}_{n+k}}\mathbf{1}_{i_1=1}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\nu_{i_j}\left(1_{j}\right)
			\\&=\sum_{\bm{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{n}_{n+k}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n}\nu_{i_j}\left(\psi_{n}\right).
		\end{align*}
		This establishes the result for $b_{n+k,0}^{(n)}$ and thus for the $(k+1)$-th diagonal by induction in Figure \ref{fig: triangular induction}, which concludes the induction on $k$. This also shows that \eqref{eq:constraint nui} holds for all $i\geq1$. 
		
		\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Analyticity of $z\mapsto\nu_i(f_z)$.\endgroup  We first show that for all $x\in[-1,1]$, $z\mapsto\Xi^{-1}[f_z](x)$ is analytic. The map $g_{x,t}:z\in\mathcal{O}\mapsto\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-t^2}}\dfrac{f_z(x)-f_z(t)}{x_1-t}$ is holomorphic for all $x\in[-1,1]$, $t\in(-1,1)$ and for all compact set $K\subset\mathcal{O}$ and $z\in K$, $|g_{x,t}(z)|\leq \|\partial_xf_z\|_{\infty,K}$ which is $t$-integrable on $(-1,1)$. Thus the result follows by \eqref{eq:inverse Xi formula} and the analyticity under the integral sign Theorem. The analyticity of $z\mapsto\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[f_z](x_1,x_2)$ for all $x_1,x_2\in[-1,1]$, follows easily. Furthermore, the map $$h_{x,t}:z\in\mathcal{O}\mapsto\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-t^2}}\dfrac{f_z(t)-f_z(x)-\partial_zf_z(x)(t-x)}{(x-t)^2}$$ is holomorphic for all $-1\leq t,x\leq1$ and for all compact set $K\subset\mathcal{O}$ and $z\in K$, $|h_{x,t}(z)|\leq \|\partial_x^{2}f_z\|_{\infty,K}$, thus $z\mapsto\partial_{x}\Xi^{-1}[f_z](x)$ is analytic for all $x\in[-1,1]$.
		
		We now show the result by induction on $n$. For $n=1$, we have by Definition \ref{def:nu1 theta}:
		$$\nu_1(f_z)=\tau[f_z]=\int_{-1}^{1}\partial_x\Xi^{-1}[f_z](x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x).$$
		Thus since $z\mapsto\partial_x\Xi^{-1}[f_z](x)$ is holomorphic for all $x\in[-1,1]$ and $$|\partial_x\Xi^{-1}[f_z](x)|\leq\max_{(x,z)\in[-1,1]\times K}|\partial_x\Xi^{-1}[f_z](x)|$$ for all compact $K\subset\mathcal{O}$ and $z\in K$, $z\in\mathcal{O}\mapsto\nu_1(f_z)$ is holomorphic. The result follows by induction using \eqref{eq:constrait nu i}.
<!-- end pro-3 -->
<!-- rem-3: body_md -->
\begin{enumerate}
			\item The map $\nu_1$  can be made explicit in the Gaussian case, it is given by the mean in the Johansson's CLT \cite{johansson}. It was already shown in \cite{benaych2015poisson}, that:
			$$\bm{\mu}_N-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\nu_1=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\delta_{-1}+\delta_{1}\right)-\dfrac{1}{\pi\sqrt{1-x^{2}}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x $$
			where the convergence holds when tested against sufficiently smooth function. Furthermore $\nu_2$ is given by, using \eqref{eq:constraint nui}:
			$\nu_2(\psi)=\nu_1\left(\Xi^{-1}[\psi]'\right)+\dfrac{1}{2}\nu_{1}^{\otimes2}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi]\right).$
			\item For example, for a symmetric function $\psi_3$ of 3 variables, $$b_{5,0}^{(3)}(\psi_3)=\left[3\nu_1^{\otimes2}\nu_3+3\nu_1\nu_2^{\otimes2}\right](\psi_3), $$
			see Figure \ref{fig: triangular induction} for the general pattern.
			\item From, $\Xi^{-1}[\widetilde{1}]=0$ on $[-1,1]$ by \eqref{eq:inverse Xi formula}, one can see by \eqref{eq:constrait nu i} that $\nu_k(\widetilde{1})=0$ for all $k\geq1$. This shows that for all $k\geq0$, $\mu_k(\widetilde{1})=1$.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end rem-3 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:mun stieltjes}Let $z\in\mathcal{U}_+$, denoting by $m_p(z)\coloneqq\mu_p\left(\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z}\right) $, we have for all $p\geq0$ with the convention $m_{-1}=0$ and where $W_z:\lambda\mapsto\tfrac{V'(\lambda)-V'(z)}{\lambda-z}$:
		$$m_{p}(z)^{2}+V'(z)m_{2p}(z)+\mu_{2p}\left(W_z\right)+\dfrac{2}{\alpha}\partial_zm_{2p-1}(z)=0.$$
		Furthermore,  setting $\sigma_k(z)\coloneqq\nu_k\left(\tfrac{1}{\cdot-z}\right)$, we have for all $k\geq1$:
		$$\sigma_k(z) =-\dfrac{\partial_z\sigma_{k-1}(z)+\dfrac{1}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\sigma_j(z)\sigma_{k-j}(z)+\dfrac{\nu_k\left(W_z\right)}{2}}{S(z)\sqrt{z^{2}-1}}.$$
		Let $\delta>0$, then for all $z\in\mathcal{U}_+$ such that $\operatorname{Re}{z}\in(-1+\delta,1-\delta)$ and $p\geq0$, $\operatorname{Im} m_p(z)\geq0$ for $\alpha$ large enough (depending on $\delta$, $p$).
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- pro-4: body_md -->
We use \eqref{eq:constrait nu i} with $f=\Xi[g]$, Definition \ref{def:ope D} to get:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:constraintnukproof}
			\dfrac{1}{2}\nu_k(V'g)-\nu_k\otimes\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\left(\mathcal{D}[g]\right) =\nu_{k-1}\left(g'\right)+\dfrac{1}{2}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\nu_j\otimes\nu_{k-j}\Bigg) \left(\mathcal{D}[g]\right).
		\end{equation}
		Combining the second term in the LHS with the sum in the RHS, multiplying by $\alpha^{-k}$ and summing for $k\in\llbracket1,2n\rrbracket$, we get:
		$$\mu_{2n}(V'g)-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(V'g) =\dfrac{2}{\alpha}\mu_{2n-1} \left(g'\right)+\mu_n^{\otimes2}  \left(\mathcal{D}[g]\right)-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{\otimes2} \left(\mathcal{D}[g]\right).$$
		Finally using \eqref{eq:contrainst mu infinity}, taking $g(x)=(x-z)^{-1}$ and using:
		$$\mu_{2n} (V'g)=V'(z)m_{2n}(z)+\left(\sum_{k=0}^{2n}\dfrac{\nu_k\left(W_z\right)}{\alpha^{k}}\right),\quad\mu_{n}^{\otimes2}  \left(\mathcal{D}[g]\right)=-m_n(z)^{2},\quad \partial_zm_{2n-1}(z)=-\mu_{2n-1} \left(g'\right)$$
		yields the first formula.
		
		For the second one, we use again \eqref{eq:constraintnukproof} to deduce that:
		$$	\dfrac{1}{2}\nu_k\left(W_z\right)+\left[\dfrac{V'(z)}{2}+\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\left(\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z}\right)\right] \sigma_k(z) =-\partial_z\sigma_{k-1}(z)-\dfrac{1}{2}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\sigma_j(z)\sigma_{k-j}(z)\Bigg).$$
		Thus  to conclude about the formula, we just use \eqref{roots}: $$V'(z)+2\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\left(\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z}\right)=S(z)\sqrt{z^{2}-1}$$ where the RHS is non zero close to the bulk. Finally, the positivity of $\operatorname{Im} m_n(z)$ for all $n\geq0$ above the bulk, comes from the fact $m_0$ is the Stieltjes transform of $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}$ whose density is strictly positive and that $\nu_k \left(\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z}\right)$ is bounded above the bulk in $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k}}$-norm on $(-1+\delta,1-\delta)+\mathrm{i}[0,+\infty)$. From this, it is easy to see by a simple induction and the fact $\nu_k\left(W_z\right)/[S(z)\sqrt{z^{2}-1}]$ is bounded at infinity, that $\sigma_{k}(z)$ is bounded above the bulk for all $k\geq0$. Thus for $\alpha$ large enough it yields the result.
<!-- end pro-4 -->
<!-- def-4: body_md -->
\label{def:improved recentring}
		Let $k\geq1$ and $f\in\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})$ for $r$ large enough (depending on $k$), we define the linear map $L_N^{(k)}(f)$ by:
		$$L_N^{(k)}(f)\coloneqq (\bm{\mu}_N-\mu_k)(f).$$
		We also define the anisotropy term by:
		$$A_N^{(k)}(f)\coloneqq \left(\bm{\mu}_N-\mu_k\right)^{\otimes2}\left(\mathcal{D}[f]\right) .$$
<!-- end def-4 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:rewriting1stloopeq}
		Let $j\geq1$ and $g\in\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})$ for $r$ large enough, then the following holds true:
		\begin{multline*}
			-L_N^{(0)}(g)+\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])+ \left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right) \bm{\mu}_N(\Xi^{-1}[g]')
			\\=-L_N^{(j)}(g)+\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(\lfloor j/2\rfloor)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])+(\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor j/2\rfloor}\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(j-i)})\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right) + \dfrac{1}{\alpha}  L_N^{(j-1)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')-\dfrac{\bm{\mu}_N(\Xi^{-1}[g]')}{2N} .
		\end{multline*}
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- pro-5: body_md -->
We prove the statement by induction. For $j=1$, we just use the fact that, by \eqref{eq:constrait nu i} with $n=1$: $$\nu_1(g)=\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(\Xi^{-1}[g]').$$
		Assume that the proposition is true for a $j\geq1$, defining:
		$$F_j\coloneqq\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(\lfloor j/2\rfloor)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])+(\sum_{i=1}^{(\lfloor j/2\rfloor)}\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(j-i)})\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right),$$
		we first notice that if $j=2\ell$ is even then:
		\begin{equation*}
			F_{2\ell}=F_{2\ell+1}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2\ell+1}}(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\nu_i\otimes \nu_{2\ell+1-i})\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right)
		\end{equation*}
		If $j=2\ell+1$ is odd, then using that for a symmetric function $g_2(x,y)$:
		$$L_N^{(k)\otimes2}(g_2)=L_N^{(k+1)\otimes2}(g_2)+\dfrac{2}{\alpha^{k+1}}\big(\nu_{k+1}\otimes L_N^{(k+1)}\big)(g_2)+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2(k+1)}}\nu_{k+1}^{\otimes2}(g_2),$$
		we obtain: 
		\begin{equation*}
			F_{2\ell+1}=F_{2\ell+2}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2\ell+2}}(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}\nu_i\otimes \nu_{2\ell+2-i})\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right).
		\end{equation*}
		Thus for all $j\geq1$:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:proof rewriting loopeq anis}
			F_j=F_{j+1}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{j+1}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor \frac{j+1}{2}\rfloor}\nu_i\otimes \nu_{j+1-i}\Big)\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right).
		\end{equation}
		Now, using \eqref{eq:proof rewriting loopeq anis}, we have:
		\begin{multline*}
			-L_N^{(j)}(g)+F_j+\dfrac{1}{\alpha} L_N^{(j-1)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')=-L_N^{(j+1)}(g)+F_{j+1} + \dfrac{1}{\alpha}  L_N^{(j)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')
			\\-\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{j+1}}\bigg(\nu_{j+1}(g)-\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor  \frac{j+1}{2}\rfloor}\nu_{j+1-i}\otimes\nu_i\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right)-\nu_{j}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')\bigg).
		\end{multline*}
		Finally, using the induction hypothesis and \eqref{eq:constrait nu i} with $n=j+1$ conclude the proof.
<!-- end pro-5 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:initial concentration}
	\textit{(i)}There exists $c,C>0$ such that for all $t>C\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{\alpha}}$:
		$$\mathbb{P}_N\left(d(\bm{\mu}_N,\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})>t\right)\leq e^{-ct^{2}N\alpha}.$$
		\textit{(ii)} Furthermore, let $M>0$ such that $V(x)=x^{2}+C$ for all $|x|\geq M$ and $\chi\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\mathbf{1}_{[-M,M]}\leq\chi\leq\mathbf{1}_{[-2M,2M]}$, for all $g\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\leq1$, there exists $c,C'>0$ such that for all $t\geq C'\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{\alpha}}$:
		$$\mathbb{P}_N\left(|\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))|>t\right)\leq e^{-ct^{2}N\alpha}.$$
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- pro-6: body_md -->
\textit{(i)} We start with $\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}$ defined in Definition \ref{def:spacedconfig}, we have:
		$$d(\bm{\mu}_N,\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})\leq d(\bm{\mu}_N,\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u})+d(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}).$$
		In the RHS, the first is term is a $O(1/N^{3})$ by Definition \ref{def:spacedconfig} and for a constant $C>0$:
		$$\Big|\int_\mathbb{R} f(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})(x)\Big|^{2}\leq C\Big|\int_\mathbb{R}|t|^{1/2}\widehat{f}(t)\dfrac{\overline{\widehat{(\mu_{N,u}-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}})}(t)}{|t|^{1/2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\Big|\leq C\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^{2}\mathfrak{D}^{2}[\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}].$$
		We thus deduce that by Proposition \ref{thm:concentration}, there exists $C,c>0$ such that for all $t>C\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{\alpha}}$:
		$$\mathbb{P}_N\left(d(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})>t\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_N\Big(\mathfrak{D}^{2}[\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}]>\dfrac{1}{C}(t-\dfrac{C}{N^{3}})^{2}\Big)\leq e^{-ct^{2}N\alpha}.$$
		This concludes the proof of \textit{(i)}. For the point \textit{(ii)}, we use again Markov inequality:
		$$\mathbb{P}_N\left(|\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))|>t\right)\leq e^{-t^{2}N\alpha}\mathbb{E}_N\left[\exp(N\alpha\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))^{2})\right].$$
		Furthermore, by Proposition \ref{thm:concentration}, we have for some $C'''>0$
		$$\mathbb{E}_N\left[\exp(N\alpha\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))^{2})\right]\leq e^{C'' N\log N}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\alpha \left(\frac{1}{2}V_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)-g(\lambda)\right)\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \lambda\right)^{N} $$
		Now since there exists $c>0$ (independent of $g$) such that for all $\lambda\geq 2M$, $\frac{1}{2}V_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)-g(\lambda)\geq c \lambda^{2}$, the integral converges and we have:
		$$\mathbb{E}_N\left[\exp(N\alpha\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))^{2})\right]\leq e^{C'' N\log N}\left(C+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-c\lambda^{2}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \lambda\right)^{N}\leq e^{C''' N\log N},$$
		which allows to conclude that for constants $c'',C'''>0$:
			$$\mathbb{P}_N\left(|\bm{\mu}_N(f(1-\chi))|>t\right)\leq e^{-t^{2}N\alpha+C'''N\log N}\leq e^{-c''t^{2}N\alpha}.$$\qedsymbol{}
<!-- end pro-6 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:inital moment estimate}
		\textit{(i)} Let $f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}<\infty$, then for all $q\geq1$:
		$$\|L_N^{(0)}(f)\|_{q}\lesssim \left(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}+\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}\right) \left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right ).$$
		\textit{(ii)} Let $g\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})$, under the same assumptions as in \textit{(ii)} in Lemma \ref{lem:initial concentration}, we have for all $q\geq1$:
			$$\|\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))\|_{q}\lesssim \|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right ).$$
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- pro-7: body_md -->
Setting $\widetilde{f}=f/(\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}+\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})})$, we have for a constant $C>0$ independent of $f$:		\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}[|L_N^{(0)}(\widetilde{f})|^{q}]&=q\int_{0}^{+\infty}x^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\left(|L_N^{(0)}(\widetilde{f})|>x\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x
		\\&\lesssim C^{q}q\left(\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{q/2}+C^{q}q \int_{C\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{\alpha}}}^{+\infty}x^{q-1}e^{-cx^{2}N\alpha}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x
		\\&\lesssim C^{q}q\left(\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{q/2}+C^{q}q \left(\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}\right)^{q/2}\Gamma(q/2)
		\\&\lesssim C^{q}q\left(\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{q/2}+C^{q}q\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}\right)^{q/2}.
	\end{align*}
	We thus, obtain:
	$$\|L_N^{(0)}(f)\|_q\lesssim (\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}+\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})})\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right).$$
	This concludes the proof of \textit{(i)}. Setting $\widetilde{g}=g/\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}$, the proof of \textit{(ii)} is identical.
<!-- end pro-7 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop: moment estimates}
			Let $k\geq0$, there exists $\ell_k\geq1$ such that for all $g\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\ell_k}(\mathbb{R})$ and for all $q\geq1$:
		$$	\|L_N^{(k)}(g)\|_q \lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell_k}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right).$$
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- pro-8: body_md -->
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Case $k=0$.\endgroup Taking $F=N\bm{\mu}_N(f)$ in \eqref{eq:def L}, it is easy to see that by using \eqref{eq:contrainst mu infinity}:
		\begin{align}\label{eq:eq boucle L}
			\dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N\alpha}&=-\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right) \bm{\mu}_N(f'')+\dfrac{1}{2}\bm{\mu}_N(V'f')-\dfrac{1}{2}\bm{\mu}_N^{\otimes2}(\mathcal{D}[f'])\nonumber
			\\&=L_N^{(0)}(\Xi[f'])-\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(0)}(f')- \left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right) \bm{\mu}_N(f'').
		\end{align}
		One can then obtain the following decomposition:
		$$X_0=\dfrac{1}{N}\left(\mathcal{L} \left[F\right] +Z_0\right) ,$$
		where $X_0=\alpha L_N^{(0)}\left(\Xi[f']\right)$ and $$Z_0\coloneqq N\alpha\left[\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right) \bm{\mu}_N(f'')+\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(0)}(f')\right] .$$
		We now set $g=\Xi[f']$ and suppose that $g\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})$ for $\ell$ large enough, thus by Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi} and \ref{prop:bound_normq} with $\lambda=N$:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:stein inequality}
					\|X_0\|_q \le \sqrt{\dfrac{q \|\Gamma_0 \|_{q/2}}{N}} +\dfrac{ \|Z_0\|_q }{N}\lesssim\alpha\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\|g\Xi^{-1}[g]'\|_{\infty}}{N\alpha}}+\|A_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])\|_q+\dfrac{\|\Xi^{-1}[g]'\|_{\infty}}{\alpha}\right)  , 
		\end{equation}
		where we noticed that
		$$\Gamma_0\coloneqq \nabla F \cdot\nabla X_0=\dfrac{\alpha}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(\lambda_i)\Xi^{-1}[g]'(\lambda_i)=\alpha\bm{\mu}_N(g\Xi^{-1}[g]').$$
		To control $|A_N^{(0)}(g)|$, we use Helffer–Sjöstrand formula from Proposition
		\ref{prop:Helffer–Sjöstrand} with $\delta=1$ and a pseudo-analytic extension of degree $4$. We start by taking $\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_c(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\mathbf{1}_{[-1/2,1/2]}\leq\chi\leq\mathbf{1}_{[-1,1]}$ and $h\in\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$, this yields:
		$$\dfrac{h(\lambda)-h(\lambda')}{\lambda-\lambda'}=\dfrac{-1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{\bar{\partial}\big(h_4(z)\chi(y)\big)}{(\lambda-z)(\lambda'-z)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{2}z$$
		and thus by Fubini:
		$$|A_N^{(0)}(h)|\lesssim\iint_{\mathbb{C}}|\bar{\partial}\big(h_4(z)\chi(y)\big)|\cdot|(s_N-m_\mathrm{eq})(z)|^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{2}z.$$
		We thus obtain:
		\begin{align*}
				\mathbb{E}_N\left[|A_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])|^{q}\right]&\lesssim\mathbb{E}_N\left[\iint\hdots\iint\prod_{j=1}^{q}|\bar{\partial}\big(h_4(z_j)\chi(y_j)\big)|\cdot|(s_N-m_\mathrm{eq})(z_j)|^{2} \cdot\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{2}z_j\right]
				\\&\lesssim\iint\hdots\iint\mathbb{E}_N\left[\prod_{j=1}^{q}|(s_N-m_\mathrm{eq})(z_j)|^{2} \right]\prod_{j=1}^{q}|\bar{\partial}\big(h_4(z_j)\chi(y_j)\big)|\cdot\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{2}z_j.
		\end{align*}
		Now for all $y_1,\dots,y_q\in(0,1)$ with $z_j=x_j+\mathrm{i} y_j$, we have by Hölder inequality and Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate}:
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb{E}_N\left[\prod_{j=1}^{q}|(s_N-m_\mathrm{eq})(z_j)|^{2} \right]&\leq\prod_{j=1}^{q}\|(s_N-m_\mathrm{eq})(z_j)\|_{2q}^{2}
			\\&=\prod_{j=1}^{q}\|L_N^{(0)}(\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z_j})\|_{2q}^{2}
			\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{q} \prod_{j=1}^{q}\left(\|\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z_j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z_j}\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}\right)^{2}
			\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{q} \prod_{j=1}^{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{y_j^{2}}+\dfrac{1}{y_j}\right)^{2}
			\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)^{q} \prod_{j=1}^{q}\dfrac{1}{y_j^{4}}.
		\end{align*}
		A simple computation gives: $$\bar{\partial}\big(h_4(z)\chi(y)\big)=\bar{\partial}\big(\sum_{j=0}^4 \frac{1}{j!} (\mathrm{i} y)^j h^{(j)}(x)\chi(y)\big)=\left( \dfrac{\chi(y)}{2}\frac{(\mathrm{i} y)^4 }{4!}h^{(5)}(x)+\dfrac{\mathrm{i}\chi'(y)}{2}h_4(z) \right)\underset{y\rightarrow0}{=}O(y^{4}).$$
		We thus obtain:
		\begin{align*}
			\|A_N^{(0)}(h)\|_q&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)\iint\dfrac{1}{y^{4} }|\bar{\partial}\big(h_4(z)\chi(y)\big)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{2}z
			\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)\int_\mathbb{R}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x\left(|h^{(5)}(x)|+\int_{1/2}^{1}\dfrac{\chi'(y)}{2}|h_4(z)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y\right) 
			\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)\|h \|_{\mathcal{C}^{5}(\mathbb{R})}.
		\end{align*}
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      
                 
                                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                                                                         
               
                                                                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                                              
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          
               
   
                                                                                                                                                                                                         
                 
                                                                                      
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                                                                                              
                                                                                                       
               
                                                                                                                                       
		This leads to the following bound (noticing that $\Xi^{-1}[\mathcal{C}_c^{6}(\mathbb{R})]\subset\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$):
		$$\|A_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])\|_{q}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{6}(\mathbb{R})} \left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha} \right )$$
		and thus for all $1\leq q\leq N\log N$:
				\begin{equation*}
			\| L_N^{(0)}(g)\|_q \lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right)\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right) .
		\end{equation*}
		We can now bootstrap this estimate, using that:
		$\|\dfrac{1}{\cdot-z}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\dfrac{1}{y^{\ell+1}},$
		and taking the pseudo-analytic extension of degree $\ell+1$, we obtain in the exact same way, for some $\ell'\geq1$:
		$$\|A_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])\|_{q}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})} \left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{(\log N)^{2}}{\alpha^{2}} \right ).$$
		It leads to for all $q\leq N\log N$, using that $\alpha\gg(\log N)^{k}$ for all $k\geq1$:
			\begin{equation*}
			\| L_N^{(0)}(g)\|_q \lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right) .
		\end{equation*}
                                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                
                                              
                                                                                                                      
		By Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate}, when $q\geq N\log N$, it is easy to see that:
		$$\|L_N^{(0)}(g)\|_{q}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right)\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}.$$
		This concludes the proof for the case $k=0$.
		
		\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Induction step.\endgroup Suppose for all $j\leq k-1$, there exists some $\ell_j\geq1$ such that:
		$$\|L_N^{(j)}(g)\|_{q}\lesssim \|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell_j}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{j+1}}\right).$$We now use again \eqref{eq:eq boucle L} together with Proposition \ref{prop:rewriting1stloopeq}, to obtain with $g=\Xi[f']$:
		\begin{align}\label{eq:X_k+Z_k}
			\begin{aligned}
				\alpha^{k+1}\dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N\alpha}&=\alpha^{k+1}L_N^{(k)}(g)-\alpha^{k+1}\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(\lfloor\frac{k}{2} \rfloor)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])-\alpha^{k+1}(\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor \frac{k}{2}\rfloor}\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(k-i)})\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right)
				\\&\quad- \alpha^{k} L_N^{(k-1)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')+\alpha^{k+1}\dfrac{\bm{\mu}_N(\Xi^{-1}[g]')}{2N}
				\\&=X_k-\dfrac{\alpha^{k}}{N}Z_k,
			\end{aligned}
		\end{align}
		with $X_k=\alpha^{k+1}L_N^{(k)}(g)$ and
		$$Z_k=N\alpha\left(\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(\lfloor\frac{k}{2} \rfloor)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])+(\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor \frac{k}{2}\rfloor}\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(k-i)})\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right)+ \dfrac{1}{\alpha} L_N^{(k-1)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')	-\dfrac{\bm{\mu}_N(\Xi^{-1}[g]')}{2N}\right).$$
		We now use again
		Proposition \ref{prop:bound_normq} with $\lambda=N/\alpha^{k}$, to deduce that:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:stein inequality k}
			\begin{aligned}
				\|X_k\|_q &\le \sqrt{\dfrac{q\alpha^{k} \|\Gamma_k \|_{q/2}}{N}} +\dfrac{ \alpha^{k}\|Z_k\|_q }{N}
				\\&\lesssim\alpha^{k+1}\Bigg(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\|g\Xi^{-1}[g]'\|_{\infty}}{N\alpha}}+\|A_N^{(\lfloor\frac{k}{2} \rfloor)}(\Xi^{-1}[g])\|_q+\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor \frac{k}{2}\rfloor}\|\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(k-i)}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right)\|_q
				\\&\quad+ \dfrac{1}{\alpha}\|L_N^{(k-1)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')\|_q+\dfrac{1}{N}\|\Xi^{-1}[g]'\|_\infty\Bigg),
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		where we noticed that
		$$\Gamma_k=\alpha^{k}\Gamma_0= \alpha^{k}\nabla F \cdot\nabla X_0=\alpha^{k+1}\bm{\mu}_N(g\Xi^{-1}[g]').$$
		By induction hypothesis, we have the following estimates for some $\ell\geq1$:
		$$\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor \frac{k}{2}\rfloor}\|\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(k-i)}\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[g]\right)\|_q\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})} \left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right) $$
		and
		$$\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\|L_N^{(k-1)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]')\|_q\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right).$$
		Finally, to control the anisotropy, we use the same Helffer–Sjöstrand formula as before, choosing a pseudo analytic extension of degree $m$ sufficiently large (depending only on $k$), we obtain just as before with $h=\Xi^{-1}[g]$:
			\begin{align*}
			\|A_N^{(\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor)}(h)\|_q&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor+2}}\right)\int_\mathbb{R}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x\left(|h^{(m+1)}(x)|+\int_{1/2}^{1}\dfrac{\chi'(y)}{2}|h_m(z)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y\right) 
			\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right)\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}(\mathbb{R})}\\&\lesssim\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right)\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}
		\end{align*}
		where we used that $2\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor+2\geq k+1$ and Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi}.
		
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       
                                                                                                                                                     
               
                                                                                                                      
                 
                                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                                                                                      
               
                                     
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       
                                                                                                                   
		Using \eqref{eq:stein inequality k}, and collecting all the bounds, it leads to, when $q\leq N\log N$:
		\begin{equation*}
			\|L_N^{(k)}(g)\|_q \lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right).
		\end{equation*}
		Furthermore, when $q\geq N\log N$, from Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate}, we have:
	\begin{align*}
		\|L_N^{(k)}(g)\|_q\lesssim\|L_N^{(0)}(g)\|_q+\dfrac{\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}}{\alpha}&\lesssim(\|g\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})})\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)
		\\&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}.
	\end{align*}
		This concludes the induction and the proof.
<!-- end pro-8 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
\label{prop: moment estimates infinity}
			Let $\beta\gg N^{-1+\varepsilon}$ for some $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\ell>0$ (depending only on $\varepsilon$) such that for all $g\in\mathcal{C}^\ell(\mathbb{R})$, under the same assumptions as in \textit{(ii)} in Lemma \ref{lem:initial concentration}, we have for all $q\geq1$:
		$$\|\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))\|_{q}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})} \sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}.$$
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- pro-9: body_md -->
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Preliminaries.\endgroup We proceed in a similar way as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates}. We take $f(x)=O(1/x)$ at infinity and $\chi$ as in Lemma \ref{lem:initial concentration} \textit{(ii)} and set $g=\Xi[(f(1-\chi))']$ and $F=N\bm{\mu}_N(f(1-\chi))$ in \eqref{eq:def L}. We obtain for all $k\geq0$:
\begin{align*}
	\dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N\alpha}&=-\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right) \bm{\mu}_N((f(1-\chi))'')+\dfrac{1}{2}\bm{\mu}_N(V'(f(1-\chi))')-\dfrac{1}{2}\bm{\mu}_N^{\otimes2}(\mathcal{D}[(f(1-\chi))'])
	\\&=L_N^{(2k)}(\Xi[(f(1-\chi))'])-\dfrac{1}{2}A_N^{(k)}((f(1-\chi))')-(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(2k-i)})\left(\mathcal{D} [(f(1-\chi))']\right)
	\\&\quad- \dfrac{1}{\alpha} L_N^{(2k-1)}((f(1-\chi))'')+\dfrac{\bm{\mu}_N((f(1-\chi))'')}{2N}
	\\&=X-\dfrac{Z}{N\alpha}
\end{align*}
where by Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi}, $X\coloneqq L_N^{(2k)}(g)=L_N^{(2k)}(g(1-\chi))=\bm{\mu}_N(g(1-\chi))$ and:
\begin{align*}
	Z&\coloneqq \dfrac{N\alpha}{2}A_N^{(k)}(\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))+N\alpha(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\nu_i}{\alpha^{i}}\otimes L_N^{(2k-i)})\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)]\right)
	\\&\quad+NL_N^{(k-1)}((\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))')-\dfrac{\alpha}{2}\bm{\mu}_N((\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))').
\end{align*}
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Estimation of $\|Z\|_q$.\endgroup Since $f(x)=O(1/x)$ at infinity, by Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi}, $g(x)\sim V'(x)f(x)\sim xf(x)=O(1)$ at infinity, thus $$\Xi^{-1}[g(1-\chi)](x)=\dfrac{g(x)}{V(x)}(1+o(1))=O\left (\dfrac{1}{x}\right ),$$
where the above estimate is differentiable.
Since $ L_N^{(2k-1)}((\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))')= \bm{\mu}_N((\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))')$ and $g=O(1)$, we have for all $q\geq1$ by Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate}:
$$\Big\|\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right) \bm{\mu}_N((\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))')\Big\|_q\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right ).$$
Furthermore,
$$\sum_{i=1}^{k}\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{i}}\|(\nu_i\otimes L_N^{(2k-i)})\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)]\right)\|_q=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{i}}\Big\|\int_{[-M,M]^{c}}\nu_i\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(y)\Big\|_q,$$
and since for all $|y|>M$,  $\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)$ is smooth and bounded so is the map $y\allowbreak\mapsto\nu_i\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)\right)$, thus by Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate} the continuity of $\nu_i$, $\mathcal{D}$ and $\Xi^{-1}$, we have for some $r\geq0$:
\begin{multline*}
	\sum_{i=1}^{k}\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{i}}\|(\nu_i\otimes L_N^{(2k-i)})\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)]\right)\|_q\\\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right )\max_{i, |y|>M}\nu_i\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)\right)
	\\\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right )\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})}.
\end{multline*}
Finally, for the anisotropy, noticing that $\mathcal{D}[h(1-\chi)](x,y)=0$ for $|x|,|y|\leq M$, we have:
\begin{align*}
	A_N^{(k)}(\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi))&=\int_{([-M,M]^{c})^{2}}\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](x,y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(y)
	\\&\quad+\int_{[-M,M]^{c}}L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(y).
\end{align*}
For the first term, we simply use that $\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,\cdot)$ is bounded by Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi}, thus by Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate}:
\begin{align*}
	\Big\|\int_{([-M,M]^{c})^{2}}\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](x,y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(y)\Big\|_q&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}_N(1-\chi)\|_{2q}^{2}
	\\&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right).
\end{align*}
For the last term, we use:
\begin{multline*}
	\Big\|\int_{[-M,M]^{c}}L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(y)\Big\|_q
	\\\lesssim\Big\|\max_{|z|>M}|L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,z)\right)|\cdot\bm{\mu}_N(1-\chi)\Big\|_q
	\\\lesssim\Big\|\max_{|z|>M}|L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,z)\right)\Big\|_{2q}\Big\|\bm{\mu}_N(1-\chi)\Big\|_{2q}.
\end{multline*}
We now use the fact that for all $|z|>M$, $x\mapsto\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](x,z)\in\mathcal{W}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})$. Indeed, it is $\mathcal{C}^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})$ and both it and its derivative behave like $O\left(1/x^{2}\right)$ at infinity. Thus, we use the fact that, setting $Y(z)\coloneqq L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,z)\right)$ and using that $Y\in\mathcal{W}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})$ and $Y(z)\rightarrow0$ as $z\rightarrow\infty$:
$$|Y(z)|^{2}\leq\int_{-\infty}^{z}2Y(t)Y'(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\leq\|Y\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\|Y'\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})},$$
thus the following inequality holds:
$$\max_{|z|>M}|Y(z)|\leq\sqrt{\|Y\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\|Y'\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}}.$$
We then obtain by Cauchy-Schwarz, Fubini, Hölder inequality and Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates}:
\begin{align*}
	\Big\|\max_{|z|>M}|L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,z)\right)\Big\|_{2q}&\lesssim\Big\|\|Y\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}\Big\|_{2q}^{1/2}\cdot\Big\|\|Y'\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}\Big\|_{2q}^{1/2}
	\\&\lesssim\Big\|\|Y(\cdot)\|_{2q}\Big\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}^{1/2}\cdot\Big\|\|Y'(\cdot)\|_{2q}\Big\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}^{1/2}.
\end{align*}
Now, decomposing the function into a compactly supported part and a part supported near infinity and using Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates} and Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate} for the part near infinity, we get for $i\in\{0,1\}$:
\begin{align*}
	\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\partial^{i}_tY(t)\|_{2q}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|L_N^{(k)}\left(\chi(\cdot)\partial^{i}_t\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,t)\right)\|_{2q}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t
	\\&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\bm{\mu}_N\left((1-\chi)(\cdot)\partial^{i}_t\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,t)\right)\|_{2q}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t
		\\&\lesssim\int_\mathbb{R}\|\partial^{i}_t\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\cdot\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right) 
\end{align*}
For all $i\in\{0,1\}$, the map $t\mapsto\|\partial_z^{i}\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})}$ is bounded on compact sets and at infinity behaves at least like $O(1/t)$ since $\Xi^{-1}[g]$ and its derivatives vanish like $O(1/t)$ at infinity, thus the integral is finite and we have:
$$\int_\mathbb{R}\|\partial^{i}_t\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r'}(\mathbb{R})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x}{x^{2}+1}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r'}(\mathbb{R})}$$
and finally we obtain:
$$\Big\|\int_{[-M,M]^{c}}L_N^{(k)}\left(\mathcal{D}[\Xi^{-1}[g](1-\chi)](\cdot,y)\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(y)\Big\|_q\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r'}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right) .$$
We thus, obtain that for all $q\leq N\log N$:
$$\|Z\|_q\lesssim N\alpha\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r'}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right).$$
Finally, there exists $h\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})$ with $\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}$ such that:
$$\Gamma= \nabla F \cdot\nabla X=\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(f(1-\chi)\right)' (\lambda_i)\left(g(1-\chi)\right) '(\lambda_i)=\bm{\mu}_N(h(1-\chi)),$$
thus $\|\Gamma\|_{q/2}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{r}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\sqrt{\tfrac{\log N}{\alpha}}\right).$

\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}3. Conclusion.\endgroup We can now use Proposition \ref{prop:bound_normq} to obtain that there exists $\ell\geq1$, such that for all $q\leq N\log N$:
\begin{equation*}
	\|X\|_q \lesssim \sqrt{\dfrac{q \|\Gamma \|_{q/2}}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\|Z\|_q}{N\alpha}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\log N}{\alpha}\right) .
\end{equation*}
We can now bootstrap this inequality to obtain for all $k\geq0$ and $q\leq N\log N$:
\begin{equation*}
	\|X\|_q \lesssim \sqrt{\dfrac{q \|\Gamma \|_{q/2}}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\|Z\|_q}{N\alpha}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1 }{\alpha^{k+1}}\right) ,
\end{equation*}
and thus remove the second term in the RHS, taking $k$ large enough. Finally by Proposition \ref{prop:inital moment estimate} for all $q\geq N\log N$:
$$\|X\|_q\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}.$$
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-9 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:varformula}
	The following identity holds for all $g\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})$:
	$$\int_{-1}^1\Xi^{-1}[g]'(x)g(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)=
\sigma^2(g)$$
where $\sigma^2$ was defined in \eqref{eq: norm H 1/2 global} and $\Xi^{-1}[g]$ given in \eqref{eq:inverse Xi formula}.
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- pro-10: body_md -->
Let $f$ such that $f'=\Xi^{-1}[g]$, we take again the decomposition of \eqref{eq:X_k+Z_k} with $F=N\bm{\mu}_N(f)$:
		$$	\widetilde{X}_k=\sqrt{N\alpha}L_N^{(k)}(g)=\dfrac{\sqrt{N\alpha}}{\alpha^{k+1}}X_k=\dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F]+Z_k}{\sqrt{N\alpha}},$$
		Using Proposition \ref{prop:bound Wp}, we obtain with $\lambda=\sqrt{N\alpha}$:
			$$\mathbf{W}_q\left(\widetilde{X}_k,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sigma^2(g)\right)\right)\leq \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}\big(\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\sigma(g)}\| W_k\|_{q}+\|Z_k\|_{q} \big), \qquad  W_k = \nabla \widetilde{X}_k \cdot \nabla F - \sqrt{N\alpha}\sigma^{2}(g). $$
			We obtain easily using Lemma \ref{lem:varformula}:
