			$$W_k =\sqrt{N\alpha}\left(\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g'(\lambda_i)\Xi^{-1}[g](\lambda_i)-\int_{-1}^1\Xi^{-1}[g]'(x)g(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)\right)=\sqrt{N\alpha}L_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]'g),$$
			and from Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates}, Proposition \ref{prop:inverse Xi} and \ref{prop: moment estimates infinity} and using a cutoff $\chi$:, 
			\begin{align*}
				\dfrac{	\|W_k\|_q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}=\|L_N^{(0)}(\Xi^{-1}[g]'g)\|_q &\lesssim\|\Xi^{-1}[g]'g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right) \\&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\|\Xi^{-1}[g]'\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right).
				\\&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell'}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right).
			\end{align*}
			Using the exact same bounds as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates}, we obtain for all $q\geq1$ and for some $r\geq1$:
			\begin{align*}
				\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}\|Z_k\|_q&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{N\alpha} \left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\dfrac{1}{N}\right)\\&\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})} \left(\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{N\alpha}}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right).
			\end{align*}
			We thus obtain for some $r'\geq1$ and all $q\geq1$:
			$$\mathbf{W}_q\left(\sqrt{N\alpha}L_N^{(k)}(g),\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sigma^2(g)\right)\right)\lesssim C(g) \left (\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}+\dfrac{\sqrt{N\alpha}}{\alpha^{k+1}}\right )$$
			which concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-10 -->
<!-- pro-11: body_md -->
First notice that the equilibrium measure associated with the potential $V(x)=2x^{2}$ is supported on $[-1,1]$, see \cite[Section 7.2.2]{BoG2}. Using differentation under the integral sign and setting $V_t(x)=tV(x)+(1-t)2x^{2}$, we obtain:
		\begin{align*}
			-\dfrac{2}{N\alpha}\log\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_N[2x^{2}]}&=\int_0^1\mathbb{E}_N^{V_t}\left[\int_\mathbb{R} (V(x)-2x^2)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \bm{\mu}_N(x)\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t
			\\&=\int_0^1\braket{V-2x^2}^{V_t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t+\int_0^1\int_{-1}^{1}(V(x)-2x^2) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_t}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t.
		\end{align*}
		Above, we have set $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_t}=t\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}+(1-t)\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_0}$ for the equilibrium associated with the potential $V_t$ and \textit{idem} for $\mathbb{E}_N^{V_t}$ and $\braket{\cdot}^{V_t}$. The integrand in the first term of the RHS admits an expansion at all order. Showing that the each coefficients is $t$-integrable relies on \cite[Lemma 5.1]{BoG1} and the fact that the remainder is uniform in $t$ comes from the constant in the control of $\Xi^{-1}$. The fact that $\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}\log\mathcal{Z}_N\left[V\right]=-\dfrac{\mathcal{E}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})}{2}$ comes from a large deviation principle argument, see \cite[Proof of Theorem 2.6.1]{anderson2010introduction}. Finally, the term proportional to $\dfrac{1}{\alpha}$ of $-\dfrac{2}{N\alpha}\log\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_N[2x^{2}]}$ is, by Proposition \ref{prop:asympcorrelators}: $$\int_0^1\nu_1(V-2x^2)^{V_t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t=\int_0^{1}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\int_{-1}^{1}\partial_1\Xi_t^{-1}[V-2\cdot^{2}](x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)$$
		with $\nu_1(\cdot)^{V_t}$ and $\Xi_{t}$ denotes respectively the linear functional $\nu_1$ and the master operator under the choice of potential $V_t$. By \cite[Proof of Corollary 1.3]{dworaczekguera2025clt},
		\begin{align*}
			\int_0^{1}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\int_{-1}^{1}\partial_1\Xi_t^{-1}[V-2\cdot^{2}](x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)=2\int_0^{1}\partial_t\mathrm{Ent}\left[\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_t}\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t
			&= 2\left(\mathrm{Ent}\left[\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\right]-\mathrm{Ent}\left[\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_0}\right]\right)
			\\&=2\mathrm{Ent}\left[\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\right]-2\log\pi+1.
		\end{align*}
		Above we have used that $\mathrm{Ent}\left[\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_0}\right]=\log\pi-\frac{1}{2}$ by \cite{dworaczekguera2025clt}. This concludes the proof. \qedsymbol{}
<!-- end pro-11 -->
<!-- rem-4: body_md -->
\label{rem:asymp gaussian partition}We recall that the Gaussian partition is given, see \cite[Section 7.2.2]{BoG2}, by:
		$$\mathcal{Z}_N[2x^{2}]=\dfrac{N!(2\pi)^{N}\left(\frac{N\alpha}{2}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-N} }{\Gamma\left(\dfrac{\alpha}{2N}\right)^{N}\Gamma_2\left(N+1,\dfrac{2N}{\alpha},1\right)  }\cdot(4N)^{-\frac{N\alpha}{4}-\frac{N}{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}},$$
		where $\Gamma_2$ is the so-called Barnes double Gamma function, see \cite{alexanian2023barnes}.
<!-- end rem-4 -->
<!-- def-5: body_md -->
\label{def: delta}
	We define for all $z\in\mathcal{U}$ and $k\geq0$
	$$\Delta_k(z)\coloneqq \left(\bm{\mu}_N-\mu_k\right) (W_z),\hspace{1.5cm}\text{with}\quad W_z\coloneqq\dfrac{V'(\cdot)-V'(z)}{\cdot-z}.$$
<!-- end def-5 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{cor: glocon}
	There exists $c>0$ such that for all $k\geq1$ and $z\in\mathcal{U}$, for all $q\geq1$ and $M$ large enough, we have:
	$$\|L_N^{(k)}(W_z)\|_{q}\lesssim\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\right). $$
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- pro-12: body_md -->
Let $\varepsilon>0$ and $1\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\mathbf{1}_{[-M,M]}\leq1\leq\mathbf{1}_{[-M-1,M+1]}$ decomposing $W_z=W_z1+(1-1)W_z$ and using that supp $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}=[-1,1]$, we have by Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates} and \ref{prop: moment estimates infinity} using that $W_z\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ and hence bounded, we have:
\begin{align*}
	\|L_N^{(k)}(W_z)\|_{q}&\leq\|L_N^{(k)}(1 W_z)\|_{q}+\|L_N^{(k)}\left((1-1)W_z\right) \|_{q}
	\\&\lesssim \|W_z1\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\right)+\|\bm{\mu}_N\left((1-1)W_z\right) \|_{q}
	\\&\lesssim \left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\right).
\end{align*}
<!-- end pro-12 -->
<!-- lem-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:Leq}
	                                                           
	For a random variable $G(z)\in\mathcal{C}^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$, one has for  $z\in \mathcal{U}_+$,
	\[
	\mathbb{E}_N\left[\left( s_N^2(z) + V'(z) s_N(z)  + \bm{\mu}_N(W_z) + \left( \dfrac{2}{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{N}\right) \partial_z s_N(z) \right)G(z)\right] 
	= \frac1{\alpha N} 	\mathbb{E}_N\bigg[ \sum_{j=1}^{N} \frac{\partial_{\lambda_j}G(z)}{\lambda_j-z}  \bigg] . 
	\]
<!-- end lem-4 -->
<!-- lem-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:eq}
	
	There exists $\eta_0>0$ such that for $\Omega \coloneqq B+\mathrm{i}(0,\eta_0)$ where $\bar{B} \subset (-1,1)$, there exists $c>0$ such that the discriminant $ \triangle_0(z)$ of the polynomial $P_0(\cdot)$ satisfies for all $z\in \Omega$:
	$$| \triangle_0(z)| \ge c.$$
	Moreover, for all $z\in\mathcal{U}$ the Stieltjes transform $\xi_0(z)$ of the equilibrium measure is the root  of $P_0(\cdot)$ and satisfies:
	$$2m_0(z)=2\xi_0(z) = -V'(z)+2S(z)\sqrt{z^{2}-1}$$ where $\sqrt{\cdot}$ is the principal branch of the square root.
	In particular, there exists $C>0$ such that $$\|m_0\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C.$$
<!-- end lem-5 -->
<!-- pro-13: body_md -->
By the law of large numbers, for a fixed $z\in\Omega$,  $s_N(z) \to m_0(z)$ and $\partial_z s_N(z) \to \partial_z m_0(z)$ with overwhelming probability as $N\rightarrow\infty$. Thus taking the limit as $N\to\infty$ in \eqref{Leq} with $G=1$, we obtain $P_0(m_0) =0$ for all $z\in\Omega$. 
	
	Recall that for all $E\in\mathbb{R}$, the density of the equilibrium measure satisfies:
	\begin{equation} \label{PS}
		\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E) = \dfrac1\pi\lim_{\eta\to0}\operatorname{Im}{ m_0(E+\mathrm{i} \eta) }.
	\end{equation}
	The two roots of the polynomial $P_0$ are:
	\begin{equation}\label{roots}
		2m_0(z)=2\xi_0(z)=-V'(z)+2S(z)\sqrt{z^{2}-1}, \qquad  2\widetilde{\xi}_0 =  -V'(z)-2S(z)\sqrt{z^{2}-1}.
	\end{equation}
Also by \cite[Eq (B.7)]{bourgade2022optimal}, there exists $\eta_0>0$ and $c>0$ such that for all $z\in\Omega$: $$|\triangle(z)|^{1/2}=2|S(z)|\cdot|z^{2}-1|^{1/2}\geq c.$$
	Finally, the bound $\|m_0\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C$ for $z\in \Omega$ follows directly from the expression  \eqref{roots}.
<!-- end pro-13 -->
<!-- def-6: body_md -->
\label{def:Pk}
	We define for $k\geq1$ and $z\in\Omega$:
	\[
	P_k : s \in \mathbb{C}\mapsto s^2 + V'(z) s + \mu_k(W_z)  + \tfrac2\alpha \partial_z \xi_{k-1}(z), 
	\]
	where $\xi_k(z)$  is the root of $P_k$  such that for all $z\in\Omega$, $\xi_k(z) = m_0(z) +O(\alpha^{-1})$.
<!-- end def-6 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:m}
	For all $k\geq1$, $m_k$ is analytic in $\Omega$ and satisfies:
	\begin{equation}\label{mind}
		\xi_k  = \xi_{k-1} +  O(\alpha^{-k})
	\end{equation}
	where the error term is controlled in $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}$. Moreover, for all $z\in\Omega$:
	\begin{equation} \label{Pfact}
		P_k(s) = (s- \xi_k(z))(s-\widetilde{\xi}_k(z)),
	\end{equation}
	where there exists $c>0$, such that for all $z\in \Omega$, $-\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{ \xi}_k(z)  >c/2$. Finally, there exists $C_k>0$ such that for any $k\geq1$:
	\[
	\| \xi_k \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C_k ,\qquad\qquad \| \widetilde{\xi}_k \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C_k . 
	\]
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- pro-14: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on $k\geq0$ using that, according to Lemma~\ref{lem:eq}, 
	$$\|m_0\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}=\|\xi_0\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}\le C_0$$  and $| \triangle_0(z)| \ge c>0$ for $z\in \Omega$. 
	Moreover, one also has that for all $k\geq0$, $z\in\Omega\mapsto\mu_k(W_z)$ is holomorphic by Proposition \ref{prop: nu holomoprhic}, thus $\|z\mapsto\mu_k(W_z)\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C_{k-1} $.
	
	We assume that there exists $k\ge 0$ such that $\xi_k  = \xi_{k-1}+  O(\alpha^{-k})$ holds in $\Omega$ where the error is analytic in $\Omega$ and controlled in $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}$ and the discriminant of $P_k(\cdot)$ satisfies $| \triangle_k(z)| \ge 2^{-k}c $ and $\| \triangle_k \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le  C_k$.
	
	
	
	By construction, we can write $P_{k+1}(s) = P_k(s) + O(\alpha^{-(k+1)})$ in $\Omega$ where the perturbation is analytic in $\Omega$ and controlled in $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}$ (by hypothesis, one has $\|\mu_{k+1}(W_z)\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}  , \|\xi_k\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C_k$). 
	Then, the discriminant of the polynomial $P_{k+1}(\cdot)$ satisfies $\triangle_{k+1}=\triangle_k - O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}) $ so that since $\alpha\gg 1$, 
	\begin{equation} \label{deltacond}
		|\triangle_{k+1}(z)| \ge c/2^{k+1} \text{ for $z\in\Omega$ \quad\quad\quad  and }\quad\quad\quad
		\| \triangle_{k+1} \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le  C_{k+1}. 
	\end{equation}
	This implies that there exists a  branch $\sqrt{\cdot}$ such that the roots of $P_{k+1}$ (see \eqref{Pfact}) are analytic in $\Omega$ satisfy the following expansion and are given by:
	\[\begin{cases}
		2\xi_{k+1}(z)&\hspace{-0,3cm}=  -V'(z)+\sqrt{\triangle_{k+1}(z)} = 2\xi_k(z) +O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}) \\
		2	\widetilde{\xi}_{k+1}(z)&\hspace{-0,3cm}=-V'(z)- \sqrt{\triangle_{k+1}(z)}= 2\widetilde{\xi}_{k}(z) +O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}) 
	\end{cases}.\]
	Then, using \eqref{deltacond}, we verify that 
	$\| \xi_{k+1} \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} , \| \widetilde{\xi}_{k+1} \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le  C_{k+1}$. 
	
	Moreover,  according to \eqref{roots}, since there exists $c>0$ such that $|\operatorname{Im} V'(z)| \le c$  ($V$ is real-analytic), taking $\eta_0$ sufficiently small, we deduce that there exists $c_1>0$ such that $-\operatorname{Im}  \xi_0(z)\ge c_1$ for $z\in\Omega$
	.
	Hence, for any $k\in\mathbb{N}$ if $\alpha\gg1$ (depending on $k$), one also has for $z\in\Omega$, 
	\[ 
	-\operatorname{Im} \xi_k(z) = -\operatorname{Im} \xi_0(z) + O(\alpha^{-1}) \ge c_1/2 . 
	\]
	This completes the proof.
<!-- end pro-14 -->
<!-- rem-5: body_md -->
\begin{enumerate}
		\item  In the one-cut regular case, choosing $B=[-1+\delta,1-\delta]$ for  $\delta>0$,  one has $c= c_0 \delta$ where the constant $c_0>0$ depends on $V$ and $\eta_0>0$.
		\item The same arguments show that for any $q\geq1$, there are constants so that for any $k\geq1$, $\| \xi_k \|_{\mathcal{C}^q(\Omega)} \le C_{k,q}$. 
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end rem-5 -->
<!-- prop-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:approx zeta m}
	Let $k\geq0$ and $z\in\Omega$, one has $$\| \xi_k - m_k\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} =O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}).$$
<!-- end prop-12 -->
<!-- pro-15: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on $k\in\mathbb{N}$. In particular, the claim is true for $k=0$ since $\xi_0$ is exactly the Stieltjes transform of the equilibrium measure $\mu_\mathrm{eq}$. 
	According to Proposition~\ref{prop:mun stieltjes}, the Stieltjes transform of $\mu_k$ satisfies for $z\in\Omega$:
	\[
	m_{k}^{2}(z)+V'(z)m_{k}(z)+\mu_k(W_z)+\dfrac{2}{\alpha}\partial_zm_{k-1}(z) + O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}) = 0
	\]
	using that $m_{2k} = m_{k} +  O(\alpha^{-(k+1)})$ and $\mu_{2k}(W_z) = \mu_{k}(W_z)+  O(\alpha^{-(k+1)})$ where the errors are controlled in $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)}$.
	Using the induction hypothesis, $\xi_{k-1} = m_{k-1} +O(\alpha^{-k})$, we obtain:
	\[
	P_k(m_k) = O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}).
	\]
	Then, using the factorisation \eqref{Pfact} from Proposition~\ref{prop:m}, one has  $|m_k-\widetilde{\xi}_k| \ge 
	-\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{\xi}_k(z)  >c/2$ since $\operatorname{Im} m_k \ge0$ by Proposition \ref{prop:mun stieltjes} so that: 
	\[
	(m_k-\xi_k) =  O(\alpha^{-(k+1)}) .
	\]
<!-- end pro-15 -->
<!-- prop-13: body_md -->
\label{prop:Leq}
	Let $k\geq1$, $G(z)\in\mathcal{C}^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$ and  $z\in\Omega$, then:
	\begin{equation} \label{Leq}
		\mathbb{E}_N\left[\left( P_k(s_N) +  \Delta_k(z)+  \dfrac{2}{\alpha'}   \partial_zs_{N,k-1}(z)  +O(N^{-1})\right)G(z)\right] 
		= \frac1{\alpha N} 	\mathbb{E}_N\bigg[ \sum_{j=1}^{N} \frac{\partial_{\lambda_j}G(z)}{\lambda_j-z}  \bigg] 
	\end{equation}
	where $\alpha' \coloneqq (\frac1\alpha-\frac1{2N})^{-1}$ and where the error term is deterministic and controlled uniformly in $\Omega$ (the implied constants depend on $k$). 
	Moreover, there is a constant $C_k\ge 0$ such that for any $k\in\mathbb{N}$ and $z\in \Omega$:
	\begin{equation} \label{stab}
		|s_{N,k}(z)| \le C|P_{k}(s_N(z))|\wedge|P_{k}(s_N)|^{1/2} . 
	\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-13 -->
<!-- pro-16: body_md -->
Using the previous definitions, one has for all $k\in\geq1$ and $z\in\Omega$, 
	\[
	s_N^2(z) + V'(z) s_N(z)  + \bm{\mu}_N(W_z) + \left( \tfrac{2}{\alpha}-\tfrac{1}{N}\right) \partial_z s_N(z)
	= P_k(s_N(z)) +  \Delta_k(z)+  \tfrac{2}{\alpha'} \partial_z s_{N,k-1}(z)    -\tfrac{1}{N} \partial_z \xi_{k-1}(z) . 
	\]
	Then, by Lemma~\ref{lem:Leq}, this gives~\eqref{Leq} with $-\tfrac{1}{N} \partial_z \xi_{k-1}  = O(N^{-1})$
	(by Proposition~\ref{prop:m}).  Finally, by \eqref{Pfact}, one has $P_{k}(s_N) = s_{N,k}(s_N-\widetilde{\xi}_k)$ and since $\operatorname{Im}(s_N-\widetilde{\xi}_k) >c/2$ for all $z\in \Omega$ if $\alpha\gg1$. This yields the first bound in \eqref{stab}. For the second one, we use the fact that for some $c>0$:
	$$|s_N-\widetilde{\xi}_{k}|\geq|\operatorname{Im}{(s_N-\widetilde{\xi}_{k})}|\geq-c\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{\xi}_{0}}\geq c'/2>0.$$
	Now, using that:
	$$|P_k(s_N)|^{1/2}\geq|s_{N,k}|\wedge|s_N-\widetilde{\xi}_{k}|,$$
	we obtain the conclusion.
<!-- end pro-16 -->
<!-- lem-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:Lineq}
	There exists a constant $C>0$ such that for any  $k\geq1$, $q\geq1$ and $z\in\Omega$:
    
                                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                
    
		\begin{equation*}
	\mathbb{E}_N\left[ |P_k(s_N)|^{2q}  \right] 
	\leq C^{q}	\mathbb{E}_N\left[\dfrac{q^{q}}{(N\alpha\eta^{2})^{q}}\big(|s_{N,k}(z)| + 1 \big)^{q}+|\Delta_k(z)|^{2q}+  \dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2q}}   |\partial_zs_{N,k-1}(z)|^{2q} +N^{-2q} \right]
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lem-6 -->
<!-- pro-17: body_md -->
Let $k\geq1$, $z\in\Omega$ and $q\geq1$, we apply Proposition~\ref{prop:Leq} with $G(z)  = |P_k(s_N)|^{2(q-1)} P_k(s_N)^* $.
	First, observe that if $s\mapsto G(s)$ is a complex polynomial and $G(z) = G(s_N(z))$, one has:
	\begin{equation}\label{Gbound}
		\begin{aligned}
			\left | {\displaystyle  \sum_{j=1}^{N} \dfrac{\partial_{\lambda_j}G(z)}{\lambda_j-z}} \right | 
			&\le \big( |\partial_s G(s_N(z))| +  |\overline{\partial}_{s} G(s_N(z))| \big) \left | {\displaystyle  \sum_{j=1}^{N} \dfrac{\partial_{\lambda_j}{s_N}(z)}{\lambda_j-z}} \right | \\
			&\le 2\eta^{-2} | s_N(z)| \big(  |\partial_s G(s_N(z))| \vee | \overline{\partial}_{s}  G(s_N(z))| \big)
		\end{aligned},
	\end{equation}
	using the complex chain rule and that $$ \sum_{j=1}^{N} \dfrac{\partial_{\lambda_j}s_N(z)}{\lambda_j-z} =- \partial^{2}_zs_N(z)$$ and we use the bound  $|\partial^{2}_zs_N(z)| \le \eta^{-2} \operatorname{Im} s_N(z) $ for all $z\in\Omega$.
	
	Then, applying \eqref{Gbound} to $G = P_k(s)^{q-1}P_k^*(s)^{q}$, using that $\partial_s P_k(s) = 2(s-\xi_{k}) +O(1)$ according to Proposition~\ref{prop:m} (uniformly in $z\in\Omega$), there exists a constant $C_k>0$ such that: 
	\[
	|\partial_s G(s_N)| \vee | \overline{\partial}_{s}  G(s_N)| \le q \big(|s_{N,k}| +C_k \big) |P_k(s_N)|^{2(q-1)}.
	\]
	Similarly, one can bound $| s_N|  \le |s_{N,k}| + |\xi_{k}| $ so that, combined with \eqref{Gbound}, we obtain :
	\[
	\left | {\displaystyle  \sum_{j=1}^{N} \frac{\partial_{\lambda_j}G(z)}{\lambda_j-z}} \right |  \le 2q \eta^{-2} 
	\big(|s_{N,k}(z)| + C_k \big)^2 |P_k(s_N)|^{2(q-1)}.
	\]
	This leads to for all $\lambda>0$:
	\begin{multline*}
			\mathbb{E}_N\left[ |P_k(s_N)|^{2q}  \right] 
		\leq	\mathbb{E}_N\bigg[\lambda| P_k(s_N)|^{2(q-1)}  \cdot\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^{2}\lambda} \big(|s_{N,k}(z)| + C_k \big)^2  \bigg] \\+\mathbb{E}_N\left[\lambda|P_k(z)|^{2q-1}\cdot\dfrac{1}{\lambda}(|\Delta_k(z)|+  \dfrac{2}{\alpha'}   |\partial_zs_{N,k-1}(z) | +O(N^{-1}))\right].
	\end{multline*}
	We then apply Young's inequality ($xy\leq \frac{x^{a}}{a}+\frac{y^{b}}{b}$ if $a^{-1}+b^{-1}=1$) with $a=\frac{2q}{2(q-1)}$ and $b=q$ $a=\frac{2q}{2q-1}$ and $b=2q$ and obtain:
		\begin{multline*}
		\mathbb{E}_N\left[ |P_k(s_N)|^{2q}  \right] 
		\leq	(\lambda^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+\lambda^{\frac{2q}{2q-1}})\mathbb{E}_N\left[ |P_k(s_N)|^{2q}  \right] + \dfrac{q^{q}}{\lambda^{q}(N\alpha\eta^{2})^{q}}	\mathbb{E}_N\bigg[\big(|s_{N,k}(z)| + C_k \big)^{q}  \bigg] \\+\mathbb{E}_N\left[\dfrac{1}{2q\lambda}(|\Delta_k(z)|^{2q}+  \dfrac{2}{\alpha'^{2q}}   |\partial_zs_{N,k-1}(z) |^{2q} +O(N^{-2q}))\right].
	\end{multline*}
	Taking $\lambda=1/10$ allows to absorb the first in the RHS in the LHS.
	Using that $P_k(s_N) G  = |P_k(s_N)|^{2q}$ and $|s_{N,k}| \le C|P_{k}(s_N)|$, by Proposition~\ref{prop:Leq}, we conclude that  there exists constants $C>0$ such that:
		\begin{equation*}
	\mathbb{E}_N\left[ |P_k(s_N)|^{2q}  \right] 
	\leq C^{q}	\mathbb{E}_N\left[\dfrac{q^{q}}{(N\alpha\eta^{2})^{q}}\big(|s_{N,k}(z)| + C_k \big)^{q}+|\Delta_k(z)|^{2q}+  \dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2q}}   |\partial_zs_{N,k-1}(z)|^{2q} +N^{-2q} \right]
	\end{equation*}
                   
                                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                 
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                  
	This completes the proof.
<!-- end pro-17 -->
<!-- prop-14: body_md -->
\label{prop:BMP}
	There exists $C> 0$ such that for any $q\geq1$ and $z\in \Omega$ with $\alpha\eta \ge C$: 
	\[
	\|s_N(z) -m_{0}(z)\|_q \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{ q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}
	+  \frac{1}{\alpha\eta} .
	\]
<!-- end prop-14 -->
<!-- pro-18: body_md -->
Let $z\in\Omega$ and set $Z \coloneqq   |P_0(s_N)|^2$.
	We first apply Lemma~\ref{lem:Lineq} with $k=0$ $(s_{N,0}=s_N)$ using the trivial bound, to get:
	\[
	|\partial_z s_{N}| \le \frac1N \sum_{j=1}^{N} \frac{1}{|z-\lambda_j|^2} \le \eta^{-1}  \operatorname{Im} s_N . 
	\]
	Then, by re-centering and using the bounds $\| \xi_0 \|_{\mathcal{C}^1(\Omega)} \le C$ and  \eqref{stab}  (with $s_{N,0}=s_N-\xi_0$)with Proposition  \ref{prop:Leq}, we obtain:
	\[
	|  \partial_zs_{N}| \lesssim \eta^{-1}  \big (1+ |P_0(s_N)| \big) .
	\]
	By  Lemma~\ref{lem:Lineq} and Proposition \ref{prop:Leq}, this yields that there is a constant~$C>0$ such that for any $q\geq1$: 
	\begin{equation} \label{Leq0}
		\mathbb{E}_N\left[|P_0(s_N)|^{2q}\right]   \le \frac{C^{q} q^{q}}{(\alpha N\eta^2)^{q}} \mathbb{E}\left[|P_0(s_N)|^{q}+1 \right] 
		+ C^{q}\mathbb{E}_N\left[  |\Delta_0|^{2q}+  \frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{2q}}   + \frac{1}{N^{2q}} \right]. 
	\end{equation}
	Now using Corollary \ref{cor: glocon} and that $x\leq a\sqrt{x}+b$ implies $x\leq a^{2}+b$  when $x,a,b\geq0$, implies that:
		\begin{equation*} 
		\mathbb{E}_N\left[|P_0(s_N)|^{2q}\right]   \le \frac{C^{q} q^{2q}}{(\alpha N\eta^2)^{2q}} +\frac{C^{q} q^{q}}{(\alpha N\eta^2)^{q}}
		+ C^{q} \left(\dfrac{q^{q}}{(\sqrt{N\alpha})^{q}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{4q}}+  \frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{2q}}   + \frac{1}{N^{2q}}\right) . 
	\end{equation*}
	When $q\leq N\eta$, we obtain:
	\begin{align*}
		\|s_N(z)-\xi_0(z)\|_{2q}\lesssim	\|P_0(s_N)\|_{2q}&\lesssim  \sqrt{\frac{ q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}
	+  \frac{1}{\alpha\eta}.
	\end{align*}
	When $q\geq N\eta$, we use:
		\begin{align*}
		\|s_N(z)-\xi_0(z)\|_{2q}\lesssim	\|P_0(s_N)\|_{q}^{1/2}&\lesssim  \sqrt{\frac{ q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}
+  \frac{1}{\alpha\eta}.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end pro-18 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:LL1}
	Let $k\geq0$, for any $q\ge 1$ and $z\in \Omega$ with $\alpha\eta \ge C$, 
	\[
		\|s_{N}(z)-\xi_k(z) \|_q \lesssim  \sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2} } +\frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{k+1}} . 
	\]
	In particular, if $\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta$ for some $\delta>0$, then for $k\geq0$ large enough we obtain the sharp bounds for any $q\ge 1$:
	\[
	\|s_{N}(z)-\xi_k(z) \|_q \lesssim   \sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^{2}}} .
	\]
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- pro-19: body_md -->
Let $z\in\Omega$, t he starting point is again the inequality from Lemma~\ref{lem:Lineq}  for $k\ge 0$.
	Observe that to control the RHS, using the induction hypothesis (the bound holds for $k=0$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:BMP}), we can control (using Cauchy's formula) for any $q\geq1$: 
	\begin{equation}
		\begin{aligned} \label{sest}
			\|\partial_z s_{N,k}(z)\|_q &\le \eta^{-1} \sup_{w\in\overline{B}(z,\eta/2)}\| s_{N,k}(w)\|_{q} \\
			\alpha^{-1} \|\partial_z s_{N,k}(z)\|_q, &\lesssim \frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{k+1}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\frac{ q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}.
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
  
                                                                                                        
    
                                                                                                                     
                                                                                                                                                  
    
                                                                          
    
                                                                                                                                                   
    
  
By Lemma~\ref{lem:Lineq}  we have:
	\[
	\|  P_k(s_N)\|_{2q} \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}\big(\|s_{N,k}(z)\|_q^{1/2} + 1 \big) + \|\Delta_k\|_{2q}+  \frac{\|\partial_zs_{N,k}\|_{2q}}{\alpha} + \frac{1}{N} .
	\]
	We thus obtain, using again, $|s_{N,k}(z)|\lesssim |P_k(s_N)|$ by Proposition \ref{prop:Leq} and the fact that $x\leq a\sqrt{x}+b$ implies that $x\leq a^{2}+b$:
	\[
	\|  P_k(s_N)\|_{2q} \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}+\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2}+ \|\Delta_k\|_{2q}+  \frac{\|\partial_zs_{N,k}\|_{2q}}{\alpha} + \frac{1}{N} .
	\]
	The $\Delta_k$-term is controlled by Corollary \ref{cor: glocon} and the $\partial_zs_{N,k}$-term is controlled by \eqref{sest}.  Adjusting the constants, we obtain  that if $\alpha\eta \ge C$:
\begin{align*}
		\|  P_k(s_N)\|_{2q} &\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2} }+  \frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2} +\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}} +\frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{k+1}}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\frac{ q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}
+\dfrac{1}{N}
	\\&\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2} }+  \frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2}   +\frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{k+1}}
\end{align*}
	the terms $\frac{1}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\frac{ q}{\alpha N\eta^2}}$, $\sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}$ and $\frac{1}{N}$ being negligible.
	Now using for $q\leq N\eta$, $|s_{N,k}|\lesssim|P_k(s_N)|$, we obtain:
	\[
	\|  s_{N,k}(z)\|_{2q} \lesssim  \sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2} } +\frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{k+1}}.
	\]
	For $q\geq N\eta$, we use $|s_{N,k}|\lesssim|P_k(s_N)|^{1/2}$, to obtain:
		\[
	\|  s_{N,k}(z)\|_{2q} \lesssim 	\|  P_k(s_N)\|_{q}^{1/2}\lesssim \sqrt{\frac{q}{\alpha N\eta^2} } +\frac{1}{(\alpha\eta)^{k+1}}.
	\]
	Finally, replacing $ Z \coloneqq   |P_k(s_N)|^2$ and using the bound \eqref{stab}, this completes the proof.
<!-- end pro-19 -->
<!-- lem-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:gronwall square}
	Let $f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}([a,b])$ be a positive function such that there exists positive functions $1,\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([a,b])$ such that for all $x\in[a,b]$
	$$-f'(x)\leq\dfrac{1(x)}{f(x)}+\psi(x),$$
	then
	$$f(a)\leq\int_{a}^{b}\psi(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x+\left(f(b)^{2}+2\int_{a}^{b}1(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x\right)^{1/2}.$$
<!-- end lem-7 -->
<!-- pro-20: body_md -->
Let $g(x)\coloneqq\int_{x}^{b}\psi(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x+\left(f(b)^{2}+2\int_{x}^{b}1(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x\right)^{1/2}$, notice that:
	$$-g'(x)=\psi(x)+\dfrac{1(x)}{\left(f(b)^{2}+2\int_{x}^{b}1(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x\right)^{1/2}}\geq \psi(x)+\dfrac{1(x)}{g(x)}$$suppose there exists a maximal interval $[u,v]$ in $\{f>g\}$ with $f(v)=g(v)$, thus for all $x\in[u,v]$
	$$-f'(x)\leq\dfrac{1(x)}{f(x)}+\psi(x)< \dfrac{1(x)}{g(x)}+\psi(x)\leq-g'(x).$$
	Thus $g-f$ is non-increasing while equal to 0 at $v$, thus $g\geq f$ which is a contradiction. This proves the result.
<!-- end pro-20 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:LL2}Let $\delta>0$, assume that $\alpha \ge N^{\delta}$, then for all $k\geq0$ large enough (depending only on $\delta>0$) and $q\ge 1$, $z\in \Omega$ with $\eta  \le \alpha^{-1/2}$:
	\[
	\| s_N(z) - \xi_k(z) \|_q \lesssim  \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\eta}}+\dfrac{q}{N\eta}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}. 
	\]
	Thus for $k\geq0$ large enough, for any $q\geq1$:
		\[
	\| s_N(z) - \xi_k(z) \|_q \lesssim  \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\eta}}+\dfrac{q}{N\eta}. 
	\]
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- pro-21: body_md -->
Here $k\geq0$ is fixed such that  $ \frac{1}{\eta^2 N}  \gg \frac{1}{\alpha^k} $ and we set $G = q|s_{N,k}|^{2(q-1)} s_{N,k}^*$ in the loop equation from Proposition~\ref{prop:Leq}. 
	Like in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:Lineq}, $G$ is a complex polynomial in $s_N$, so using \eqref{Gbound}, we obtain:
	\begin{equation}\label{Gbound2}
		\begin{aligned}
			\left | {\displaystyle  \sum_{j=1}^{N} \dfrac{\partial_{\lambda_j}G(z)}{\lambda_j-z}} \right | 
			& \le 2 \eta^{-2} q^2 | s_N(z)|\cdot |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2(q-1)} \\
			& \le  2 \eta^{-2} q^2 C_k |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2(q-1)}  + 2 \eta^{-2} q^2 |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q-1}
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
	using that $|m_k(z)| \le C_k$ for $z\in\Omega$.
	
	Then, using that $\xi_{k-1}= \xi_{k} + O(\alpha^{-k})$ according to \eqref{mind}, taking the imaginary part on the RHS of \eqref{Leq} and using the factorization \eqref{Pfact} for $P_k$, one has for all $z\in\Omega$:
	\begin{equation} \label{Leq2}
		\operatorname{Im}\left\{\left( P_k(s_N) +   \dfrac{2}{\alpha'}  \partial_zs_{N,k}(z) \right)G(z)\right\} 
		= - \dfrac1{\alpha'}\partial_\eta [|s_{N,k}|^{2q}](z)  + q|s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q}  \operatorname{Im}(s_N(z)-\widetilde{\xi}_k(z))    
	\end{equation}
	where we used that 
	\[
	-\partial_\eta |s_{N,k}|^{2q}  = 2  \operatorname{Im}\big(s_{N,k}^{*q} \partial_z \overline{s_N}^q) \big)
	= 2  \operatorname{Im}\big(G  \overline{s_N}' \big) .
	\]
	The key observation is that,  by Proposition~\ref{prop:m},  $ \operatorname{Im}(s_N-\widetilde{\xi}_k) > 0$ for $z\in \Omega$ so that the second term on the RHS of \eqref{Leq2} is non-negative. 
	Thus, combining \eqref{Leq} with \eqref{Gbound2} and \eqref{Leq2}, there is a constant $C_k>0$ such that for any $q\geq1$ and $z\in\Omega$:
	\[
	\mathbb{E}_N\big[ -\partial_\eta |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q} \big]  \le \frac{\alpha'}{\alpha} C_k \mathbb{E}_N\left[ \frac{q^2}{ N\eta^2} |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q-2}+\bigg( \frac{\alpha |\Delta_k(z)|}2 + \frac{q^2}{\eta^2 N} + \frac{ \alpha}{N}  + \frac{1}{\alpha^k} \bigg)   |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q-1} \right]. 
	\]
	At this stage, we observe that $\alpha' \le 2\alpha$ and using the conditions  $ \frac{1}{\eta^2 N}  \gg \frac{1}{\alpha^k} $ and $\eta \le \alpha^{-1/2}$, the last two terms can be neglected.
	Hence, there is a constant $C>0$ such that for any $q\geq1$ and $z\in\Omega$ and $\eta\leq\alpha^{-1/2}$:
	\begin{equation} \label{Leq4}
		\mathbb{E}_N\big[ -\partial_\eta |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q} \big]  \le \frac{C q^2}{ N\eta^2}\mathbb{E}_N \left[|s_{N,k}(z)|^{2(q-1)} \right] 
		+  C\mathbb{E}_N\left[\bigg(  \alpha |\Delta_k(z)| + \frac{ q^2}{\eta^2 N} \bigg)   |s_{N,k}(z)|^{2q-1} \right]  . 
	\end{equation}
	\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Case $q\leq N\eta$.\endgroup Now, let   $u_q : \eta \in\mathbb{R}_+ \mapsto   \|s_{N,k}(z)^2\|_{q}  $ for $q\ge 0$.
	By H\"older's inequality, we have the following bound:
	\[
	\mathbb{E}_N\left[   |\Delta_k| \cdot |s_{N,k}|^{2q-1}  \right] \le \| \Delta_k \|_{2q} \cdot\|s_{N,k} \|_{2q}^{{2q-1}} \lesssim\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}  u_{q}^{q-\frac12} 
	\]
	where we used the bound Corollary \ref{cor: glocon} at last (the term proportional to $\tfrac{1}{\alpha^{k+1}}$ is again negligible).
	
	Observe that $q\mapsto u_q$ is non-decreasing, so we deduce from \eqref{Leq4} that:
	\begin{equation} \label{Leq5}
		- \partial_\eta(u_q^{q})(\eta) = \mathbb{E}_N\big[ -\partial_\eta |s_{N,k}|^{2q}(z) \big]  
		\le \frac{C q^2}{N\eta^2}   u_q^{q-1}(\eta) +  C\bigg( \sqrt{\dfrac{q\alpha}{N}}   \frac{ q^2}{\eta^2 N} \bigg) u_q^{q-\frac12}(\eta) . 
	\end{equation}
	
	This inequality is almost suitable to apply Gr\"onwall's estimate. 
	Multiplying \eqref{Leq5} by $\tfrac1{q}u_{q}^{1-q}$, we obtain  for any $\theta\ge 1$:
	\begin{equation} \label{Leq6}
		\begin{aligned}
			-  \partial_\eta(u_q) (\eta)
			&\le   \frac{C q}{N\eta^2} +  C\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N /\alpha}}+ \frac{q}{\eta^2 N} \bigg)\sqrt{u_q(\eta)}  \\
			&\le   \frac{C q}{ N\eta^2} + \frac{C}{2\theta\sqrt{N /\alpha}}  + C\theta   \bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N /\alpha}}+ \frac{q}{\eta^2 N} \bigg)u_q (\eta)
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
	by Young's inequality ($2\sqrt{u} \le \theta^{-1}+ \theta u$ for $u\ge 0$). 
	
	
	Then, integrating the inequality \eqref{Leq6} between $[\eta, \epsilon]$ with $\eta\le \epsilon$ and $\epsilon \le \alpha^{-1/2}$,  we can apply Gr\"onwall Lemma.
	Choosing $\theta^{-1} = \frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{N /\alpha}}$, we obtain if $N \eta \ge q$:
	\[
	\theta\int_\eta^{\epsilon} \bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N /\alpha}}+ \frac{Cq}{t^2 N} \bigg) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \le C +\frac{Cq}{N\eta} \le C. 
	\]	Similarly, with $\epsilon = \alpha^{-1/2}$ ($\theta=\sqrt{N}$), we also have:
\begin{align*}
		\int_\eta^{\epsilon}   \bigg(\frac{q}{t^2 N}+\frac{1}{\theta\sqrt{N /\alpha}}\bigg) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t
	\le  \frac{C q}{N\eta}  +  \dfrac{\epsilon}{\theta\sqrt{N/\alpha}} \lesssim\frac{C q}{N\eta}.
\end{align*}
                                                                                         
    
                                                                                                                    
                                                                                                    
    
	Applying Gr\"onwall's lemma to \eqref{Leq6}, we conclude that for $\eta \le \epsilon = \alpha^{-\frac12}$:
	\[
	u_q(\eta) \le u_q(\epsilon) + C\frac{ q}{N\eta} .
	\]
	Finally, by Theorem~\ref{prop:LL1} ($\alpha\epsilon = \epsilon^{-1}\ge N^{\delta/2}$ so that the first term dominates), 
	$\sqrt{u_q(\epsilon)} \le \sqrt{\frac{C q}{N}}$ so that for any $q\geq1$ and $\eta \in \left [\dfrac qN, \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\right ]$:
	\[
	\| s_N(z) - \xi_k(z) \|_{2q}=\sqrt{u_q(\eta)}\lesssim   \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\eta}}. 
	\]
	\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Case $q\geq N\eta$.\endgroup We start with \eqref{Leq4} and set $v_q(\eta)=\sqrt{u_q(\eta)}=\| s_{N,k}(z)\|_{2q}$, we thus obtain:
	$$- \partial_\eta(v_q^{2q})(\eta) =-2q \partial_\eta v_q(\eta)v_q^{2q-1}(\eta)
	\le \frac{C q^2}{N\eta^2}   v_q^{2q-2}(\eta) +  C\bigg( \sqrt{\dfrac{q\alpha}{N}} +    \frac{ q^2}{\eta^2 N} \bigg) v_q^{2q-1}(\eta).$$
	Dividing by $2qv_q^{2q-1}$ (because $|s_{N,k}(z)|$ is not equal to 0 almost surely), we obtain:
		$$ -\partial_\eta v_q(\eta)
	\le \frac{C q}{N\eta^2}   \dfrac{1}{v_q(\eta)} +  C\bigg( \sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}} +    \frac{ q}{\eta^2 N} \bigg).$$
	Thus by Lemma \ref{lem:gronwall square} and Theorem \ref{prop:LL1}, we obtain for $\eta_0=C/\alpha$:
	$$v_q(\eta)\leq C\int_{\eta}^{\eta_0}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}} +    \frac{ q}{t^2 N}\right) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t+\left(v_q(\eta_0)^{2}+2\int_{\eta}^{\eta_0}\frac{C q}{Nt^2}  \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{q}{N\eta}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\eta}}\lesssim\dfrac{q}{N\eta}.$$
	Finally, we use Proposition \ref{prop:m} to extend the result to any $k\geq0$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end pro-21 -->
<!-- rem-6: body_md -->
Combining Theorems \ref{prop:LL1} and \ref{prop:LL2} with Proposition \ref{prop:approx zeta m} gives Theorem \ref{thm:local law}.
<!-- end rem-6 -->
<!-- prop-15: body_md -->
\label{prop:Helffer–Sjöstrand}

                                                                                 
	Let $r \in \mathbb{N}$ and $1 \in \mathcal{C}_c^{r+1}(\mathbb{R})$. Let $\delta>0$. We define the pseudo-analytic extension of $1$ of degree $r$ by
	\[
	\Phi(z)=\Phi (x+\mathrm{i} y) 
	\coloneqq  \chi_\delta(y)\sum_{j=0}^r \frac{1}{j!} (\mathrm{i} y)^j 1^{(j)}(x),
	\]
	where $\chi_\delta=\chi(\delta^{-1}\cdot)$ and $\chi\in \mathcal{C}_c^\infty(\mathbb{R})$ is even and such that $\mathbf{1}_{[-1,1]}\leq\chi_\delta\leq \mathbf{1}_{[-2,2]} $.	Then, for all $\lambda \in \mathbb{R}$, and $r\geq1$:
	\[
	1(\lambda) = \operatorname{Re}\left(\int_{\mathbb{C}_+}\frac{\overline{\partial}\Phi(z)}{\lambda-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{\pi}\right).
	\]
<!-- end prop-15 -->
<!-- lem-8: body_md -->
\label{eq:HS_L_N(f)}
	Let $k\geq0$, $1 \in \mathcal{C}_c^3(\mathbb{R})$ supported on $[-1+\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon]$ and let $\Omega$ denote the neighborhood in Lemma \ref{lem:eq}. Let $\delta>0$ be arbitrary, and denote the Stieltjes transform of $\bm{\mu}_N-\mu_k$ by 
	\begin{equation*}
	\widetilde{s_N}(z)\coloneqq s_N(z)-m_k(z).
	\end{equation*}
	Then, with $z=x+\mathrm{i} y$ we have
	\begin{equation*} 
L_N^{(k)}(1)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}\bigg(1''(x)y\chi_\delta(y)+1(x)\Big(2(\chi_\delta)'(y)+y(\chi_\delta)''(y)\Big)\bigg) \operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end lem-8 -->
<!-- pro-22: body_md -->
We first apply Proposition \ref{prop:Helffer–Sjöstrand} with a degree $2$ pseudo-analytic extension and expand to obtain:
	\begin{align*}
	L_N^{(k)}(1)&=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1''(x)y\chi_\delta(y)\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}(\chi_\delta)'(y)1(x)\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \\&\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1'(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}y\chi_\delta(y)1^{(3)}(x)\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \\&\quad+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}(y\chi_\delta(y))'1''(x)\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z.
	\end{align*}
	The last two terms in fact cancel. Integrating by parts in $x$ and using Cauchy-Riemann,
	\begin{align*}
	-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}y\chi_\delta(y)1^{(3)}(x)\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+} y\chi_\delta(y)1''(x)\partial_x \operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \\
	&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+} y\chi_\delta(y)1''(x)\partial_y \operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z
	\end{align*}
	using the compact support of $1$ to guarantee the boundary term vanishes. Integrating by parts in $y$ now using that $y\chi_\delta(y)\equiv 0$ on the real axis, we find
	\begin{equation*}
	-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}y\chi_\delta(y)1^{(3)}(x)\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_+} (y\chi_\delta(y))'1''(x)\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z.
	\end{equation*}
	This leaves us with 
	\begin{multline}\label{eq: firstfluctexp}
	L_N^{(k)}(1)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1''(x)y\chi_\delta(y)\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}(\chi_\delta)'(y)1(x)\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \\-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1'(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z.
	\end{multline}
	
	We can also remove the $1'$ in the last term for easier estimation later on. Integrating by parts in $x$, using that $1$ is compactly supported 
	\begin{equation*}
	-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1'(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\partial_x(\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z
	\end{equation*}
	and using Cauchy-Riemann we may write 
	\begin{equation*}
	\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\partial_x(\operatorname{Re} \widetilde{s_N}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\partial_y(\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z.
	\end{equation*}
	Integrating by parts in $y$ using that $(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)$ vanishes on the real axis, we obtain 
	\begin{equation*}
	\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1(x)(\chi_\delta)'(y)y\partial_y(\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_+}1(x)\left((\chi_\delta)''(y)y+(\chi_\delta)'(y)\right)\operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z.
	\end{equation*}
	Substituting into \eqref{eq: firstfluctexp} and simplifying yields the result.
<!-- end pro-22 -->
<!-- prop-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:HS_L_N}
	Let $\varepsilon>0$, and suppose $B \subset [-1+\varepsilon, 1-\varepsilon]$. Suppose $\alpha \geq N^\delta$,  for some $\delta\in (0,1]$, $q=q(N)\geq1$. Define for all $1\in \mathcal{C}_c^3(B)$ and all $\eta\in(0,\eta_0]$, a constant $\mathtt{c}(1)>0$ such that
	for all $m\in\llbracket0,3\rrbracket$:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq: Cnorm}
		\|1^{(m)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\leq \mathtt{c}(1)\eta^{1-m}.
	\end{equation}Then for $k\geq1$ large enough (depending only on $\delta$) and all $1\in \mathcal{C}_c^3(B)$,
we have: 
	\begin{equation*}
		\|L_N^{(k)}(1)\|_{q}
\lesssim	\mathtt{c}(1)\begin{cases}
\dfrac{qN^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}  & \text{if }\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta, \\
\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}&\text{otherwise.}
\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end prop-16 -->
<!-- pro-23: body_md -->
We first choose the cutoff $\chi_\delta$ with $\delta=\eta$. We then write $L_N^{(k)}(1)$ using Lemma \ref{eq:HS_L_N(f)}; using the triangle inequality along with $y|(\chi_\eta)''(y)|\leq -(\chi_\eta)'(y)=\eta^{-1}|\chi'(\eta^{-1}y)|$, we obtain:
\begin{align*}\label{eq: secondfluctexp}
\left\|L_N^{(k)}(1)\right\|_q &\lesssim  \|1''\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \underbrace{\int_0^{2\eta} \hspace{-.2cm}  y\chi_\eta( y) \sup_{E\in B} \| \operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(E+\mathrm{i} y)\|_q   \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y}_{\coloneqq \mathcal{I}_1}\nonumber
\\&\quad+O\bigg(
\|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}  \underbrace{\sup_{\substack{E\in B\\y\in [\eta,2\eta]}} \| \operatorname{Im} \widetilde{s_N}(z) \|_q}_{\coloneqq \mathcal{I}_2}  \bigg) 
\end{align*}
We consider the Random matrix and Poisson regimes separately. 

\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Case $ \eta \geq \tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$.\endgroup
First, suppose $ \eta \geq \tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$. Then, applying Theorems \ref{prop:LL1} and \ref{prop:LL2}, we obtain with $k$ sufficiently large:
                  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                
                
\begin{align*}
	\mathcal{I}_1 \lesssim \int_0^{\frac{N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{\alpha}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{Ny}}+\frac{q}{Ny}\right)y\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y+\int_{\frac{N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{\alpha}}^{2\eta} \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y \lesssim\sqrt{\frac{qN^{2\delta}}{\alpha^{3} N}}+\frac{qN^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha}+\sqrt{\frac{q\eta^{2}}{N\alpha}}\lesssim \frac{qN^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha}+\sqrt{\frac{q\eta^{2}}{N\alpha}}
\end{align*}
where we neglegted the term $\sqrt{\frac{qN^{2\delta}}{\alpha^{3} N}}$ because $\alpha \eta \geq N^{\delta}$.
Thus, we have the bound
\begin{equation*}
 \|1''\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\mathcal{I}_1 \lesssim  \mathtt{c}(1)\eta^{-1}\left(\frac{qN^{\delta}}{ N\alpha}+\sqrt{\frac{q\eta^{2}}{N\alpha}}\right)= \mathtt{c}(1)\left(\frac{qN^{\delta}}{ N\alpha\eta}+\sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}\right).
\end{equation*}We also have 
\begin{equation*}
\|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\mathcal{I}_2 \lesssim\mathtt{c}(1)\eta \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}
\end{equation*}
from Theorem \ref{prop:LL1}; we thus obtain:
\begin{equation*}
\left\|L_N^{(k)}(1)\right\|_q \lesssim\mathtt{c}(1)\left(\frac{qN^{\delta}}{ N\alpha\eta}+\sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}\right).
\end{equation*}
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Case $ \eta \leq \tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$.\endgroup If $\eta \leq\tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$, the cutoff is equal to $1$ on $[0,\eta]$ and then decays outside. Using Theorem \ref{prop:LL2} yields 
\begin{equation*}
 \|1''\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} 	\mathcal{I}_1 \lesssim \mathtt{c}(1)\eta^{-1} \int_0^{2\eta}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{qy}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y 
	\lesssim\mathtt{c}(1)\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right) 
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
\|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\mathcal{I}_2 \lesssim \eta\mathtt{c}(1)\left(\sqrt{\frac{q\eta}{N}}+\frac{q\eta}{N}\right) .
\end{equation*}
Again using \eqref{eq: Cnorm}, we obtain 
\begin{equation*}
\left\|L_N^{(k)}(1)\right\|_q \lesssim \mathtt{c}(1)\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right) ,
\end{equation*}
as desired.
<!-- end pro-23 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{lem: genHS_L_N}
Under the same assumptions as in Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N}, let $m:\Omega_+\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ satisfying $\|m\|_{\mathcal{C}^0(\Omega_+)}\lesssim 1$. We have:
                                   
                                                                 
                

	\begin{equation*}
		\left\|\operatorname{Re}\left(\int_{\Omega_+}\overline{\partial}\Phi(z)m(z)\widetilde{s_N}(z)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{\pi}\right)\right\|_{q}
\lesssim\mathtt{c}(1)\begin{cases}
	\dfrac{qN^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta}+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}  & \text{if }\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta, \\
	\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}&\text{otherwise.}
\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- pro-24: body_md -->
The proof is analogous to that of Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N}.
<!-- end pro-24 -->
<!-- lem-9: body_md -->
\label{eq:HS_A_N(f)}
	Suppose $k\geq0$, and $B \subset [-1+\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon]$. Let $\Omega_+$ be as in Lemma \ref{lem:eq}. Let $1\in \mathcal{C}_c^3(B)$ and let $\Phi$ denote the degree $2$ pseudo-analytic extension. Then, we have
\[
A_N^{(k)}(1)   =  -\operatorname{Re}\bigg(\int_{\Omega_+}  \overline{\partial}\Phi(z) \, \widetilde{s_N}(z)^2  \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{2\pi}  \bigg) .
\]
<!-- end lem-9 -->
<!-- prop-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:HS_A_N}
Under the same assumptions as in Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N}, we have:
                                     
                                                                                                                                                                    
                
      
		\begin{equation} \label{eq:HS_bound_A_N RMT}
			\|A_N^{(k)}(1)\|_q\lesssim\mathtt{c}(1)\begin{cases}
				\dfrac{q}{N\alpha \eta}+\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac\delta2}}{N^{2}\alpha\eta^{2}}  & \text{if }\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta, \\
				\dfrac{q}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}&\text{otherwise.}
			\end{cases} 
		\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-17 -->
<!-- pro-25: body_md -->
Let $\Phi$ be a degree 2 pseudo-analytic extension in Proposition \ref{prop:Helffer–Sjöstrand} with cutoff at scale $\delta=\eta$. Via a direct computation, we see that
\begin{equation*}
|\overline{\partial}\Phi(x+\mathrm{i} y)| \lesssim y^2\chi_\eta(y)|1^{(3)}(x)|+\sum_{k=0}^2 |(\chi_\eta)'(y)|y^k|1^{(k)}(x)|.
\end{equation*}
From this and Lemma \ref{eq:HS_A_N(f)} we obtain:
\begin{equation*}
\|A_N^{(k)}(1)\|_{q}\lesssim \int_{\Omega_+}\left(\chi_\eta(y)y^2|1^{3}(x)|+|(\chi_\eta)'(y)|\sum_{k=0}^2y^k|1^{(k)}(x)|\right) \cdot\|\widetilde{s_N}(x+\mathrm{i} y)\|_{q}^2\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y
\end{equation*}
with $\Omega_+$ as in Lemma \ref{lem:eq}. 

                                                                                            
   
                                                               
                                                                                           
                                                                                                                        
              
   
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Case $ \eta \geq \tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$.\endgroup Let $ \eta \geq \tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$, we make use of Theorem \ref{prop:LL2} up to scale $\sqrt{\frac{\eta}{\alpha}}$ and Theorem \ref{prop:LL1} above. Then,
               
                                                                                                                                                                                                           
                                                                                                           
                                                                                                                              
                                         
              
  
 \begin{align*}
 	\|A_N^{(k)}(1)\|_{q}&\lesssim \|1^{(3)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\left( \int_0^{\frac{N^{\delta
 			/2}}{\alpha}}y^{2}\left(\dfrac{q}{Ny}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}y^{2}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y  +\int_{\frac{N^{\delta/2}}{\alpha}}^{2\eta}y^2\frac{q}{N\alpha y^{2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y\right)
 	\\&\quad+\sum_{k=0}^2\|1^{(k)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\int_{\eta}^{2\eta} y^k|(\chi_\eta)'(y)|\frac{q}{N\alpha y^2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y \\
 	&\lesssim  \mathtt{c}(1)\eta ^{-2}\left(\frac{qN^{\delta}}{N\alpha^{2}}+\frac{q^{2}N^{\delta/2}}{N^{2}\alpha}+\dfrac{q\eta}{N\alpha}\right)+\sum_{k=0}^2\mathtt{c}(1)\eta^{1-k}\frac{q}{N\alpha } \eta^{k-2}\\
 	&\lesssim \mathtt{c}(1)\left(\frac{q}{N\alpha \eta}+\frac{q^{2}N^{\delta/2}}{N^{2}\alpha\eta^{2}}\right),
 \end{align*}
where we neglegted the term $\tfrac{qN^{\delta}}{N\alpha^{2}\eta^{2}}$ because $\eta\geq\tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$.

\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. $ \eta \leq \tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$.\endgroup Let $\eta\leq\tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$, we have:
\begin{align*}
\|A_N^{(k)}(1)\|_{q}&\lesssim \|1^{(3)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \int_0^{2\eta}y^2\left(\dfrac{q}{Ny}+\frac{q^2}{N^2y^2}\right) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y 
\\&\quad+\sum_{k=0}^2\|1^{(k)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\int_{\eta}^{2\eta} |(\chi_\eta)'(y)|y^k\left(\frac{q}{Ny}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}y^{2}}\right) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y \\
&\lesssim \mathtt{c}(1)\eta ^{-2}\left(\frac{q\eta^{2}}{N}+\frac{q^{2}\eta}{N^{2}}\right)+\sum_{k=0}^2 \mathtt{c}(1)\eta^{1-k}\left(\dfrac{q\eta^{k-1}}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}\eta^{k-2}}{N^{2}}\right)
\\&\lesssim  \mathtt{c}(1)\left(\frac{q}{N}+\frac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}\right).
\end{align*}
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-25 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:meso sec5}
	Let $\beta_N\gg N^{-1+\kappa}$ for some $\kappa>0$. For all $E\in(-1+\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon)$ for some fixed $\varepsilon>0$ and all $q\geq1$, with $f_\eta\coloneqq f\left(\eta^{-1}(\cdot-E)\right)$, the following holds:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}\textit{(Random Matrix regime)}\endgroup if $\eta\gg\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$ then for all $f\in\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$:
		\begin{equation*}
			\mathbf{W}_q\left (\sqrt{N\alpha}L_N^{(k)}(f_\eta), \mathcal{N}\left(0,\|f\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^{2}\right)\right ) \lesssim \begin{cases}
			\mathfrak{R}_1 & \text{if } \eta \geq \tfrac{N^\delta}{\alpha}, \\\mathfrak{R}_2
				&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
		\end{equation*}
		where $$	\mathfrak{R}_1 \coloneqq\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{(\alpha\eta)^{2}}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta^{2}}+	\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}\eta}\log(\eta^{-1})+\sqrt{q}\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\eta\right )+\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}$$
		and
		$$	\mathfrak{R}_2 \coloneqq\dfrac{q^{2}}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+	\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+q\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha\eta}}+\eta\right)+\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}.$$
		\item \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}(\textit{Poisson regime})\endgroup if $\tfrac{1}{N}\ll\eta\ll\theta\ll\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$ then for all $f\in\mathcal{C}_c^{3}(\mathbb{R})$:
		$$		\mathbf{W}_q\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(f_\eta), \mathcal{N}(0, \|f\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}^2)\right) \lesssim \mathfrak{R}_3$$
		where 
		$$\mathfrak{R}_3\coloneqq q^{2}\dfrac{\alpha\theta}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta}{N}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}\left(\alpha\theta+\dfrac{\eta}{\theta}\right)+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}+\alpha\theta\right)+\alpha\theta\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}.$$
		\item \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}(\textit{Critical regime})\endgroup If $\alpha\eta\mu_\mathrm{eq}(E)\tau= 1$ for some $\tau>0$ then  for all $f\in\mathcal{C}_c^{3}(\mathbb{R})$:
		$$\mathbf{W}_q\left (\sqrt{\tfrac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(f_\eta), \mathcal{N}(0, \Sigma_\tau^2(f))\right )\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_4$$
		where 
		$$\mathfrak{R}_4\coloneqq\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{N\eta}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{\eta}{N}}.$$
	\end{enumerate}
	The semi-norm $\|\cdot\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}$ is given in \eqref{def: H12 norm} and
	\begin{equation*}
		\Sigma_\tau^2(1):=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{|\xi|\cdot|\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- prop-18: body_md -->
\label{prop: SteinRMT}
	Let $\varepsilon>0$ be fixed and suppose $B \coloneqq(-1+\varepsilon, 1-\varepsilon)$ and let $\psi \in \mathcal{C}_c^3(B)$ satisfy \eqref{eq: Cnorm} with $\eta>0$. Let $f$ be such that $f'=\psi$, and write $F= N\bm{\mu}_N(f_\eta)$. Then for $k$ large enough:
		\begin{equation}\label{Stein1} 
\frac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N\alpha}   =   L_N^{(k)}(\Xi[\psi])
+  \boldsymbol{\zeta}(\psi)  +O\bigg(\frac{\eta}N\mathtt{c}(\psi) \bigg)
\end{equation}
where the error term is deterministic and the random part is given by:
\begin{equation}\label{def:zetaRM}
	\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\psi)   \coloneqq   -\bigg(\frac1\alpha-\frac1{2N}\bigg)L_N^{(k)}(\psi') +\sum_{1\le \ell\le k} \operatorname{Re}\left(\alpha^{-\ell}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_\ell(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}\right)  +A_N^{(k)}(\psi) .
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-18 -->
<!-- pro-26: body_md -->
We expand the terms in \eqref{eq:Stein0}. Using a degree $2$ pseudo-analytic extension $\Psi$ of $\psi$ and applying Proposition \ref{prop:Helffer–Sjöstrand} we find:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq: psiexp}
	-\int_\mathbb{R} \psi'(x) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(x) =  \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \partial s_N(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}  = -L_N^{(k)}(\psi') + \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)  \partial m_k(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{\pi} 
\end{equation}
where $m_k$ is the Stieltjes transform of $\mu_k$ defined in Definition \eqref{def:mup}. Similarly, we may expand  
\begin{align*}\label{eq: Vpsiexp}
\nonumber \int_\mathbb{R} V'(x) \psi(x) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \bm{\mu}_N( x) &=  L_N^{(k)}(V'\psi)  +\mu_k\left(    \bigg( \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \frac{\overline{\partial}\Psi(z)}{\cdot-z} \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}   \bigg)V'  \right) \\
 &=                                                                
L_N^{(k)}(V'\psi)  +\operatorname{Re} \int _{\Omega_+}\overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \mu_k\left(\frac{V'}{\cdot-z}\right) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} 
\end{align*}
and with $\mathcal{D}$ defined in Definition \ref{def:ope D}:
\begin{align}\label{eq: aniexp}
-\frac{1}2 \iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}} \mathcal{D}[\psi](x,y) &\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N( x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N( y) \nonumber  =\frac12 \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) s_N^2(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}\nonumber
\\&=  \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) m_k(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}  + \frac12 \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+}\overline{\partial}\Psi(z) m_k^2(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} + A_N^{(k)}(\psi).
\end{align}
using the formula
\begin{equation*}
	\mathcal{D}[g](\lambda,\lambda')=\frac{g(\lambda)-g(\lambda')}{\lambda-\lambda'} 
	=   -\operatorname{Re}\iint_{\Omega_+} \frac{\overline{\partial}G (z)}{(\lambda - z)(\lambda' - z)} \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{\pi}
	\end{equation*}
	for general $g$ with pseudo-analytic extension $G$, Lemma \ref{eq:HS_A_N(f)}. Using this formula again, the first term may be written explicitly, using Definition \ref{def:ope D} as:
\begin{align*}
 \operatorname{Re} \int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) m_k(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} 
 &=-L_N^{(k)}\left( \int_{-1}^{1} \mathcal{D}[\psi](\cdot,u) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}( u)\right)
\\&\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}  \alpha^{-\ell}\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_{\ell}(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} \\
&= -\frac12 L_N^{(k)}(\psi V') + L_N^{(k)}( \Xi[\psi])
+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k} \alpha^{-\ell}\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_{\ell}(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}
\end{align*}
Inserting these into \eqref{eq:Stein0}, we obtain:
                                                    
                                                                      
                  
                                                          
                                                                                                                         
                                     
                
                                                                                                               

\begin{align*}
\frac{\mathcal{L}[ F]}{N\alpha}   & =L_N^{(k)}(\Xi [\psi])+\bigg( -\bigg(\frac1\alpha-\frac1{2N}\bigg)L_N^{(k)}(\psi')  +\sum_{\ell=1}^{k} \alpha^{-\ell}\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_\ell(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}  + A_N^{(k)}(\psi) \bigg) \\
&\quad+\frac12  \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\left( \frac2\alpha\partial m_k(z)+m_k^2(z)+\mu_k\bigg( \frac{V'}{\cdot-z}\bigg)\right)  \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} +O\bigg(\frac{\eta}N \mathtt{c}(\psi)  \bigg)
\end{align*}
where the $O$ error term is deterministic and controlled by
\begin{equation*}
\frac{1}{2N} \bigg| \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \partial m_k(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} \bigg| 
\lesssim  \frac1{N} \int_{\Omega_+} \big|\overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \big| \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \lesssim  \frac1{2N} \sum_{0\le \ell\le 3 } \eta^{\ell} \|\psi^{(\ell)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} 
\lesssim \frac{\mathtt{c}(\psi) \eta}{2N} .
\end{equation*}
By coupling Propositions \ref{prop:mun stieltjes}, the second line is $\mathtt{c}(\psi)\eta O\big(\frac{ 1}{\alpha^{k+1}}+\frac{1}N \big)$ which is negligible if $\alpha^k \ge N$.
<!-- end pro-26 -->
<!-- lem-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:hilbert}
	Let $1\in\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$ then: $$\mathcal{H}[1](x)\underset{|x|\to\infty}{=}-\dfrac{1}{\pi x}\int_\mathbb{R} 1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t+o\left(\dfrac{1}{x}\right) ,\quad\quad\quad\mathcal{H}[1'](x)\underset{|x|\to\infty}{=}O\left(\dfrac{1}{x^{2}}\right)$$
	and $\mathcal{H}[1]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{4}(\mathbb{R})$.
<!-- end lem-10 -->
<!-- pro-27: body_md -->
We start with the fact that $1\in\mathcal{W}^{5,2}(\mathbb{R})$, thus $\mathcal{H}\left[1\right]\in\mathcal{W}^{5,2}(\mathbb{R})\subset\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})$ and for all $k\in\llbracket0,5\rrbracket$, $\mathcal{H}[1]^{(k)}=\mathcal{H}[1^{(k)}]$ (see \cite{Hilberttransforms}). Thus, by \cite[Lemma 2.3]{dworaczek2024clt}, the fact that $1\in L^1(\mathbb{R})$ and setting $g:t\mapsto t1(t)$, we have for all $x\in\mathbb{R}$:
	$$x\mathcal{H}[1](x)+\dfrac{1}{\pi}\int_\mathbb{R}1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t=\mathcal{H}[g](x)$$
	Since $g\in\mathcal{W}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})$ so does $\mathcal{H}[g]$ and since functions in $\mathcal{W}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})$ go to zero at infinity, we conclude that $\mathcal{H}[g](x)=o(1)$ and that $\mathcal{H}[1](x)=\tfrac{-1}{\pi x}\int_\mathbb{R}1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t+o(x^{-1})$ at infinity. For the second point, setting $g:t\mapsto t1'(t)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})$ and $h:t\mapsto t^{2}1'(t)$, we have since for all $x\in\mathbb{R}$ :
	$$x^{2}\mathcal{H}[1](x)+\dfrac{x}{\pi}\int_\mathbb{R}1'(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t+\dfrac{1}{\pi}\int_\mathbb{R} t1'(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t=x\mathcal{H}[g](x)+\dfrac{1}{\pi}\int_\mathbb{R} t1'(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t=\mathcal{H}[h](x).$$
	Now since $\int_\mathbb{R}1'(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t=0$ and $h\in \mathcal{W}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})$, we deduce the second point.
<!-- end pro-27 -->
<!-- prop-19: body_md -->
\label{prop: RMT error control}
	Let $1\in\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$ and $1_\eta \coloneqq 1\big(\frac{\cdot-E}{\eta}\big)$ where $E \in B$ and $\eta>0$ satisfies $1\gg\eta \gg\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$. Let $\chi(x)\in \mathcal{C}_c^\infty(B)$ satisfy \eqref{eq: Cnorm} with $\eta=1$ such that $\chi =1$ on a neighborhood of $E$. Define 
	\begin{equation*}\label{eq:invert MO}
		\psi(x):=\frac{\chi(x)\mathcal{H}[1_\eta](x)}{\pi \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)}, \qquad x\in\mathbb{R}.
	\end{equation*} 
	Then $ \mathtt{c}(\psi) \lesssim \log(\eta^{-1})$ for $k\geq1$ large enough and all $q\geq1$, with $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ is as in Proposition \ref{prop: SteinRMT}                  
	one has:
	\begin{equation*} \label{err2}
		\sqrt{N\alpha} \cdot \|\boldsymbol{\zeta} \|_q \lesssim  \begin{cases}
			\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{(\alpha\eta)^{2}}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+ \dfrac{q}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha\eta}  &\quad\quad\quad \text{if }\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta, \\
			\dfrac{q^{2}}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+q\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{\alpha\eta}}&\quad\quad\quad\text{otherwise},
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	and for $\boldsymbol\xi \coloneqq  L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta-\Xi[\psi])$, one has $\sqrt{N\alpha} \cdot \|\boldsymbol{\xi} \|_q \lesssim \eta\sqrt{q}$.
<!-- end prop-19 -->
<!-- pro-28: body_md -->
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Estimation of $\bm\zeta$.\endgroup We first notice that $\mathcal{\psi}\in\mathcal{C}^{4}_c(B)$ because of Lemma \ref{lem:hilbert} and use that $|\mathcal{H}[1_\eta](x)| \lesssim \frac{C(1)}{1+|x-E|/\eta}$ for all $x\in\mathbb{R}$ where $C(1)>0$ is a bounded constant depending only on $1$, which yields:
	\begin{equation*}
		\|\psi\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \int_\mathbb{R} \chi(x)\cdot |\mathcal{H}[1_\eta](x)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x \lesssim \eta \log(\eta^{-1}) .
	\end{equation*}
	Let $\varrho:x\mapsto  \dfrac{\chi(x)}{\pi \mu_\mathrm{eq}(x)} \in \mathcal{C}^\infty_c(B)$ for notational ease. Then, $\psi'= \varrho'  \cdot \mathcal{H}[1_\eta] + \varrho \cdot \mathcal{H}[1_\eta]'.$
	A computation reveals that $\mathcal{H}[1_\eta]'=\eta^{-1}\mathcal{H}[1']_\eta$. Since $\mathcal{H}[1']  \in L^1(\mathbb{R})$ by Lemma \ref{lem:hilbert} then, $\displaystyle \int_\mathbb{R} \mathcal{H}[1']_\eta (x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x \lesssim \eta.$ Thus,
	\[
	\|\psi'\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \eta \int_\mathbb{R}  |\varrho'(x\eta+E)|\cdot| \mathcal{H}[1](x)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x +  O(1)\lesssim 1.
	\]
	We can show similarly that for all $k\in\llbracket1,4\rrbracket$:
	\[
	\|\psi^{(k)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \eta^{1-k}.
	\]
	Consequently, we have $ \mathtt{c}(\psi) \lesssim \log(\eta^{-1})$ and $ \mathtt{c}(\psi') \lesssim \eta^{-1}$. So, we may make use of the estimates given in Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N}, Corollary \ref{lem: genHS_L_N} and Proposition \ref{lem:HS_A_N} to deduce with the constant $C>0$ in Theorem \ref{prop:LL1} and any $q \geq1$:
	\[
	\|L_N^{(k)}(\psi')\|_q\lesssim \left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha\eta^{2}}}+\dfrac{qN^{\frac{2}{3}\delta}}{N\alpha\eta^{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\alpha\eta\geq N^{\delta}}+\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\eta}}+\dfrac{q}{N\eta}\right)\mathbf{1}_{C\leq\alpha\eta\leq N^{\delta}},
	\]
	$$\|A_N^{(k)}(\psi)\|_q \lesssim \log(\eta^{-1})\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha\eta}+\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{N^{2}\alpha\eta^{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\alpha\eta\geq N^{\delta}}+\log(\eta^{-1})\left(\dfrac{q}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}\right)\mathbf{1}_{C\leq\alpha\eta\leq N^{\delta}},$$
	$$\dfrac{1}{\log(\eta^{-1})}\bigg\|\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_{\ell}(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} \bigg\|_q  \lesssim   \left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{qN^{\frac{2}{3}\delta}}{N\alpha\eta}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\alpha\eta\geq N^{\delta}}+\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right)\mathbf{1}_{C\leq\alpha\eta\leq N^{\delta}} $$
	      
	                                  
	                  
	               
	                                                                                                                                                   
	                                                      
	            
	                
	                                  
	Combining all the estimates above with \eqref{def:zetaRM}, we obtain for $\alpha\eta\geq N^{\delta}$:
	\begin{align*} 
		\|\boldsymbol{\zeta} \|_q &\lesssim\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\dfrac{\log(\eta^{-1})}{\alpha}\right) \left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{qN^{\frac{2}{3}\delta}}{N\alpha\eta}\right)+\log(\eta^{-1})\left(\dfrac{q}{N\alpha\eta}+\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{N^{2}\alpha\eta^{2}}\right)
		\\&\lesssim \dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}+\dfrac{q}{N\alpha\eta}\log(\eta^{-1})+\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{N^{2}\alpha\eta^{2}}\log(\eta^{-1})\end{align*}
	while for $1\ll\alpha\eta\leq N^{\delta}$:
	\begin{align*}
		\|\boldsymbol{\zeta} \|_q &\lesssim\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\dfrac{\log(\eta^{-1})}{\alpha}\right) \left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right)+\log(\eta^{-1})\left(\dfrac{q}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}\right)
		\\&\lesssim \dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\log(\eta^{-1})+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}\log(\eta^{-1}).\end{align*}
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
	We thus obtain:
	\begin{equation*}
		\sqrt{N\alpha}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta} \|_q \lesssim\begin{cases} \dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{(\alpha\eta)^{2}}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+ \dfrac{q}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha\eta} & \text{if } \eta \alpha \ge N^{\delta} \\
			\dfrac{q^{2}}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+q\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{\alpha\eta}} & \text{if } 1\ll\eta\alpha\le N^{\delta}. \\
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Estimation of $\bm\xi$.\endgroup Now, with this choice of $\psi$, we have for all $x\in \mathbb{R}$, using that $\mathcal{H}\left[\mathcal{H}[1]\right]=-1$, \eqref{eq:effpotentialV}, Definition \ref{def:ope D} and the fact that $\psi=0$ outside of a neighborhood of $E$:
	\begin{align*}
		\Xi[\psi] (x) = \fint_{\mathbb{R}} \dfrac{\psi(y)}{x-y} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}} ( y)
		&= \frac1\pi \fint_{\mathbb{R}}  \dfrac{\chi(y)}{x-y} \mathcal{H}[1_\eta](y) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y 
		\\
		& = \chi(x)  \fint_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{ \mathcal{H}[1_\eta](y)}{x-y}  \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y}{\pi}  - \frac1\pi\int_{\mathbb{R}} \dfrac{\chi(x)-\chi(y)}{x-y} \mathcal{H}[1_\eta](y) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y \\
		&=:  1_\eta(x) +\eta\mathfrak{R}(x)
	\end{align*}
	where 
	                                                           
	\begin{equation*}
		x\in\mathbb{R} \mapsto \mathfrak{R}(x)=\frac{-1}{\pi\eta}\int \dfrac{\chi(x)-\chi(u)}{x-u} \mathcal{H}[1_\eta](u) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u
	\end{equation*} 
	is a smooth function. Using the Fourier transform, we may compute explicitly:
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac{\chi(x)-\chi(u)}{x-u}   = \int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\chi}(u) \frac{e^{\mathrm{i} u x}-e^{\mathrm{i} u y}}{x-y}  \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u}{2\pi}=
		\int_\mathbb{R}\int_0^1  \mathrm{i} u \widehat{\chi}(u) e^{\mathrm{i} u((1-t) x+ ty)} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u}{2\pi}
	\end{equation*}
	and for all $t\in(0,1)$, $u\in\mathbb{R}$:
	\begin{equation*}
		\int_\mathbb{R} \mathcal{H}[1_\eta](y) e^{\mathrm{i} y tu}  \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y 
		= \widehat{ \mathcal{H}[1_\eta]}(-u t)  =- \mathrm{i} \mathrm{sgn}(u) \widehat{1_\eta}(-u t)
		= - \eta\mathrm{i} \mathrm{sgn}(u)  e^{\mathrm{i} u t E} \widehat{1}(-u t \eta). 
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, we may write by Fubini:                     
	\begin{align*}
		\mathfrak{R}(x) &= \frac{-1}{\pi\eta} \int_{\mathbb{R}} \dfrac{\chi(x)-\chi(u)}{x-u} \mathcal{H}[1_\eta](u) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u 
		\\&=\dfrac{-1}{2\pi^{2}\eta}\int_{0}^{1}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u \mathrm{i} u \widehat{\chi}(u) e^{\mathrm{i} u(1-t) x}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}[1_\eta](y) e^{\mathrm{i} y tu}  \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y 
		\\&=  \dfrac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_\mathbb{R} |u| \widehat{\chi}(u)  \int_0^1  \widehat{1}(-u t \eta) e^{\mathrm{i} u((1-t) x+ t E)} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u
	\end{align*}
	Differentiating in $x$ and using that $\| \widehat{1} \|_{\mathcal{C}^0(\mathbb{R})} \le \|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} $ and that $\chi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})$, we have for any $p\geq1$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}$ that
	\[
	|\mathfrak{R}^{(p)}(x)| \le \|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}  \int_\mathbb{R}  |u|^{p+1} |\widehat{\chi}(u)| \int_0^1 t^p     \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u
	\lesssim C_k.
	\]
	While we cannot use the mesoscopic fluctuation results from Section \ref{sec: concentration} as with the error terms above since $1_\eta-\Xi[\psi]$ is not supported inside of the bulk, we may still use the moment estimates  (Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates}) to obtain
	                                                                                                               
	\[
	\|\bm{\xi}\|_{q}\leq\eta\| L_N^{(k)}(\mathfrak{R}) \|_q \lesssim \eta\sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}} 
	\]
	with $\boldsymbol\xi =  L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta-\Xi[\psi])$.
<!-- end pro-28 -->
<!-- prop-20: body_md -->
\label{prop: RMTCLT}
Make the same conventions as in Proposition~\ref{prop: RMT error control}. Then for $k$ large enough and all $q\geq1$ and for $G\sim \mathcal{N}\big (0, \|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2\big )$, one has:\begin{itemize}
	\item For $ \eta \geq \tfrac{N^\delta}{\alpha}$:
	$$\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G) \lesssim 
		\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{(\alpha\eta)^{2}}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+
		\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta^{2}}+	\dfrac{q}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1}) +\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\eta\right) +\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}.$$
			\item For $\tfrac{1}{\alpha}\ll\alpha \eta \leq \tfrac{N^\delta}{\alpha}$:
			\begin{equation*}
				\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G)\lesssim
				\dfrac{q^{2}}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1}) +\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+q\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha\eta}}+\eta\right)+\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}.
			\end{equation*}
\end{itemize}
<!-- end prop-20 -->
<!-- pro-29: body_md -->
We first begin with the fact that since $1\in\mathcal{C}_c^{5}(\mathbb{R})$, $ \|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}<+\infty$. Propositions \ref{prop: SteinRMT} and \ref{prop: RMT error control} show that we may rewrite \eqref{Stein1}, with $\mathbf{X} = \sqrt{N\alpha} L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta) $, as:
\[
\sqrt{N\alpha}\frac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N\alpha} = \mathbf{X}+ \sqrt{N\alpha}( \boldsymbol{\zeta} +\boldsymbol{\xi}) + O\bigg(\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}\bigg)
\]
using that $\mathtt{c}(\psi)\lesssim \log\eta^{-1}$ (as shown in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: RMT error control}). Applying Proposition \ref{prop:bound Wp} with $G\sim  \mathcal{N}\big (0, \|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2\big )$ and $\lambda=\sqrt{N\alpha}$, we find: 
                  
                                                                         
                                                                                                                 
                                                                                               
                      
                                                            
   
                                    
\begin{equation*}
\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X},G) \lesssim
\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}\Big\| \nabla \mathbf{X} \cdot \nabla F -\sqrt{N\alpha}\|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2\Big\|_q + \sqrt{N\alpha}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_q +\sqrt{N\alpha}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_q+\eta\log\eta^{-1}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}.
\end{equation*}
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Estimation of quadratic term.\endgroup Since $\nabla F = \big(\psi(\lambda_i) \big)_{i=1}^N$ and $\nabla\mathbf{X} =\tfrac{\sqrt{N\alpha}}{N\eta}\big(\,(1')_\eta(\lambda_i) \big)_{i=1}^N$, we have $\nabla \mathbf{X} \cdot \nabla F=\eta^{-1}\sqrt{N\alpha}\bm{\mu}_N(\psi (1')_\eta) $ and thus:
                                                                                           
\[
\sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\Big\| \nabla \mathbf{X} \cdot \nabla F -\sqrt{N\alpha}\|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2\Big\|_q\leq\eta^{-1}\sqrt{q}\| L_N^{(0)}(\psi (1')_\eta)\|_q+\sqrt{q}\left|\|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2-\eta^{-1}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}( (1')_\eta\psi) \right| 
\]
Since, $\mathtt{c}(\psi (1')_\eta)=O(1)$ we have by Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N} (and noticing that $\|m_0-m_k\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(B)}=O(\alpha^{-1})$ for all $q\geq1$),
$$\eta^{-1}\sqrt{q}\|L_N^{(0)}(\psi (1')_\eta)\|_{q}
\lesssim\begin{cases}
	\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta^{2}}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}\eta}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha\eta}  & \quad\quad\text{if }\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta, \\
	\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha\eta}&\quad\quad\text{otherwise.}
\end{cases}$$
For the second term, by Plancherel's formula, one has using that with our conventions $\widehat{\mathcal{H}[g]}(x)=\mathrm{i}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(x)\widehat{f}(x)$:
\begin{align*}
	\eta^{-1}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(\psi (1')_\eta)&=\frac{1}{\pi} \int_{\eta^{-1}(-1+\varepsilon-E)}^{\eta^{-1}(1-\varepsilon-E)} \chi(u\eta+E)1'(u)\cdot \mathcal{H}[1](u) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u 
	\\&=\frac{1}{\pi} \int_{-\infty}^{+\infty} 1'(u)\cdot \mathcal{H}[1](u) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u +O(\eta)
	\\ &=  \frac{1}{2\pi^2} \int_\mathbb{R} \mathrm{i}\xi  \widehat{1}(\xi) \cdot \big(-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\overline{\widehat{1}(\xi)}\big) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi +O(\eta) \\
	&=\frac{1}{2\pi^2} \int_\mathbb{R} |\xi| |\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2 \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi  +O(\eta)
	\\&= \|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2 +O(\eta).
\end{align*}
                                                             
                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
We thus obtain:
\begin{equation*}
	\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}\Big\| \nabla \mathbf{X} \cdot \nabla F -\sqrt{N\alpha}\|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2\Big\|_q\lesssim\begin{cases}
		\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta^{2}}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\alpha}\eta} +\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\eta\right) & \quad\quad\text{if }\alpha \eta \geq N^\delta, \\
		\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\eta\right) &\quad\quad\text{otherwise.}
	\end{cases}
\end{equation*}
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
   
                                                                                                                                
   
                        
                   
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                 
   
                                                                                                                                                                                                         
   
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
   
                                                
   
                                   
   
                                                               
   
                                                                                                     
   
                                                                                                                                                                                                         
   
                                                  
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Conclusion.\endgroup So, with Proposition \ref{prop: RMT error control}, we obtain using for $\eta\geq\tfrac{N^{\delta}}{\alpha}$:
\begin{equation*}
\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G) \lesssim\dfrac{q^{2}N^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{(\alpha\eta)^{2}}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1})+
\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}N^{\frac{2\delta}{3}}}{ N\alpha\eta^{2}}+	\dfrac{q}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1}) +\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha\eta}+\eta\right) +\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}
\end{equation*}
while
\begin{equation*}
	 \mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G)\lesssim
\dfrac{q^{2}}{\alpha\eta}\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha^{3}}{N^{3}}}\log(\eta^{-1}) +\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+q\sqrt{\dfrac{\alpha}{N}}\log(\eta^{-1})+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha\eta}}+\eta\right)+\eta \log \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{N}}
\end{equation*}
which is the desired result.
<!-- end pro-29 -->
<!-- prop-21: body_md -->
\label{prop: SteinPoisson}
	Let $\varepsilon>0$ be fixed and suppose $B \coloneqq(-1+\varepsilon, 1-\varepsilon)$ and let $\psi \in \mathcal{C}_c^3(B)$ satisfy \eqref{eq: Cnorm} with $\eta>0$. Let $f$ such that $f'=\psi$, and write $F= N\bm{\mu}_N(f)$. Then for $k$ large enough:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:Stein2}
 \dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N}   =   -L_N^{(k)}(\psi')
+  \boldsymbol{\zeta}   +O\bigg(\frac{\eta \mathtt{c}(\psi)\alpha}N\bigg)
\end{equation}
where the error term is deterministic and the random part is given by 
\begin{align*}
\boldsymbol{\zeta}  &=   \frac\alpha{2N} L_N^{(k)}(\psi') +  \frac{\alpha}{2} \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} V'(z) \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} +\frac{\alpha}{2}\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\Delta_k(z)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}\\&\quad+ \alpha \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) m_k(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} +\alpha A_N^{(k)}(\psi).
\end{align*}
<!-- end prop-21 -->
<!-- pro-30: body_md -->
Starting from \eqref{eq:Stein0}, using \eqref{eq: psiexp}-\eqref{eq: aniexp}
\begin{align*}
\dfrac{\mathcal{L}[ F]}{N}\   & = -L_N^{(k)}(\psi')+\frac\alpha2\frac2\alpha \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\left( \partial m_k(z)+m_k^2(z)+\mu_k\Big(  \frac{V'(\cdot)}{\cdot-z} \Big)\right)  \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}  
\\
