&\quad + \frac\alpha{2N}L_N^{(k)}(\psi') + \frac{\alpha}{2} \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} V'(z) \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} +\frac{\alpha}{2}\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\Delta_k(z)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}\\\
&\quad+  \alpha\operatorname{Re} \int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) m_k(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} + \alpha A_N^{(k)}(\psi)+O\bigg(\frac{ \mathtt{c}(\psi) \eta\alpha}N\bigg)
\end{align*}
where the error term is deterministic and controlled by
\[
\frac{\alpha}{N}\bigg|\operatorname{Re} \int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \partial m_k(z) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \bigg| 
\lesssim  \frac{\alpha}{N} \int_{\Omega_+} \big|\overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \big| \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \lesssim  \frac{\alpha}{N} \sum_{0\le k\le 3 } \eta^{k} \|\psi^{(k)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} 
\lesssim \frac{\mathtt{c}(\psi) \alpha\eta}{N} 
\]
and we have made use of the expansion
\begin{align*}
L_N^{(k)}(V'\psi)&=L_N^{(k)}\left( V'(\cdot)\int \frac{\overline{\partial}\Psi(z)}{\cdot-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{\pi}\right)\\
&=\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} V'(z)\overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\widetilde{s_N}(z)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}+\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\Delta_k(z)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z}{\pi}
\end{align*}
with $\Delta_k$ as in Definition \ref{def: delta}. The second line of our $\mathcal{L}[F]$ expansion is deterministic and is a $O(\alpha^{-(k+1)})$ by Proposition \ref{prop:mun stieltjes} which is negligible for $k$ large enough.
<!-- end pro-30 -->
<!-- prop-22: body_md -->
\label{prop: Poissonerror}
Let $1\in \mathcal{C}_c^{3}(\mathbb{R})$, $\tfrac{1}{N}\ll\eta\ll\theta\ll\tfrac{1}{\alpha}$, $\chi\in \mathcal{C}_c^\infty(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\mathbf{1}_{(-1,1)}\leq\chi\leq\mathbf{1}_{(-2,2)}$ and set $\chi_\theta\coloneqq\chi(\tfrac{\cdot-E}{\theta})$ and define
\begin{equation*}\label{eq: defpsiP}
\psi(x) \coloneqq\chi_\theta(x) \int_x^{\infty} 1_\eta(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t.
\end{equation*}
Then, $ \mathtt{c}(\psi) \lesssim \theta$, with $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ is as in Proposition \ref{prop: SteinPoisson} and $\boldsymbol{\xi}\coloneqq  -L_N^{(k)}( \psi' +1_\eta )$,
we have for $k$ large enough and all $q\geq1$:
\begin{equation*}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta} \|_q  \lesssim   \alpha\theta\left(\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\right) , \qquad \sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi} \|_q  \lesssim\frac{\eta}{\theta} \left(\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q} \right).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end prop-22 -->
<!-- pro-31: body_md -->
We first notice that $\psi\in\mathcal{C}_c^{4}(B)$ and that we have for all $x\in\mathbb{R}$:
                   
                                                                       
                
 \begin{equation*}
	\psi'(x) =-1_\eta(x) + \gamma (\chi')_{\theta}(x)\int_{\eta^{-1}(x-E)}^{+\infty}1(u)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u
\end{equation*}
 with $\gamma \coloneqq \tfrac{\eta}{\theta}\ll1$. Inserting this into our expansion of $\mathcal{L}[F]$ in Proposition \ref{prop: SteinPoisson}, we find 
 \begin{equation*}
 \dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F] }{N}  =   L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta)
+  \boldsymbol{\zeta} +\boldsymbol{\xi}  +O\bigg(\frac{ \mathtt{c}(\psi)\eta\alpha}N\bigg)
\end{equation*}
with $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ as in Proposition \ref{prop: SteinPoisson}, and:
\begin{equation*}
\boldsymbol{\xi}\coloneqq  -L_N^{(k)}( \psi' +1_\eta )  =  - \gamma L_N^{(k)}\left ( (\chi')_\theta\int_{\eta^{-1}(\cdot-E)}^{+\infty}1(u)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u\right ).
\end{equation*}
Now, computing directly,
\begin{equation*}
\|\psi\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \|\chi_\theta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \|1_\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \eta \theta.
\end{equation*}
Using Leibniz differentiation formula, we find for all $p\in\llbracket1,4\rrbracket$:
\begin{align*}
	\psi^{(p)} &= -\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-2}\binom{p-1}{\ell}(\chi^{(\ell)})_\theta\theta^{-\ell}(\eta)^{-(p-1-\ell)}(1^{(p-1-\ell)})_\eta  + \gamma \theta^{-(p-1)} (\chi^{(p)})_\theta\int_{\eta^{-1}(\cdot-E)}^{+\infty}1(u)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u
	\\&\quad+\gamma \sum_{\ell=0}^{p-2}\binom{p-1}{\ell}(\chi^{(\ell+1)})_\theta\theta^{-\ell}(\eta)^{-(p-1-\ell)}(-1^{(p-2-\ell)})_\eta
 \end{align*}we have 
\begin{equation*}
\|\psi^{(p)} \|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim  \theta \eta^{1-p}  + \gamma\theta^{2-p} +\gamma\theta\eta^{1-p}\lesssim  \theta \eta^{1-p},
 \end{equation*}
 which allows to deduce that $ \mathtt{c}(\psi) \lesssim \theta$ and  $\mathtt{c}(\psi') \lesssim \theta$. 
                     
                   
                                              
                                                             
                
Thus, applying Propositions \ref{lem:HS_L_N} and \ref{lem:HS_A_N} we find, noting that $\mathtt{c}((\chi')_\theta)\lesssim1$:
$$ \|  L_N^{(k)}\left( (\chi')_\theta \right) \|_q\lesssim\sqrt{\dfrac{q\theta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N},\quad\quad\quad\| L_N^{(k)}(\psi') \|_q \lesssim\theta\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q\theta}{N},\quad\quad \| A_N^{(k)}(\psi)\|_q \lesssim\theta
\left(	\dfrac{q}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}\right).$$
                                      
                  
                                                           
                                
             
               
Furthermore, by Corollary \ref{lem: genHS_L_N},
 \[
  \bigg\| \operatorname{Re} \int_{\Omega_+} V'(z) \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \bigg\|_q +\bigg\| \operatorname{Re} \int_{\Omega_+} m_k(z) \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z \bigg\|_q\lesssim \theta  \left(	\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right) ,
 \]
 and by Corollary \ref{cor: glocon},
 \begin{equation*}
 \left\|\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z)\Delta_k(z)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}\right\|_q \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}\int_{\Omega_+}  |\overline{\partial}\Psi(z)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2 z\lesssim \sqrt{\frac{q}{N\alpha}}\sum_{\ell=0 } ^{3}\eta^{\ell} \|\psi^{(\ell)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} 
\lesssim \frac{\theta\eta\sqrt{q}}{\sqrt{N\alpha}}
 \end{equation*}
Recalling the definitions of $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ and $\boldsymbol{\xi}$, this implies that if $\eta \ge N^{-1}$, $\alpha \ll N$ and $\eta \ll \theta\ll \alpha^{-1}$, the anisotropy is negligible and
\[
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta} \|_q \lesssim    \alpha\theta\left(\sqrt{q}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}\right), \qquad \sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi} \|_q  \lesssim \gamma \left(\sqrt{q}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}} \right)   .
\]
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-31 -->
<!-- prop-23: body_md -->
\label{prop: PoissonCLT}
Take the same conventions as in Proposition \ref{prop: Poissonerror}. Then for $G\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0, \|1\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}^2\right)$, $k$ large enough and any $q\geq1$:
	\begin{equation*}
\mathbf{W}_q\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta), G\right) \lesssim q^{2}\dfrac{\alpha\theta}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta}{N}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}\left(\alpha\theta+\dfrac{\eta}{\theta}\right)+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}+\alpha\theta\right)+\alpha\theta\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end prop-23 -->
<!-- pro-32: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{X} = \sqrt{\frac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}} L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta)$, using \eqref{eq:Stein2} and the control on $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ and $\boldsymbol{\xi}$ from Proposition \ref{prop: Poissonerror} we obtain:
\[
\mathbf{X} = \sqrt{\frac{N}{\eta \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\left(\dfrac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N} -  \boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\xi}\right)  +O\bigg(\alpha\theta\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}\bigg).
\]
Applying Proposition \ref{prop:bound Wp} with $G \sim  \mathcal{N}(0,\|1\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^2)$, we find, with $\Gamma = \nabla \mathbf{X}\cdot \nabla F$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:WpPoissonproof}
	\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G) \lesssim \sqrt{q}\Big\| \tfrac\Gamma{\sqrt{N\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}} -\|1\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^2\Big\|_q +\sqrt{\tfrac{N}{\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\left(\|\bm{\zeta}\|_q+\|\bm\xi\|_q\right)+\alpha\theta\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}.
\end{equation}
Since $\nabla F = \big(\psi(\lambda_i)\big)_{i=1}^N$ and $\nabla\mathbf{X} = \tfrac{\eta^{-1}}{\sqrt{N\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\big( (1')_\eta(\lambda_i)\big)_{i=1}^N$, we have:
\[
\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{N\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\Gamma = \frac{N\eta^{-1}\bm{\mu}_N(\psi (1')_\eta) }{N\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)} =  \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(\psi (1')_\eta)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\eta^2}+\frac{(\mu_k-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})(\psi (1')_\eta)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\eta^2}
+\frac{L_N^{(k)}(\psi (1')_\eta)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\eta^2}.
\]
The fluctuation term is an error term; by definition of $\psi$ we have $\psi (1')_\eta  =\chi_\theta(1')_\eta  \int_\cdot^{\infty} 1_\eta(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t$, so that :
\[
\|\psi (1')_\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \|(1')_\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \|1_\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim \eta^2 , \qquad
\|(\psi (1')_\eta)^{(k)}\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})} \lesssim  \eta^{2-k} \text{ for $k\in\llbracket1,3\rrbracket$}.
\]
Hence, $\mathtt{c}(\psi (1')_\eta) \lesssim \eta$ and we may conclude from Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N} that 
\[
\sqrt{q}\|L_N^{(k)}(\psi (1')_\eta)\|_q \lesssim  \eta q\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta}{N}. 
\]
Furthermore, we find 
\begin{equation*}
\left|\frac{(\mu_k-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})(\psi (1')_\eta)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\eta^2}\right|\lesssim \frac{1}{\alpha\eta^2}\|\psi (1')_\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim \frac{1}{\alpha}.
\end{equation*}
Using changes of variables, the fact that $\chi(x)=1$ on $(-1,1)$, that there exists $M>0$ such that supp $1\subset(-M,M)$ and an integration by part, we have:
\begin{align*}
\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(\psi (1')_\eta)  &= \eta\int_{E-2\theta}^{E+2\theta} (1')_\eta(x)  \int_{\eta^{-1}(x-E)}^{+\infty}1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x
\\&=\eta^{2}\int_{-M}^{M}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(u\eta+E) 1'(u)\int_{u}^{+\infty}1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}  u
\\&=\eta^{2}\int_{-M}^{M}\Big(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)+\int_{E}^{E+u\eta}\rho_{\mathrm{eq}}'(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t\Big) 1'(u)\int_{u}^{+\infty}1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}  u
\\&=\eta^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\int_{\mathbb{R}} 1'(u)\int_{u}^{+\infty}1(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}  u+O(\eta^{3})
\\&=  \eta^{2} \, \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\|1\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2} +O(\eta^{3}).
\end{align*}
                        
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       
                
                              
               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                  
                                                           
             
We thus conclude that:
$$\sqrt{q}\Big\|\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{N\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\Gamma- \|1\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\Big\|_{q}\lesssim \dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta}{N}+ \eta q\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}+\eta\right) ,
$$
and thus by Proposition \ref{prop: Poissonerror} and \eqref{eq:WpPoissonproof}:
$$ \mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G) \lesssim q^{2}\dfrac{\alpha\theta}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta}{N}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}\left(\alpha\theta+\dfrac{\eta}{\theta}\right)+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha}+\alpha\theta\right)+\alpha\theta\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}.$$\qedsymbol{symbol}
<!-- end pro-32 -->
<!-- prop-24: body_md -->
\label{prop: Steintrans}	Let $\varepsilon>0$ be fixed and suppose $B \coloneqq(-1+\varepsilon, 1-\varepsilon)$ and let $\psi \in \mathcal{C}_c^3(B)$ satisfy \eqref{eq: Cnorm} with $\eta>0$. Let $f$ such that $f'=\psi$, and write $F= N\bm{\mu}_N(f)$. Then for $k$ large enough:
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:Stein3}
\frac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N\alpha}   =   L_N^{(k)}\bigg(\Xi[\psi]-\frac{\psi'}{\alpha}\bigg)
+  \boldsymbol{\zeta}(\psi)  +O\bigg(\frac{\eta}N \mathtt{c}(\psi) \bigg)
\end{equation}
where the error term is deterministic and the random part is given by 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:zetaCrit}
	\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\psi)   \coloneqq  \frac1{2N}L_N^{(k)}(\psi') +\sum_{\ell=1}^{k} \operatorname{Re}\left(\alpha^{-\ell}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_\ell(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi}\right)  +A_N^{(k)}(\psi) .
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-24 -->
<!-- prop-25: body_md -->
\label{prop: transerror}
Let $1\in \mathcal{C}_c^3(\mathbb{R})$. Assume $\eta \tau \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)=\alpha^{-1}$ for some fixed $\tau>0$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq: deftranspsi}
\psi \coloneqq  \frac{\chi \cdot  \varphi_\eta}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}}
\end{equation}
for some $\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_c(\mathbb{R})$ and $\varphi$ as in \eqref{eq: defvarphi}. Then, with $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ is as in Proposition \ref{prop: Steintrans} and $\boldsymbol{\xi}$ defined in \eqref{eq: defxitrans}, we have:
\begin{equation*}
\frac{\mathcal{L} [F]}{N \alpha}   =   L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta)
-  \boldsymbol{\zeta} -\boldsymbol{\xi}  +O\bigg(\frac{\eta}N\bigg)
\end{equation*}
with
\begin{equation*}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_q \lesssim\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{\eta^{-1}}{N}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right), \qquad \sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_q\lesssim \dfrac{q}{\alpha\sqrt{N\eta}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end prop-25 -->
<!-- pro-33: body_md -->
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}1. Approximate inversion.\endgroup
Using our definition of  $\psi$, we have for all $x\in\mathbb{R}$, with $\varrho = \chi/\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}$ :
\[
\Xi[\psi](x)    - \alpha^{-1}\psi'(x) =  \fint_\mathbb{R} \frac{\chi(y)\varphi_\eta(y)}{x-y} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y - \frac{\tau\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)} \chi(x)  (\varphi')_\eta(x) - \frac{\varrho'(x)\varphi_\eta(x)}{\alpha},
\]
with $\Xi$ as in Definition \ref{def:ope D}, and hence:
\begin{align*}
\Xi[\psi](x)    -\dfrac{\psi'(x)}{\alpha} &= \pi \chi(x) \fint_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{\varphi_\eta(y)}{x-y} \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y}\pi - \int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{\chi(x)-\chi(y)}{x-y} \varphi_\eta(y) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y - \tau  \chi(x) (\varphi')_\eta(x)
\\&\quad+\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)} \bigg) \tau\chi(x) (\varphi')_\eta(x) - \frac{\varrho'(x)\varphi_\eta(x)}{\alpha} \\
&= 1_\eta(x)- \int_\mathbb{R} \mathcal{D}[\chi](x,y) \varphi_\eta(y) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y +\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)} \bigg) \tau\chi(x) (\varphi')_\eta(x) - \frac{\varrho'(x)\varphi_\eta(x)}{\alpha}
\end{align*}
which can be written as
\begin{equation*}
\Xi[\psi](x)    - \dfrac{\psi'(x)}{\alpha}   =1_\eta(x) - \eta  \Delta (x) - \Upsilon(x) 
\end{equation*}
where we have defined
\begin{equation}\label{eq: def Delta Upsilon}
	\Delta(x) \coloneqq \frac1\eta  \int_\mathbb{R} \frac{\chi(x)-\chi(y)}{x-y} \varphi_\eta(u) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u , \qquad 
	\Upsilon(x) \coloneqq  -\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)} \bigg) \tau\chi(x) (\varphi')_\eta(x) +\frac{\varrho'(x)\varphi_\eta(x)}{\alpha}.
\end{equation}
Inserting these into \eqref{eq:Stein3} yields:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Stein3new}
\frac{\mathcal{L}[ F]}{N\alpha}   =   L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta)
-  \boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\xi}   +O\bigg(\frac{\eta}N\mathtt{c}(\psi) \bigg)
\end{equation}
where $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ is as in Proposition \ref{prop: Steintrans} and
\begin{equation}\label{eq: defxitrans}
\boldsymbol{\xi}   \coloneqq \eta L_N^{(k)}(\Delta) +L_N^{(k)}(\Upsilon)
                                                                                                                                                                                        
\end{equation}
\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}2. Control of $\boldsymbol{\zeta}$ and $\boldsymbol{\xi}$.\endgroup
First, as a rescaled test function, $\mathtt{c}(\psi)\lesssim 1$ and $\mathtt{c}(\psi')\lesssim \eta^{-1}$. Hence, using Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N}, Corollary \ref{lem: genHS_L_N} and Proposition \ref{lem:HS_A_N} we deduce
\[
\bigg\|\operatorname{Re}\int_{\Omega_+} \overline{\partial}\Psi(z) \sigma_{\ell}(z) \widetilde{s_N}(z) \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^2z}{\pi} \bigg\|_q +\eta\left\|L_N^{(k)}(\psi')\right\|_q \lesssim\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N} , \qquad\qquad 
\|A_N^{(k)}(\psi)\|_q \lesssim 	\dfrac{q}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}.
\]
Thus by \eqref{eq:zetaCrit}:
\begin{align*}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_q &\lesssim\sqrt{N\eta^{-1}}\left(\dfrac{\eta^{-1}}{N}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right) \left(\sqrt{\dfrac{q\eta}{N}}+\dfrac{q}{N}\right) +	\sqrt{N\eta^{-1}}\left(\dfrac{q}{N}+\dfrac{q^{2}}{N^{2}\eta}\right) 
\\&\lesssim\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{\eta^{-1}}{N}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right).
\end{align*}
We can control $\Delta$ using a Fourier trick as we did in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: RMT error control}. Indeed,  for all $x,y\in\mathbb{R}$
\begin{equation*}
\frac{\chi(x)-\chi(y)}{x-y}   = \int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\chi}(\xi) \frac{e^{\mathrm{i}\xi x}-e^{\mathrm{i}\xi y}}{x-y}  \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi}=
\int_\mathbb{R}\int_0^1  \mathrm{i} \xi \widehat{\chi}(\xi) e^{\mathrm{i}\xi((1-t) x+ ty)} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi}
\end{equation*}
and thus integrating over $y$:
\begin{align*}
\Delta(x) = \frac{1}{\eta} \int_\mathbb{R} \dfrac{\chi(x)-\chi(y)}{x-y} \varphi_\eta(y) \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} y
&= \frac{\mathrm{i}}{\eta} \int_\mathbb{R}\int_0^1   \xi \widehat{\chi}(\xi) \widehat{\varphi_\eta}(-t\xi) e^{\mathrm{i}\xi(1-t) x} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi}\\
&= \mathrm{i} \int_\mathbb{R} \int_0^1   \xi \widehat{\chi}(\xi) \widehat{\varphi}(-t\xi \eta)e^{\mathrm{i} t\xi E} e^{\mathrm{i}\xi(1-t) x} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi}
\end{align*}
Now, from the definition of $\varphi$ \eqref{eq: defvarphi}, we have $\widehat{\varphi}(\xi)  = \frac{\mathrm{i}  \mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\widehat{1}(\xi)}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}$. Thus, we obtain:
\[
\Delta(x) = - \int_\mathbb{R} \int_0^1 \frac{|\xi|}{\pi+\tau|\xi|t\eta} \widehat{1}(-t\eta\xi)  \widehat{\chi}(\xi) e^{\mathrm{i} t\xi E} e^{\mathrm{i}\xi(1-t) x} \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi} . 
\]
Now, using $\| \widehat{1} \|_{\mathcal{C}^0(\mathbb{R})} \le \|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}$ and the fact that $\Delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$, so for all $k\geq1$:
\[
|\Delta^{(k)}(x)| \le \|1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}  \int_\mathbb{R} |\xi|^{k+1}  |\widehat{\chi}(\xi)| \int_0^1 t^k   \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi
\lesssim C_k.
\]
Hence, even though $\Delta$ has support that extends outside of the bulk, we may apply the moment estimates from Proposition \ref{prop: moment estimates} and \ref{prop: moment estimates infinity}, to find for $k$ large enough:
\begin{equation}\label{eq: q moment Delta}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\eta L_N^{(k)}(\Delta)\|_{q}\lesssim \sqrt{\frac{q\eta}{\alpha }}.
\end{equation}
Finally, we analyze $\Upsilon$. The fluctuations of the term $\frac{\varrho'\varphi_\eta}{\alpha}$ is exactly amenable to Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N}; as a rescaled test function, $\mathtt{c}(\varphi_\eta)\lesssim 1$ and so 
\begin{equation}\label{eq: q moment varrho}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\left\|L_N^{(k)}\left(\frac{\varrho'\varphi_\eta}{\alpha}\right)\right\|_q \lesssim \dfrac{q}{\alpha\sqrt{N\eta}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}.
\end{equation}
Next, we compute $\mathtt{c}\left(\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}} \bigg) \tau\chi (\varphi')_\eta\right)\lesssim \eta$. The function $\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{4}(\mathbb{R})$ and using mean-value theorem:
\begin{align*}
\int_B \left|\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)} \bigg) \tau\chi(x) (\varphi')_\eta(x)\right|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x&\lesssim \int_B |\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(x)-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)|(\varphi')_\eta(x)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x
\\&\lesssim  \int_B|x-E|\cdot |(\varphi')_\eta(x)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x
\\&\lesssim \eta^2\int_\mathbb{R} |u|\cdot|1'(u)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u\lesssim\eta^{2}.
\end{align*}
Furthermore, we also have for all $k\in\llbracket1,4\rrbracket$ by Leibniz formula:
\begin{align*}
\left(\Big(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}} \Big) \tau\chi (\varphi')_\eta\right)^{(k)}&=\Big(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}} \Big) \tau\chi \big(\varphi^{(k+1)}\big)_\eta\eta^{-k}
\\&\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^k c_\ell\Big(\chi^{(\ell)}-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\varrho^{(\ell)}\Big)\left(\varphi^{(k+1-\ell)}\right)_\eta \eta^{\ell-k}
\end{align*}
for some constants $c_k$, from which we may deduce for all $k\in\llbracket1,4\rrbracket$ that, as before, by the mean-value theorem:
\begin{equation*}
\left\|\left(\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}} \bigg) \tau\chi (\varphi')_\eta\right)^{(k)}\right\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim \eta^{2-k}+\sum_{\ell=1}^k\eta^{1+\ell-k} \lesssim \eta^{2-k}.
\end{equation*}
Hence, $\mathtt{c}\left(\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}} \bigg) \tau\chi (\varphi')_\eta\right)\lesssim \eta$ and by Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N} we find:
\begin{equation*}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\left\|L_N^{(k)}\left(\bigg(1- \frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}} \bigg) \tau\chi (\varphi')_\eta\right)\right\|_{q}\lesssim q\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}+\sqrt{q}\eta.
\end{equation*}
So, by the previous estimate, \eqref{eq: def Delta Upsilon} and \eqref{eq: q moment varrho}, we obtain using that $\alpha\eta\asymp1$:
\begin{equation*}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|L_N^{(k)}(\Upsilon)\|_q\lesssim \dfrac{q}{\alpha\sqrt{N\eta}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}
\end{equation*}
and thus by the previous estimate, \eqref{eq: defxitrans} and \eqref{eq: q moment Delta} that: 
\begin{equation*}
\sqrt{\dfrac{N\eta^{-1}}{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_q\lesssim \dfrac{q}{\alpha\sqrt{N\eta}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{q}}{\alpha}.
\end{equation*}
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-33 -->
<!-- prop-26: body_md -->
Make the same conventions as in Proposition \ref{prop: transerror}. Then, setting 
\begin{equation}\label{def: transvar}
\Sigma_\tau^2(1):=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{|\xi||\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi
\end{equation}
we have for $k$ large enough and all $q\geq1$:
$$\mathbf{W}_q(\sqrt{\frac{N\eta^{-1}}{ \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta), \mathcal{N}(0, \Sigma_\tau^2(1)))\lesssim\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{\eta^{-1}}{N}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{\eta}{N}}.$$
<!-- end prop-26 -->
<!-- pro-34: body_md -->
We set $\mathbf{X} \coloneqq  \sqrt{\frac{N\eta^{-1}}{ \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}L_N^{(k)}(1_\eta)$ and use Proposition \ref{prop: transerror} to obtain:
\[
\mathbf{X} =  \sqrt{\frac{N\eta^{-1}}{ \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\left(\mathcal{L} \left[\frac{F}{N\alpha}\right] +  \boldsymbol{\zeta} +\boldsymbol{\xi}\right)  +O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\eta}{N}}\bigg)
\]
Applying Proposition \ref{prop:bound Wp} with $G \sim  \mathcal{N}(0, \Sigma_\tau^2(1))$, we find using $\alpha\eta\tau\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)=1$:
\[
\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G) \lesssim  \sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\| W\|_{q}+\sqrt{N\eta^{-1}}\left(\|\bm\zeta\|_{q}+\|\bm\xi\|_{q}\right) +\sqrt{\dfrac{\eta}{N}}, \qquad  W \coloneqq \nabla \mathbf{X} \cdot \nabla F - \sqrt{\dfrac{N\alpha}{\tau}} \Sigma_\tau^2(1).
\]
Since $\nabla F = \big(\psi(\lambda_i) \big)_{i=1}^N$ and $\nabla\mathbf{X} = \eta^{-1}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N\eta\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)}}\big( (1')_\eta(\lambda_i)\big)_{i=1}^N= \eta^{-1}\sqrt{\tfrac{\alpha\tau}{N}}\big( (1')_\eta(\lambda_i)\big)_{i=1}^N$, we have:
\[
\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\tau N\alpha}}W + \dfrac{1}{\tau}\Sigma_\tau^2(1)= \eta^{-1}\bm{\mu}_N(\psi (1')_\eta) =   \eta^{-1}\left(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(\psi (1')_\eta)+(\mu_k-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})(\psi (1')_\eta)
+L_N^{(k)}(\psi (1')_\eta)\right) 
\]
Since $\psi$ and $(1')_\eta$ are both rescaled at scale $\eta$, $\mathtt{c}(\psi (1')_\eta)\lesssim 1$ and we may conclude from Proposition \ref{lem:HS_L_N} that:
\[
\frac{\sqrt{q}}{\eta}\|L_N^{(k)}(\psi (1')_\eta)\|_q \lesssim\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}. 
\]
Furthermore, since $\|\mu_k-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}\|\lesssim \alpha^{-1}$,
\begin{equation*}
\left|\frac{\tau (\mu_k-\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})(\psi (1')_\eta)}{\eta}\right|\lesssim \frac{\tau}{\alpha \eta}\|\psi(1')_\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim \eta.
\end{equation*}
Using the definition of $\psi$ in \eqref{eq: deftranspsi} along with Plancherel and \eqref{eq: defvarphi}, we have:  
\begin{align*}
\frac{\tau \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(\psi (1')_\eta)}{\eta}=\frac{\tau}{\eta}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \varphi_\eta(x)(1')_\eta(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x=\tau \int_{\mathbb{R}} \varphi(x)1'(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x&=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \widehat{\varphi}(\xi)\overline{\mathrm{i}\xi\widehat{1}(\xi)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi \\
&=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{\mathrm{i} \mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\widehat{1}(\xi)}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\overline{\mathrm{i} \xi \widehat{1}(\xi)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi \\
&=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{|\xi|\cdot|\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi=\Sigma_\tau^2(1)
\end{align*}
Thus,
\begin{equation*}
 \sqrt{\dfrac{q}{N\alpha}}\| W\|_{q}\lesssim\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+ \sqrt{q}\eta.
\end{equation*}
Finally using Proposition \ref{prop: transerror}, we obtain:
$$\mathbf{W}_q(\mathbf{X}, G)\lesssim\dfrac{q^{2}}{(N\eta)^{3/2}}+\dfrac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N\eta}+\dfrac{q}{\sqrt{N\eta}}+\sqrt{q}\left(\dfrac{\eta^{-1}}{N}+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{\eta}{N}}$$
which yields the conclusion.
<!-- end pro-34 -->
<!-- rem-7: body_md -->
Notice that this variance interpolates between the Random matrix and Poisson regimes. For the sake of this comparison, define:
			\begin{equation*}
				\epsilon^{-1}:=\sqrt{N\min\left(\tau \alpha, \frac{1}{ \mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\eta}\right)},
			\end{equation*}
			If we take $\tau\downarrow 0$, then $\alpha \eta \rightarrow \infty$ (Random matrix regime) and the normalization of $L_N^{(k)}$ in the above theorem is given by $\sqrt{N\alpha \tau}$. Renormalizing by $\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau}}$ to agree with the Random matrix regime, the variance becomes:
			\begin{equation*}
				\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\xi|\cdot|\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi\underset{\tau \downarrow 0}{\longrightarrow} \frac{1}{2\pi^2}\int_{\mathbb{R}} |\xi|\cdot|\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi=\|1\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1/2}}^2
			\end{equation*}
			Analogously, if we take $\tau \rightarrow \infty$ then $\alpha \eta \rightarrow 0$ (Poisson regime), and the normalization of $L_N^{(k)}$ in the above theorem is the correct $\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\eta}{N}}$. The variance then becomes 
			\begin{equation*}
				\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\xi|\cdot|\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2}{\pi+\tau|\xi|}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \xi\underset{\tau \uparrow \infty}{\longrightarrow} \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} |\widehat{1}(\xi)|^2\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi=\|1\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}^2
			\end{equation*}
			which agrees with the variance we found in the Poisson regime.
<!-- end rem-7 -->
<!-- def-7: body_md -->
\label{def:spacedconfig} Let $\bm{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}$ such that $\lambda_1<\dots<\lambda_N$, we define the configuration $\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}$ by:
				$$\widetilde{\lambda}_{i+1}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}+\max\left(\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_i,\dfrac{1}{N^{3}}\right),$$
				and denote $\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_N\coloneqq\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\widetilde{\lambda}_i}$. We also define
				$\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}\coloneqq\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_N\star\mathcal{U}_N$
				where $\mathcal{U}_N$ is the uniform distribution on $(0,N^{-5})$.
				For an arbitrary vector $\bm{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}$, we first order by applying a permutation $\sigma$. We then obtain the same objects $\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}$, $\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_N$ and $\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}$ and can apply $\sigma^{-1}$.
<!-- end def-7 -->
<!-- def-8: body_md -->
We define the following squared-distance $\mathfrak{D}$ for all measures $\mu,\mu'\in\mathcal{M}_1(\mathbb{R})$ by:
				\begin{align}
					\label{def:distanceMesures}
					\mathfrak{D}(\mu,\mu') &\coloneqq \left( -\int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\mu-\mu')(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\mu-\mu')(y) \right)^{1/2}\\
					&= \left( \int_0^{+\infty} \frac{1}{t}\big|\widehat{(\mu-\mu')}(t) \big|^2 \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} t \right)^{1/2}.\nonumber
				\end{align}
<!-- end def-8 -->
<!-- prop-27: body_md -->
\label{thm:concentration}
				There exists $K\in \mathbb{R}$ depending on $V$, such that for any $N\geq 1$, $\bm{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}$, $\eta\in(0,1)$,
				\begin{equation}
					\label{ineq:concentration}
					\log p_N (\bm{\lambda})\leq -\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\mathfrak{D}^{2}[\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}]-\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}(1-\eta)\int_\mathbb{R} V_{\mathrm{eff}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(x)+CN(\log N)^{2}.
				\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-27 -->
<!-- pro-35: body_md -->
We first show that 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:lowerboundZN}
		\log\mathcal{Z}_N[V]\geq-\dfrac{N\alpha}{2} \mathcal{E}(\mu _{\mathrm{eq}})+O\left(N\log N\right). 
	\end{equation} Since $\rho _{\mathrm{eq}}$ is bounded by a constant $M$, we have:
	$$|\gamma_i-\gamma_j|\geq\dfrac{M}{N}$$
	where the $\gamma_i$'s are the classical positions, uniquely defined for all $i\in\llbracket1,N\rrbracket$ by:
	$$\int_{-1}^{\gamma_i}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}(t)=\dfrac{i}{N}.$$
	Restricting the space of integrations to
	$$\Omega=\left\{\bm{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\,\max\limits_{i}|\lambda_i-\gamma_i|\leq\dfrac{1}{4MN}\right\},$$
	noticing that for all $\bm{\lambda}\in\Omega$, $i\in\llbracket1,N\rrbracket$,
	$$-V(\lambda_i)\geq-V(\gamma_i)-\dfrac{\|V'\|_{\infty,[-2,2]}}{4MN}$$
	and using the change of variables $\lambda_i\rightarrow u_i+\gamma_i$, we obtain:
	\begin{multline*}
		\mathcal{Z}_N[V]\geq e^{-\alpha\frac{\|V'\|_{\infty,[-2,2]}}{8M}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} V(\gamma_i)}\cdot\int_{[\pm\frac{1}{4MN}]^{N}}\prod_{i<j}^{N}\left|u_i-u_j+\gamma_i-\gamma_j\right|^{\beta}\cdot \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\bm{u}
		\\\geq e^{-\alpha\frac{\|V'\|_{\infty,[-2,2]}}{8M}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} V(\gamma_i)}\cdot\prod_{i<j}^{N}\left(\gamma_j-\gamma_i\right)^{\beta}\int_{v_1<\dots<v_N}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{1}_{|u_i|\leq\frac{1}{4MN}}\cdot \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\bm{v}
		\\\geq e^{-\alpha\frac{\|V'\|_{\infty,[-2,2]}}{8M}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} V(\gamma_i)}\cdot\prod_{i<j}^{N}\left(\gamma_j-\gamma_i\right)^{\beta}\dfrac{1}{N!}\left(\dfrac{1}{2MN}\right)^{N}.
	\end{multline*}
	We know use, that
	$$\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}V(\gamma_i)=\int_{-1}^{1}V(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}(x)+O(1/N)$$
	and a similar argument, see \cite[Proof Theorem 4.4]{guionnet2019asymptotics},
	$$\sum_{i<j}^{N}\log(\gamma_j-\gamma_i)=\dfrac{N^{2}}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}(y)+O(N).$$
	This concludes the proof for \eqref{eq:lowerboundZN}.
	
	We now prove \eqref{ineq:concentration}. Using \eqref{eq:lowerboundZN} and the spaced configuration $\bm{\lambda}$ defined in Definition \ref{def:spacedconfig}, we obtain the existance of a constant $C>0$ such that:
	\begin{equation}
		\label{ineq:boundDensity}
		\log p_N (\bm{\lambda})\leq -\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\mathcal{E}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})+CN\log N+\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\log|\widetilde{\lambda}_i-\widetilde{\lambda}_j|-\dfrac{\alpha}{2} \sum_{i=1}^N V(\lambda_i)\, . 
	\end{equation}
	We now show the following estimate, for a constant $C>0$:
	\begin{equation}
		\label{ineq:estimeeEnergie}
		\sum_{i\neq j}\log|y_i-y_j|\leq2+N^2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^2} \log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_N(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_N(y) + CN\log N\, .
	\end{equation}
	Let $i< j$ and $u,v\in [0,N^{-5}]$. Since for $x\neq 0$ and $|h|\leq \frac{|x|}{2}$, we have $$\big|\log|x+h| - \log|x|\big|\leq \frac{2|h|}{|x|},$$
	we deduce that:
	$$\big|\log|\widetilde{\lambda}_i-\widetilde{\lambda}_j+u- v|-\log|y_i-y_j|\big| \leq \frac{2|u-v|}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_i-\widetilde{\lambda}_j|} \leq \frac{2N^{-5}}{N^{-3}} =\dfrac{2}{N^{2}}.$$
	Thus, summing over $i\neq j$ and integrating with respect to $u$ and $v$, we get
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_{i\neq j}&\log|\widetilde{\lambda}_i-\widetilde{\lambda}_j|\leq 2+ N^{10}\sum_{i\neq j}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^2}\log|\widetilde{\lambda}_i-\widetilde{\lambda}_j+u-v| \mathbf{1}_{0<u,v<N^{-5}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} v \\
		&= 2+ N^2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^2} \log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(y) - N^{11}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^2} \log|u-v|\mathbf{1}_{0<u,v<N^{-5}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} u\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} v\,
		\\&\leq2+ N^2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^2} \log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(y)+CN\log N
	\end{align*}
	for a constant $C>0$ independent of $\bm{\lambda}$, which allows us to deduce \eqref{ineq:estimeeEnergie}. We can now combine \eqref{ineq:boundDensity} and \eqref{ineq:estimeeEnergie}, we get:
	\begin{equation*}
		\log p_N (\bm{\lambda})\leq \dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\mathcal{E}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})-\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}) + \dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\int_\mathbb{R} V(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(x) -\dfrac{\alpha}{2} \sum_{i=1}^N V(\lambda_i)+CN\log N.
	\end{equation*}
	By using,
	$$\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u})=\mathcal{E}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}})+\mathfrak{D}^{2}[\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}]+\int_\mathbb{R} V_{\mathrm{eff}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} \widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(x),$$
	we obtain, since there exists $\varepsilon,C>0$ such that for any $|t|<\varepsilon$, $|V'(x+t)|\leq C(|V(x)|+1)$
	\begin{multline*}
		\Big|\int_\mathbb{R} V(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}-\bm{\mu}_N)(x)\Big|\leq\dfrac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)\max_{i}\left\{V'(\lambda_i+|t|), t\in[0,\tfrac{(i-1)}{N^{3}}]\right\}
		\\\leq\dfrac{C}{N^{2}}\left(\int_\mathbb{R} |V(x)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x+1\right), 
	\end{multline*}
	we obtain:
		\begin{equation*}
		\log p_N (\bm{\lambda})\leq \dfrac{-N\alpha}{2}\mathfrak{D}^{2}[\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}]-\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}(1-\dfrac{C}{N^{2}})\int_\mathbb{R} V_{\mathrm{eff}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u}(x)+CN\log N.
	\end{equation*}
	 This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-35 -->
<!-- lem-11: body_md -->
\label{lemma:trunc}There exists $\mathfrak{R}_N^{(n)}$ defined on 
			$\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,0}$ such that for all $f_n\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,0}:$
				$$\braket{f_n}=\braket{f_{n|c}}+\mathfrak{R}_N^{(n)}[f_n],\hspace{1cm}\left|\mathfrak{R}_N^{(n)}[f_n]\right|\leq c_n\cdot e^{-c\alpha}\|f\|_{\kappa,0} $$
				where $f_{n|c}(x_1,\dots,x_n)\coloneqq f_{n}(x_1,\dots,x_n)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}1(x_i)$ and $1\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ such that:
				$$\mathbf{1}_{[-1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]}(x)\leq1(x)\leq\mathbf{1}_{[-2,2]}(x)$$
				for $\varepsilon<1$.
<!-- end lem-11 -->
<!-- pro-36: body_md -->
For simplicity, we denote $f_n$ (resp. $f_{n|c}$) by $f$  (resp. $f_{|c}$) and because:
			$$\braket{f}=\braket{f_{\mathrm{sym}}},$$
			where $f_{\mathrm{sym}}(\bm{x})\coloneqq\dfrac{1}{n!}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_n}f\left(x_{\sigma(1)},\dots,x_{\sigma(n)}\right)$, we notice that we can assume to $f$ to be symmetric function. Now,
			$$\braket{f}=\braket{f_{|c}}+\mathfrak{A}_N(f)$$
			where $\mathfrak{A}_N(f)\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\binom{n}{i}\mathfrak{A}_{N,i}(f)$ and
			$$\mathfrak{A}_{N,i}(f)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_N\left[\int_{\left([-1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]^{c}\right)^{i}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{i}(1-1(x_\ell))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\bm{\mu}_N(x_\ell)\int_{[-2,2]^{n-i}}f(\bm{x})\cdot\prod_{\ell=i+1}^{n}1(x_\ell)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} L_N^{(0)}(x_\ell)\right].$$
			Thus a straightforward bound gives:
			$$|\mathfrak{A}_{N,i}(f)|\leq4\cdot2^{n-i}\cdot\|f\|_{\kappa,0}\cdot\|e^{\kappa V}\|_{\infty,[-2,2]}^{n-i}\cdot A_i$$
			where $A_i=\braket{\otimes_{l=1}^{i}(e^{\kappa V}\mathbf{1}_{[-1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]^c})}$. Now using the exact same proof as in \cite[Lemma 3.1.9]{borot2016asymptotic} together with \eqref{ineq:concentration}, we obtain that $\mathfrak{A}_N(f)=O\left(\|f\|_{\kappa,0} e^{-c\alpha}\right)$ for some universal constant $c>0$.
<!-- end pro-36 -->
<!-- pro-37: body_md -->
The proof can be found in \cite[Corollary 3.1.10]{borot2016asymptotic} in a similar setting by considering the event $\Omega_{M,N}\coloneqq\left\{\bm{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\quad\mathfrak{D}^{2}(\widetilde{\bm{\mu}}_{N,u},\mu _{\mathrm{eq}})>\dfrac{M\log N}{\alpha}\right\}$ for $M>0$ large enough. This event satisfies: $\mathbb{P}_N\left(\Omega_{M,N}\right)=O\left(e^{-cN\log N}\right) $ for some $c>0$ and a $M$ large enough by Proposition \ref{thm:concentration}.
<!-- end pro-37 -->
<!-- pro-38: body_md -->
Let $\big(\psi^{(i)}\big)_{a=1}^{n+1}\in \mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})^{n+1}$ and set $1^{(1)}\coloneqq\Xi^{-1}[1^{(1)}]$
			, set $$V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\lambda)\coloneqq V(\lambda)+\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\epsilon_i\left(\psi^{(i)}(\lambda)-\int_{-1}^{1}\psi^{(i)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}(y)\right) $$ and then define
			\begin{equation*}
				p_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}(\bm{\lambda})\coloneqq \dfrac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_N[V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}]}\prod_{i<j}^N|\lambda_i-\lambda_j|^{\beta }\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\lambda_i)}
			\end{equation*}
			The new normalisation constant $\mathcal{Z}_N[V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}]$ is such that $p_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}$ is still a pdf on $\mathbb{R}^N$. We then define $G_t(\mu)=\mu+t1^{(1)}(\mu)$. Since $\partial_11^{(1)}$ is bounded, for $t$ small enough $G_t$ is a diffeomorphism over $\mathbb{R}$. By a change of variable $\lambda_i=G_t(\mu_i)$, we obtain :
			\begin{equation*}
				1=\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}p_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}(\lambda_1,\dots,\lambda_N)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^N\bm{\lambda}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}p_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}\Big(G_t(\mu_1),\dots,G_t(\mu_N)\Big)\prod_{i=1}^NG_t'(\mu_i)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_i.
			\end{equation*}
			Making an asymptotic expansion up to the first order in $t$ of the right-hand side of this equality yields:
			\begin{multline*}
				1=\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^N\bm{\lambda}\cdot p_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}(\bm{\lambda})\cdot\Bigg\{1+t\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_11^{(1)}(\lambda_i)\Bigg\}\cdot\Bigg\{1+t\dfrac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\dfrac{1^{(1)}(\lambda_i)-1^{(1)}(\lambda_j)}{\lambda_i-\lambda_j}\Bigg\}\\\cdot\Bigg\{1-t\frac{\alpha}{2} \sum_{i=1}^NV_{\bm{\varepsilon}}'(\lambda_i)1^{(1)}(\lambda_i)\Bigg\}+O(t^2).
			\end{multline*}
			Identifying the terms linear in $t$ leads to, with $\braket{g_n}_{\otimes_{\ell=1}^{n}\mu_\ell}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}\left[\otimes_{\ell=1}^{n}\mu_\ell(g_n)\right] $	where $\mathbb{E}_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}$ is the expectation value with density $	p_N^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}$:	\begin{equation*}
				0=-\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\Braket{V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}'1^{(1)}}_{\bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\dfrac{N\alpha}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D}[1^{(1)}]}_{\bm{\mu}_N \otimes \bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+(N-\dfrac{\alpha}{2} )\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}}_{\bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}.
			\end{equation*}
			By definition of $V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}$, we get:
			\begin{equation*}
				\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{V'1^{(1)}}_{\bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\dfrac{\varepsilon_i}{2}\Braket{1^{(1)}\partial_1\psi^{(i)}}_{\bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D}[1^{(1)}]}_{\bm{\mu}_N \otimes \bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}-\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}}_{\bm{\mu}_N }^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}=0.
			\end{equation*}
			It becomes, after recentring the empirical measures against $\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}$ we obtain:
			\begin{multline*}
			\dfrac{1}{2}	\Braket{V'1^{(1)}}_{ L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{V'1^{(1)}}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} [1^{(1)} ]}_{ L_N\otimes  L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}-\Braket{\mathcal{D} [1^{(1)} ]}_{ L_N\otimes \mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} [1^{(1)} ]}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}\otimes \mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}
				\\-\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\left (\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}}_{ L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}\right)+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\varepsilon_i\Big(\Braket{1^{(1)}\partial_1\psi^{(i)}}_{ L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{1^{(1)}\partial_1\psi^{(i)}}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}\Big)=0.
			\end{multline*}
			Using \eqref{eq:contrainst mu infinity} and Definition \ref{def:ope D}, we get:
			\begin{multline}\label{DSbuilding}
				-\Braket{\Xi\left[1^{(1)}\right] }_{ L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}=\dfrac{-1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D}[1^{(1)}]}_{ L_N\otimes  L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}
				-\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\left (\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}}_{ L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}\right)
				\\+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\varepsilon_i\Big(\Braket{1^{(1)}\partial_1\psi^{(i)}}_{ L_N}^{(\bm{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{1^{(1)}\partial_1\psi^{(i)}}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}\Big).
			\end{multline}
			Taking $\varepsilon_i$ go to 0 for all $i$, $1^{(1)}=\Xi^{-1}[\psi]$ leads to:
			\begin{equation*}
				\Braket{\psi}_{ L_N}=\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi]'}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}}
				+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right)\Braket{\Xi^{-1}[\psi]'}_{ L_N}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi]}_{ L_N\otimes  L_N}.
			\end{equation*}
			Hence we obtain \eqref{DSlvl1}.
			
			Furthermore, in \eqref{DSbuilding} multiplying by $\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N[V_{\bm{\varepsilon}}]}{\mathcal{Z}_N[V]}$, applying $\partial_{\varepsilon_2}\dots\partial_{\varepsilon_{n+1}}$ and evaluate at $\varepsilon_i=0$ leads to
			\begin{multline}\label{DSbuildinglvl}
				\Braket{\Xi[1^{(1)}] (\xi_1).\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}1^{(i)}(\xi_i)}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes} L_N}=\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}(\xi_1)\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}1^{(i)}(\xi_i)}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}\overset{n}{\bigotimes} L_N}
				\\+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\Braket{\partial_11^{(1)}(\xi_1)\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}1^{(i)}(\xi_i)}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes} L_N}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D}[1^{(1)}](\xi_1,\xi_2)\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}1^{(i)}(\xi_{i+1})}_{\overset{n+2}{\bigotimes} L_N}
				\\+\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\Bigg(\Braket{1^{(1)}(\xi_1)\partial_11^{(i)}(\xi_1)\prod_{\substack{j=2\\j\neq i}}^{n+1}1^{(j)}(\xi_j)}_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes} L_N}+\Braket{1^{(1)}(\xi_1)\partial_11^{(i)}(\xi_1)\prod_{\substack{j=2\\j\neq i}}^{n+1}1^{(j)}(\xi_j)}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}\overset{n-1}{\bigotimes} L_N}\Bigg)
			\end{multline}
			                                                               
			Using that $ L_N$ has zero mass and defining $1_{n+1}(\xi_1,\dots,\xi_{n+1})\coloneqq \prod_{i=1}^{n+1}1^{(i)}(\xi_i)$ and using the operators defined earlier leads to:
			\begin{multline}\label{DSbuiltl}
				\Braket{\Xi[1_{n+1}]}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes} L_N}=\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\Braket{\partial_11_{n+1}}_{\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}\overset{n}{\bigotimes} L_N}+\left (\dfrac{1}{\alpha }-\dfrac{1}{2N}\right )\Braket{\partial_11_{n+1}}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes} L_N}
				\\+\dfrac{1}{2}\Braket{\mathcal{D}[1_{n+1}]}_{\overset{n+2}{\bigotimes} L_N}+\dfrac{1}{N\alpha}\Bigg(\Braket{\Theta\Xi[1_{n+1}]\ }_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes} L_N}+\Braket{\theta\Xi[1_{n+1}] }_{\overset{n-1}{\bigotimes} L_N}\Bigg).
			\end{multline}
			Using that $\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes}\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ is dense in $\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})$ for $\|.\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})}$ and that $\Xi$ and $\mathcal{D}$ are continuous for $\|.\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(K)}$ for any compact $K\subset\mathbb{R}$, we deduce that \eqref{DSbuiltl} is valid for any $1_{n+1}\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})$.
			Now using that for all $0<\kappa<\kappa'$,
			$$\|\Xi_1\left[1_{n+1}\right] \|_{\kappa',1}\leq C\|1_{n+1}\|_{\kappa,1}$$ and that $\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})$ is dense in $\mathcal{C}^1(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})$ for $\|.\|_{\kappa,1}$, we obtain \eqref{DSlvlnthm} for general function $1_{n+1}\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,1}$.
<!-- end pro-38 -->
<!-- lem-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:replacement}
	Let $V$ be a potential satisfying Assumptions \ref{assumptions}, $a>0$ the largest numbers such that $[-1-3a,1+3a]\subset\mathcal{U}\cap\mathbb{R}$. Set $V_1(x)=1(x)V(x)+(1-1(x))(x^{2}+C)$ for $1\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ such that $\mathbf{1}_{[-1-a,1+a]}\leq1\leq \mathbf{1}_{[-1-2a,1+2a]}$ and $C>0$ large enough. The potential $V_1\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ and the corresponding equilibium measures $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}=\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_1}$ and for all $k\geq1$ and $f_1,\dots,f_q\in\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,0}^{V_1}$ for some $\kappa>0$:
	$$\mathbb{E}_N^{V}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]=\mathbb{E}_N^{V_1}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]+\prod_{i=1}^{q}\|f_i\|_{\kappa,0}O\left (e^{-c\alpha}\right )$$
	where the remainder $O\left (e^{-c\alpha}\right )$ depends on $q$.
<!-- end lem-12 -->
<!-- pro-39: body_md -->
The claim that $V_1\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$ is clear from Assumption \textit{\ref{assumption1}.} To check that the two equilibrium measures coincide, we show that $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_1}$ satisfies \eqref{eq:effpotentialV}. For all $x\in[-1,1]$, since $V(x)=V_1(x)$:
	$$ C_\mathrm{eq}^{V}=V(x)-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)= V_1(x)-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x).$$
	Thus $x\in[-1,1]\mapsto V_1(x)-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)$ is constant. Furthermore, for $|x|>1$:
	\begin{align*}
	V_1(x)-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)-C_\mathrm{eq}^{V}&=1(x)\left(V(x)-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)-C_\mathrm{eq}^{V}\right)\\&\quad+(1-1(x))\left(x^{2}+C-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)-C_\mathrm{eq}^{V}\right) 
	\end{align*}
	In the RHS, the first term is positive by \eqref{eq:effpotentialV}. For the second one, since $$x\mapsto x^{2}+C-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)-C_\mathrm{eq}^{V}$$ is continuous and behaves like $x^{2}+o(x^{2})$ at infinity, it is bounded by below and by taking $C>0$ large enough:
	$$x^{2}+C-2\int_\mathbb{R}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu _{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}(x)-C_\mathrm{eq}^{V}>0.$$
	Thus $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}$ satisfies the equilibrium condition \eqref{eq:effpotentialV} for $V_1$ and thus $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}=\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_1}$ and $C_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V}=C_{\mathrm{eq}}^{V_1}$.
	
	Now, let $W\in\{V,V_1\}$ and set $J=[-1-a,1+a]$ and $\mathbb{P}_N^{W,J}$ the probability measure  defined by:
	$$\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_N^{W,J}(\bm{\lambda})=\dfrac{\mathbf{1}_{\forall j, \lambda_j\in J}}{\mathcal{Z}_N^{J}[W]}\prod_{i<j}^{N}\left |\lambda_i-\lambda_j\right|^{\beta }.\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} W(\lambda_i)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\lambda_i.$$
	It is clear that $\mathcal{Z}_N^{J}[W]/\mathcal{Z}_N[W]=\mathbb{P}_N^{W}\left(\forall j, \lambda_j\in J\right)=1+O(e^{-c\alpha})$ for some $c>0$ by Proposition \ref{appprop: LDP lambdamax}. Thus,
	\begin{align*}\label{key}
		\mathbb{E}_N^{W}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]&=\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N^{J}[W]}{\mathcal{Z}_N[W]}	\mathbb{E}_N^{W,J}\left [\prod_{i=1}^q\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]+\mathbb{E}_N^{W}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\exists j, \lambda_j\notin J}\right ]
		\\&=	\mathbb{E}_N^{W,J}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]+O\left (e^{-c\alpha}\prod_{i=1}^{q}\|f_i\|_{\kappa,0}\right )
		\\&\quad+\mathbb{E}_N^{W}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(e^{\kappa W})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\exists j, \lambda_j\notin J}\right ]O\left(\prod_{i=1}^{q}\|f_i\|_{\kappa,0}\right) .
	\end{align*}
	where the norm $\|\cdot\|_{\kappa,0}$ has to be understood with respect to the potential $W$.
	Since: $$\mathbb{E}_N^{W}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(e^{\kappa W})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\exists j, \lambda_j\notin J}\right ]\leq C_qe^{-c\alpha}$$ 
	for some $C_q>0$ and $c>0$ (this bound can be shown using the same bounds in the proof of Lemma \ref{lemma:trunc} for $A_i$).
	Since, $\mathbb{E}_N^{V_1,J}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]=\mathbb{E}_N^{V,J}\left [\prod_{i=1}^{q}\bm{\mu}_N(f_i)\right ]$, it concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-39 -->
<!-- prop-28: body_md -->
\label{appprop: LDP lambdamax}
	Let $V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ be a continuous potential satisfying:
	$$\liminf_{x\rightarrow +\infty}\dfrac{V(x)}{2\log|x|}>1.$$
	The laws of the largest particle $\lambda_{\max}=\max\limits_{i\in\llbracket1,N\rrbracket}\lambda_i$ under $\mathbb{P}_N$ satisfies a large deviaion principle at speed $\alpha$ with good rate function $\mathcal{I}_V$ defined by:
	$$\mathcal{I}_V(x)=\begin{cases}
		\dfrac{1}{2}V_\mathrm{eff}(x)\hspace{1cm}&\text{if}\quad x\geq1,
		\\+\infty&\text{if}\quad x<1,
	\end{cases}$$
	where the effective potential $V_{\mathrm{eff}}$ has been defined in \eqref{eq:effpotentialV}.
<!-- end prop-28 -->
<!-- pro-40: body_md -->
We follow \cite[Appendix A]{BoG1}, the proof is almost the same so we only stress the differences and follow the same structure of the proof. From \cite[\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}A.1\endgroup]{BoG1}, $\mathcal{I}_V$ is a good rate function for the same reasons. To get the exponential tightness of the laws of $\lambda_{\max}$, following \cite[\begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}A.2\endgroup]{BoG1}, we obtain similarly:
	\begin{equation*}
		\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N^{\beta_N}[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_{N-1}^{\beta_{N}}[V]}
		\geq\int_\mathbb{R}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\lambda_Ne^{-\alpha \frac{V(\lambda_N)}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{\beta_N}\left[\exp\left(\beta_N\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\log|\lambda_N-\lambda_i|-(\alpha-(N-1)\beta_N)\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\dfrac{V(\lambda_i)}{2}\right) \right].
	\end{equation*}
	Introducing $\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\chi(x)\coloneqq \dfrac{e^{-\frac{V(x)}{2}}}{\kappa}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x$ and $\kappa\coloneqq\int_\mathbb{R} e^{-\frac{V(x)}{2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x$, we get by Jensen's inequality:
		\begin{align*}
		\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N^{\beta_N}[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_{N-1}^{\beta_{N}}[V]}
		&\geq\kappa\exp\Bigg(\chi\otimes\mathbb{P}_{N-1}^{\beta_N}\left(-(\alpha-1)\frac{V(\lambda_N)}{2}+(\alpha-\beta_N)\int_\mathbb{R}\log|\lambda_N-x|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{N-1}(x)\right)
		\\&\quad-\beta_N\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\frac{V(\lambda_i)}{2}\Bigg)
		\\&\geq\kappa\exp\left(\alpha c+(\alpha-\beta_N)\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{\beta_N}\left[\int_\mathbb{R}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log|\lambda_N-x|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\chi(\lambda_N)-\frac{V(x)}{2}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{N-1}(x)\right]\right),
	\end{align*}
	for some $c>0$. Now since $x\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log|\lambda_N-x|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\chi(\lambda_N)$ is bounded by below (as a continuous function growing like $\log|x|$ at infinity) and by exponential tightness of \cite[Eq (2.6.21)]{anderson2010introduction}, we get:
		\begin{equation*}
		\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\log\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_N^{\beta_N}[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_{N-1}^{\beta_{N}}[V]}>-\infty.
	\end{equation*}
	For the \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}A.3\endgroup part, we first recall that the LDP for the empirical measure holds at speed $N\alpha$ \cite{Garcia}. Now, we set:
	$$Y_N\coloneqq\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_{N-1}^{\beta_N}[\frac{N}{N-1}V]}{\mathcal{Z}_N^{\beta_N}[V]},\hspace{0.8cm}\Xi_N(\xi)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{\beta_N,\frac{N }{N-1}V}\left[\exp\left(\beta_N\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\log|\xi-\lambda_i|-(\alpha-1)\frac{V(\xi)}{2}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\forall i, \lambda_i<\xi} \right].$$
	We thus have for every closed set $F\subset [1,+\infty)$,
	$$\mathbb{P}_N\left(\lambda_{\max}\in F\right)=Y_N\int_Fe^{-\frac{V(\xi)}{2}}\Xi_N(\xi)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\xi.$$ 
	The upper bound on $\Xi_N$:
	$$\sup_{\xi\in F}\Xi_N(\xi)\leq\exp\left(\alpha\Big(\eta'-\inf_{\xi\in F}(\mathcal{I}_V(\xi)+C_{\mathrm{eq}})\Big)\right) $$
	 valid for all $\eta'>0$ and the bound:
	$$\dfrac{1}{Y_N}\geq \exp\left(-\alpha\Big(\eta''+\inf_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{I}_V(\xi)+C_{\mathrm{eq}})\Big)\right) $$ that holds for any $\eta''>0$ are obtained in the exact same way. Noticing that because of \eqref{eq:effpotentialV}, $\inf_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{I}_V(\xi)=0$, this leads to the bound:
	$$\limsup_{N \rightarrow +\infty}\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\log\mathbb{P}_N\left(\lambda_{\max}\in F\right)\leq-\inf_{\xi\in F}\mathcal{I}_V(\xi)$$
	For the \begingroup\bfseries\mathversion{bold}A.4\endgroup part, the bounds are obtained in the same way which leads to the lower bound valid for all $x>1$:
		$$\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow0}\liminf_{N \rightarrow +\infty}\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\log\mathbb{P}_N\left(\lambda_{\max}\in (x-\varepsilon,x+\varepsilon)\right)\geq-\mathcal{I}_V(x).$$
		\qedsymbol{symbol}
<!-- end pro-40 -->
<!-- lem-13: body_md -->
\label{lem: stieltjes transform approx}
	The following convergence in law holds:
	$$\mu_\alpha\underset{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}.$$
<!-- end lem-13 -->
<!-- pro-41: body_md -->
The proof is similar to ideas used in \cite[Lemma 3.6]{padilla2024emergence} \cite[Lemma 2.1]{armstrong2022thermal}. First, notice that $\mathcal{E}$ defined in \eqref{eq:energy functional}	is lower semicontinuous under our assumptions by \cite[Lemma 2.4]{serfaty26}. The lack of positivity of the entropy prevents us from concluding immediately; we circumvent this issue by a trick used in \cite{armstrong2022thermal}; namely, we can rewrite the function $\mathcal{E}_\alpha$ as
	\begin{align*}
		\mathcal{E}_\alpha(\mu)&=\left(1-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} V(x)\, \mathrm d \mu(x)-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}} \log|x-y|~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(y)
		\\&\quad+\left(\int_\mathbb{R} \frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha}V(x)\, \mathrm d \mu(x) +\frac{2}{\alpha}\int_\mathbb{R} \log \dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right),
	\end{align*}
	where $\alpha_\ast$ is such that $\int_{|x|\geq 1} e^{-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{2} V(x)}~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x$ is finite (we have a good deal of freedom over what to choose for $\alpha_\ast$ since we assume that $V=x^2+C$ outside of a compact set $K$). Now, let $(\mu_N)$ be a sequence of probability measures converging weakly to some probability measure $\mu$. By \cite[Lemma 2.4]{serfaty26}, we have for any $\epsilon>0$ that 
	\begin{multline*}
		\liminf_{N \rightarrow \infty}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha_N}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} V\, \mathrm d \mu_N-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}} \log|x-y|~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_N(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_N(y)  \\\geq \left(1-\epsilon\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} V\, \mathrm d \mu-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}} \log|x-y|~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(y).
	\end{multline*}
	Next, 
		\begin{equation*}
		\liminf_{N \rightarrow \infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha_N}V(x)\, \mathrm d \mu_N(x) +\frac{2}{\alpha_N}\int_\mathbb{R}  \log \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_N(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_N(x)\right) 
		\geq \liminf_{N \rightarrow \infty}\frac{-2}{\alpha_{N}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{2}V(x)-1}~\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x=0
	\end{equation*}
	where we have used the inequality
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac{c\gamma}{\alpha_N}+\frac{\gamma \log \gamma}{\alpha_N} \geq -\frac{1}{\alpha_N}e^{-c-1},
	\end{equation*} 
	with $c=\alpha_\ast V$ and $\gamma=\mu_{N}$,
	which can be seen by minimizing the above as a function of $\gamma$. 
	
	It follows that
	\begin{equation*}
		\liminf_{N \rightarrow \infty}\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}(\mu_N)\geq  \mathcal{E}(\mu)+\int_{\mathbb{R}} (1-\epsilon)V(x)~ \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)
	\end{equation*}
	and $\epsilon>0$ was arbitrary so we are done.
	
	Now, let $\{\mu_{\alpha_N} \}$ be a sequence of minimizers of $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}$; it is sufficient for us to show that this sequence is tight. Indeed, if it is then by Prokhorov's theorem it has some weak limit $\mu$ and we can conclude as in \cite[Theorem 3.8]{serfaty26}. Namely, we have by lower semicontinuity of the functionals that 
		\begin{equation}
		\mathcal{E}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}) = \limsup_{N \rightarrow +\infty}\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}(\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}) \geq \limsup_{N \rightarrow +\infty}\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}(\mu_{\alpha_N}) \geq \mathcal{E}(\mu). 
	\end{equation}
	Uniqueness of the equilibrium measure implies $\mu=\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}$ and hence that $\mu_{\alpha_N}$ converges weakly to $\mu_V$ under the topology of weak convergence.
	
	
	
	To show that $\{\mu_{\alpha_N}\}$ is a tight sequence of probability measures, we first show that the sequence $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}(\mu_{\alpha_N})$ is bounded. Without loss of generality, assume $\alpha_1=\inf \alpha_N$. The boundedness follows from $\frac{1}{\alpha_N}\rightarrow 0$ and the minimality of $\mu_{\alpha_N}$:
	\begin{equation}
		\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}(\mu_{\alpha_N}) \leq \mathcal{E}_{\alpha_N}(\mu_{\alpha_1}) \leq \mathcal{E}_{\alpha_1}(\mu_{\alpha_1}):=C_1<+\infty
	\end{equation}
	for all $N$.  Now, for any $C_2>0$, there is a compact set $K \times K$ outside of which 
	\begin{equation}
		-\log|x-y|+\gamma_N\frac{V(x)}{2}+\gamma_N\frac{V(y)}{2}>C_2
	\end{equation}
	with $\gamma_N=\left(1-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha_N}\right)$ by our coercivity assumptions on $V$, where the set $K \times K$ is independent of $N$. Moving some of the potential onto the entropy as above, we have
	\begin{align*}
		C_1 &\geq \mathcal{E}(\mu_{\alpha_N})+\left(1-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha_N}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} V(x)\, \mathrm d \mu_{\alpha_N}(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\alpha_\ast}{\alpha_N}V+\frac{2}{\alpha_N}\log \mu_{\alpha_N}\right)\, \mathrm d \mu_{\alpha_N} \\
		&\geq \iint_{\mathbb{R}^2} \left(-\log|x-y|+\gamma_n\frac{V(x)}{2}+\gamma_N\frac{V(y)}{2}\right)\, \mathrm d \mu_{\alpha_N}(x)\, \mathrm d \mu_{\alpha_N}(y)-\frac{1}{\alpha_N}\int_{\mathbb{R}} e^{-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{2}V(x)-1}\, \mathrm \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x \\
		& \geq -C_3+C_2\mu_{\alpha_N}\otimes \mu_{\alpha_N}((K \times K)^c) \\
		& \geq -C_3+C_2\mu_{\alpha_N}(K^c),
	\end{align*}
	where we have used that $(x,y)\in K\times K\mapsto-\log|x-y|+\gamma_N\frac{V(x)}{2}+\gamma_N\frac{V(y)}{2}$ is everywhere bounded below  independently of $N$, since $V$ is bounded below, $K$ is bounded and
	$$\log|x-y|\leq\log(1+|x|)+\log(1+|y|).$$
	We have also used that $-\frac{1}{\alpha_N}\int_{\mathbb{R}} e^{-\frac{\alpha_\ast}{2}V(x)-1}\, \mathrm \mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d} x$ is bounded below as well since $\alpha_N \rightarrow \infty$. Since $C_2$ can be made arbitrarily large, this gives us the tightness of the sequence $\{\mu_{\alpha_N}\}$, and we conclude the desired convergence.
<!-- end pro-41 -->
<!-- prop-29: body_md -->
\label{appthm:conv muk}Take $V(x)=2x^{2}$, then for all  $\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([-1,1])$, for all $i\geq0$, there exists $C,r>0$ (both independent of $i$) such that
	$$|\nu_i(\psi)|\leq C r^{i}\cdot \|\psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2i^{2}}([-1,1])}.$$ 
	Thus for all $\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([-1,1])$ such that $\|\psi\|_{K,\,\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}<\infty$ for $K>0$, then for all $\alpha>K^{\sqrt{2}}r$ the serie $\sum_{j\geq0}\dfrac{\nu_j(\psi)}{\alpha^{j}}$ converges absolutely (or equivalently the sequence $\mu_k(\psi)$ converges). Furthermore, defining $\mu_{\infty}(\psi)\coloneqq\sum_{j\geq0}\dfrac{\nu_j(\psi)}{\alpha^{j}}$, we have for all $\alpha>K^{\sqrt{2}}r$:
	$$\mu_{\infty}(\psi)\leq\dfrac{C}{1-\dfrac{K^{\sqrt{2}}r}{\alpha}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{K,\,\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}$$
<!-- end prop-29 -->
<!-- pro-42: body_md -->
By \eqref{eq:inverse Xi formula} and \cite[Lemma A.1 \textit{(i)}]{dworaczekguera2025clt},
	$$\|\Xi^{-1}[\psi]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{i}([-1,1])}\leq \dfrac{1}{\pi}\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{i+1}([-1,1])}$$ for all $i\geq0$.
	We now prove the estimate on $\nu_{i}$ by induction. For $n=1$, we have: $$|\nu_1(\psi)|\leq\|\Xi^{-1}[\psi]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1])}\leq\dfrac{1}{\pi}\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([-1,1])}.$$
	We first obtain that $\nu_{i}$ is continuous with respect to the $\mathcal{C}^{2i^{2}}$-norm. For that, we recall that if for all $i\geq1$, $\nu_i$ continuous for the $\mathcal{C}^{k_i}$-norm with constant $C_i>0$ then, for all $i,j\geq1$
	\begin{align*}
		|\nu_i\otimes\nu_j\left(\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi]\right)|\leq C_iC_j\|\mathcal{D} \Xi^{-1}[\psi]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k_i+k_j}([-1,1]^{2})}&\leq C_iC_j\|\Xi^{-1}[\psi]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k_i+k_j+1}([-1,1])}
		\\&\leq \dfrac{C_iC_j}{\pi}\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k_i+k_j+2}([-1,1])}.
	\end{align*}
	We thus obtain, using \eqref{eq:constrait nu i}, that $k_n$ must satisfy $k_n\geq \max\left(k_{n-1}+2, \max\limits_{1\leq p\leq n-1} k_p+k_{n-p}+2\right)$ and $k_1\geq2$, therefore it is easy to see that choosing $k_n=2n^{2}$ works. We now compute the constant. By the same reasoning, using \eqref{eq:constrait nu i}, it can be shown that the constants $C_n$ must satisfy $C_1\geq1$ and these conditions:
	$$C_n\geq \dfrac{C_{n-1}}{\pi}+\dfrac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}C_pC_{n-p}.$$
	Looking for a sequence $(C_n)$ such that these bounds are equalities and defining $F(z)=\sum_{i\geq1}C_iz^{i}$, we obtain that:
	$$F(z)-z=\dfrac{z}{\pi}F(z)+\dfrac{1}{2\pi}F(z)^{2}\iff F(z)^{2}+\left(z-\pi\right)F(z) +2\pi z=0.$$
	Thus, $F(z)=\pi-z-\sqrt{(z-\pi)^{2}-2\pi z}$ where we used the fact that $F(0)=0$. The radius of convergence of $F$ is given by the singularity of the square root, thus one has to solve: $$(z-\pi)^{2}-2\pi z=z^{2}-4\pi z+\pi^{2}=0$$ whose solutions are $2\pi\pm\pi\sqrt{3}$. The closest solution to 0 is $z_0=2\pi-\pi\sqrt{3}\in(0,1)$ and thus since $F(z)\sim c+ c'\sqrt{1-\dfrac{z}{z_0}}$, we obtain by standard singularity analysis that such sequence $(C_n)$ exists and admits the following asymptotc bound $C_n= O\left(\dfrac{1}{z_0^{n}n^{3/2}}\right)$. This concludes the proof.
<!-- end pro-42 -->
<!-- rem-8: body_md -->
The proof of Proposition \ref{appthm:conv muk} only works because in the quadratic case, the function $S$ defined in \eqref{eq: S} is constant.
<!-- end rem-8 -->
<!-- prop-30: body_md -->
For any $f\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([-1,1])$ such that $\|f\|_{K,\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}<\infty$ for some $K>0$, then for all $\alpha$ large enough and $V(x)=2x^{2}$:
	$$\mu_\infty(V'f)-\mu_\infty\otimes\mu_\infty\left(\mathcal{D}[f]\right)-\dfrac{2}{\alpha}\mu_\infty(f')=0.$$
<!-- end prop-30 -->
<!-- pro-43: body_md -->
Using and summing the constraints \eqref{eq:constrait nu i}, the definiton of the master operator Definition \ref{def:ope D} and using the constraint satisfied by $\mu_{\mathrm{eq}}$ \eqref{eq:contrainst mu infinity}, we obtain the conlusion noticing that $$\|f\|_{K,\,\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}<\infty\Rightarrow\|f'\|_{K,\,\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}<\infty.$$\qedsymbol{symbol}
<!-- end pro-43 -->
<!-- rem-9: body_md -->
This constraint has the same form as the one satisfied by the thermal equilibrium measure $\mu_\alpha$ except that $\mu_{\infty}$ is supported on $[-1,1]$. This latter fact confirms that $\mu_{\infty}\neq\mu_\alpha$. If we were able to take $f(x)=(x-z)^{-1}$ for $z\in\mathbb{C}_+$, we would obtain the equation \cite[Eq. (12)]{AllezBouchaudGuionnet} whose unique solution is the Stieltjes transform of the termal equilbrium measure among functions which behave like $-z^{-1}$ at infinity. We would conclude that $\mu_{\infty}=\mu_{\alpha}$ which is wrong.
<!-- end rem-9 -->
<!-- prop-31: body_md -->
\label{prop:bound Wp}
	Let $q\geq1$, and $G \sim \mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})$ for $\sigma>0$. 
	Let $X \in \mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ and suppose that $X= \lambda^{-1}\big( \mathcal{L} [F] +Z \big)$ where $\lambda>0$,  $F\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ and $Z \in L^q(\mathbb{Q}_N)$.
	Then:
	\begin{equation*}\label{Steinbound-gen}
		\mathbf{W}_q(X,G)\leq \lambda^{-1}\big( \sqrt{q/\sigma^{2}}\| W\|_{q}+\|Z\|_{q} \big), \qquad  W = \nabla X \cdot \nabla F - \lambda \sigma^{2} . 
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end prop-31 -->
<!-- prop-32: body_md -->
\label{prop:bound_normq}
	Under the same conditions as in Proposition~\ref{prop:bound Wp}, one has for  all $ q\geq2$,
	\[
	\|X\|_q \le \lambda^{-\frac12} \sqrt{q \|\Gamma \|_{q/2}} + \lambda^{-1} \|Z\|_q , \qquad \Gamma := \nabla F \cdot\nabla X . 
	\]
<!-- end prop-32 -->
<!-- pro-44: body_md -->
Integrating by parts and using Holder's inequality
	\[\begin{aligned}
		\|X\|_q^q  &= \lambda^{-1}\mathbb{E}_N[(\mathcal{L} [F] +Z)X^{q-1}] \\
		& = \lambda^{-1}  \mathbb{E}_N[ ((q-1) \Gamma + ZX)  X^{q-2} ] \\
		&\le \lambda^{-1}  \|(q-1)\Gamma + ZX\|_{q/2} \|X\|_q^{q-2}
	\end{aligned}\]
	Dividing both sides by $ \|X\|_q^{q-2}$, using that $\|(q-1)\Gamma + ZX\|_{q/2}  \le q\|\Gamma \|_{q/2} + \|Z\|_q\|X\|_q$ and rearranging, we obtain 
	\[
	\|X\|_q^2 \le  \lambda^{-1} q \|\Gamma \|_{q/2} +  \lambda^{-1} \|Z\|_q\|X\|_q
	\]
	Using that if $x^2 \le \alpha +\gamma x $ for $x>0$, then   \(x\leq \sqrt{\alpha}+\gamma\) (by solving the quadratic equation), we conclude that 
	\[
	\|X\|_q \le \lambda^{-\frac12} \sqrt{q \|\Gamma \|_{q/2}} + \lambda^{-1} \|Z\|_q.
	\]
<!-- end pro-44 -->
